# Milestone 0.32 — Last breaking window, then freeze; first fair benefit test

Act on the 0.31 measurement, batch every pending breaking decision into one release with one rewrite done by a shipped rewriter, re-register the instruments, re-run the seeded-defect comparison with an equipped C# arm on the post-rewrite language, then declare the result an edition.

## Entry criteria

- The first 0.31 exit criterion is met in one of its three forms (stage 1 published, the reduced design published, or "no paid run authorised" recorded with its not-measured rows).
- Soundness sweep green on the 0.30 packet for the 0.31 run count.
- The #1782 baseline is recorded, and the corpus report job (#1779) is running before #1802, #1784 and #1800 start.

## Exit criteria

- The breaking-window decision record (#1784) is closed: every listed decision was decided or deferred to a future edition by the cut-off date, every row decided "land" has merged, and the rewrite has landed or the inventory records that none was needed.
- One repository rewrite: .calr files migrated by the shipped rewriter; embedded Calor text (test literals, sweep templates, documentation fences) listed with how each was migrated; instruments re-registered (#1807). Or the inventory records that no rewrite was needed because no decision changed source.
- The edition is declared with a per-module marker and a written compatibility promise, or the decision to defer is recorded with its effect on 0.33 (#1805).
- Seeded-defect replication with three conditions published on the post-rewrite commit, or the maintainer's decline recorded; if a collection is authorised but unpublished at release, the edition declaration is deferred (#1805) and this criterion is recorded as carried to 0.33.
- Agents may create test files through the sanctioned path: the write-hook exemption, and the rule text in whichever of the agent file and the MCP server instructions the #1823 record leaves carrying the rules (#1809).
- Unknown-effect share reported per corpus project (the corpus part of #1802; its agent-run table may follow).
- calor init delivery default decided (#1823).
- #1647 closed.

## Maintainer decisions, batched

- Window record (#1784), covering every spelling decision under the amended rule #1785: #1646 flips, #1645 respellings, #1794, #1797, #1796, the edition-marker spelling (#1084). One payload manifest and one token-count dispatch per tokenizer for the whole batch. The payload date precedes the cut-off; the opt-in mechanism is the one the #1084 design record defines.
- Semantic record: #1800 (rejecting guarantees, including making the bool exhaustiveness check effective and whether obligation verification may ever be a default-build check), #1735, any effect spelling #1731 needs.
- Spend record (#1816) for both paid collections in 0.32: the replication and the #1645 re-run. Part B also registers the base-rate reading for the #1789 pairs (the #1770 rule applied to the re-run) with a run count that reaches its 30-run floor.
- Delivery and budget record: #1823, #1824, the template budget, and the division of the remaining always-loaded token headroom among #1809, #1822, #1650 and #1825.
- Edition declaration (#1805).
- Converter record (#1647): the lossy floor that cannot be restored for files preserved by design; whether the corpus report job gets blocking native floors; whether the directory-mode native-fraction column is removed.

## Contents

Actions: **new** (to be created; `NEW-NN-xx` is a placeholder until filed), **amend** (text added to or replaced in an existing issue), **move-in** (moved from another milestone, usually amended), **rewrite** (body replaced because it is stale against 0.30), **keep**, **close**. "May slip" marks issues that are off the exit chain.

Issue bodies below are shown with their section headings two levels down so this document has one outline. The bodies to file, with their original headings, are in `issues.json`.

## Epic #1649 · [0.32] Epic: stabilize the 0.30 language

Existing epic #1649. The body below replaces or amends it as marked.

#### Goal

Act on what the 0.30 agent evaluation (#1605) measured, bring the converter's native output back to its pre-0.30 level on the paths users and CI run, and clear the loud-failure and diagnostic-quality backlog. No child of this epic changes a spelling: form changes are decided in the breaking window (epic #1787) and applied by the single rewrite (epic #1792).

#### Why

0.30 rewrote the grammar, the emitter, two verification paths and every teaching surface with no backward compatibility, and its release published no new benchmark results (`CHANGELOG.md:15`). #1605 publishes at the 0.31 exit and is the first agent measurement of the 0.30 language. This epic is the capacity reserved to act on it before 0.33 builds agent reach on the language: fixes for the constructs that regressed, fix patches for the diagnostics agents actually hit, and replacement guidance for the retired forms they still write.

The converter also lost ground. On the round-trip harness's lossy leg, native files fell from 17 to 10 of 32 (MediatR), from 96 to 58 of 139 (FluentValidation) and from 54 to 36 of 112 (Serilog), and the floors were lowered to match (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:363-367`). The 0.34 coverage work, the density target and every report that reads migrated corpus output start from those numbers, so "#1647 closed" is a 0.32 exit criterion.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1645 | Act on the #1605 evaluation: fix every regression that is not a form change, refer form changes to the window record, re-run the affected pairs on the post-rewrite commit |
| #1646 | Apply the amended fewest-tokens rule to every 0.30 spelling with each tokenizer the rule names; hand candidate flips to the window record |
| #1647 | Default-path converter regressions: round-trip native files back to 17 of 32, 96 of 139 and 54 of 112; the migrated corpus projects build with effect enforcement on |
| #1790 | Fix coverage for agent-hit diagnostics: the three most frequent codes, and every wrong-form error |
| #1788 | Calor0006 names the replacement for every retired `§` marker |
| #1789 | Two modify-a-converted-module pairs on one migrated corpus project |
| #1746 | Token ratio and native fraction reported per corpus project |
| #909 | Division analysis: re-test the double report on 0.30; fix the over-strong Calor0926 suggestion |
| #1511 | Spurious Calor0410 after a lossy partial-class merge |
| #1142 | Remove the configured-vs-defaulted trap in EffectEnforcementPass caps |

Close any of these that 0.30 makes obsolete.

Conditional child: #1670 (0.31) re-tests whether `///` documentation text reaches the generated C#. If it does not, that re-test files a new issue against this epic ("`///` documentation text is not emitted into generated C#"), and the issue joins the table as backlog that may be deferred with a reason. If the re-test finds no defect, no child is added.

#### Sequence

1. #1647 starts when 0.32 opens. Its harness part waits on nothing; the parts that take over what #1782 leaves read the #1782 baseline and the corpus report job (#1779). Both are 0.32 entry conditions.
2. #1789 needs two 0.31 results: project-mode migration that verifies (#1782) and a harness that runs the 0.30 arm (#1766). It merges before the spend record (#1816) is decided, because that record registers the pair set.
3. #1645 (triage and fixes), #1790 and #1788 start when #1605 has published stage 1 or the reduced design. All three read its report, and the last two change text that a measured arm reads. If #1605 recorded "no paid run authorised", there is no report, and each of the three says what it then records.
4. #1646 runs after the amended rule (#1785) and the tokenizer tooling (#1799), and hands its candidate flips to the window record (#1784) before that record's cut-off date.
5. The #1645 fixes and #1790 merge before the commit that #1807 records. A fix that merges later is not measured by the re-run.
6. The #1645 re-run is last: on the commit after the rewrite (#1803) and the instrument re-registration (#1807), under the spend record (#1816), with the #1789 pairs in its pair set. #1645 closes, on the published re-run or the recorded decline, before the edition is declared (#1805).
7. #1746 follows the corpus report job (#1779).

Off the 0.32 exit chain, and free to slip to 0.33 without holding the release: #1788, #1746, #909, #1511, #1142.

#### Done when

- Every #1605 regression is fixed or its form decision revisited (#1645 closed).
- The token measurements use the real tokenizer (#1646 closed: every 0.30 spelling choice has a count from each tokenizer the amended rule names; the density ratio is reported by #1746 with an offline proxy on every run and an Anthropic count once per release).
- The backlog above is closed or explicitly deferred with a reason. #1647 cannot be deferred: its closure is a 0.32 exit criterion.

#### Not in this epic

- Any change to an existing spelling and any new hard error: the breaking-window record and its decisions (#1784, #1794 to #1800, #1743, #1741, #1735), epic #1787.
- The fewest-tokens rule itself (#1785) and the tokenizer tooling (#1799): epic #1787.
- The edition marker, compiler support for a decided form, the rewriter, the single rewrite, instrument re-registration and the edition declaration (#1084, #1793, #1806 to #1805): epic #1792.
- The spend record that authorises the #1645 re-run (#1816), and the seeded-defect replication: epic #1812.
- The rule that effect-row and contract fixes are never marked safe (#1801): epic #1808.
- What an agent does with a preserved C# file (#1822): epic #1804.
- Teaching-reference size (#1734) and the `calor init` delivery default (#1823): epic #1821.
- CS8669 on preserved originals in project-mode `calor migrate` (#1782) and the corpus report job (#1779): 0.31, epic #1780.
- The below-1.0x density target (#1844) and native coverage for constructs that were never supported (#1659): 0.34.
- Follow-up with no issue: running the corpus subjects' own test suites against the project-mode migrated libraries. #1647 judges tests on the round-trip legs and the member oracle, and the corpus report job (#1779) records `tests: not-run`.
- Former children of this epic: #1573 (closed 2026-10-22), #1555 (closed under epic #1758), #1648 (moved to 0.31), #1177 (moved to 0.34).

### #1649 · [0.32] Epic: stabilize the 0.30 language

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** epic, p1 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

**Current.** The epic body has a goal (absorb the fallout of the 0.30 rewrite and clear the loud-failure and diagnostic-quality backlog), a Work table of ten children (#1645, #1646, #1647, #909, #1511, #1555, #1573, #1142, #1177, #1648), and three done-when statements. It has no sequence and no list of what is out of scope.

**Amendment.** Replace the whole body with the text under `## Epic body` at the top of this file. Title, labels and milestone do not change. The differences from the current body are:

1. `## Work`: remove #1573 (closed 2026-10-22), #1555 (closed by the tracker re-baseline in epic #1758: the array form it concerns is a lexer error in 0.30), #1648 (moved to 0.31, epic #1776) and #1177 (moved to 0.34, epic #1845). Add #1790, #1788, #1789 and #1746. Reword the rows for #1645, #1646, #1647 and #909 to their amended scope. Keep the sentence "Close any of these that 0.30 makes obsolete."
2. Below the table, add the conditional child that the #1670 re-test files if `///` documentation text is not emitted into generated C#.
3. `## Goal` and `## Why`: state that no child changes a spelling, and name the converter numbers.
4. Add `## Sequence` and `## Not in this epic`.
5. `## Done when`: the three statements are kept word for word. Each gains a parenthesis naming the child that discharges it, and the third gains one sentence: #1647 cannot be deferred, because its closure is a 0.32 exit criterion.

**Why.** The table is stale against 0.30: #1573 was closed on 2026-10-22 and is still listed, #1555 concerns a `§ARR` form that has been a lexer error since 0.30, and #1648 and #1177 move to other milestones. The new children are work taken out of #1645 (fix patches, Calor0006 guidance), the pairs its re-run needs, and #1746, which moves in from 0.31. Without the added sentence, "closed or explicitly deferred with a reason" would allow #1647 to be deferred, which the 0.32 exit list does not.

### #1645 · [0.32] Act on the 0.30 agent evaluation: fix or revisit every regressed construct

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1605, #1789, #1807, #1816 · **Blocks:** #1805, #1642 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

**Current.** The issue acts on the #1605 results in three steps: fix every construct where arm B′ or B is worse than arm A (fix the teaching or the diagnostic, or revisit the form "still under the one-way and fewest-tokens rules"); read the agent error logs and class each wrong-form error as a teaching bug (#1603) or a lint gap (#1622); re-run the affected pairs and report in the release notes. It is done when no construct regresses against arm A and the re-run numbers are published. It says nothing about spend, about which commit the re-run pins, or about who decides a respelling.

**Amendment.** Title unchanged. Replace the `## Change` and `## Done when` sections and the closing line with the following.

```markdown
## Change

Act on the published 0.30 agent evaluation (#1605). An *arm* is one configuration that runs the same tasks: arm A is the pre-0.30 compiler, arms B′ and B are the 0.30 compiler (B′ with the old primer translated, B with the shipped primer), and #1605 adds a C# arm on the same pinned model. #1605 files every regression against this issue.

1. **Triage every regression filed here.** Keep one table in this issue: the construct or pair, the #1605 numbers, and one cause: a teaching gap, a lint gap, a diagnostic, or the form itself. What counts as a regression is what #1605 files under its registration (#1770); do not re-derive thresholds here. Also read the 0.30 arms' error logs in the archived #1605 run directories (`journal.jsonl`, field `diagnostics[].code`) for wrong-form errors, meaning the agent wrote a retired form or a C# habit that 0.30 rejects, and add a row for each form that recurs.
2. **Fix what is not a form change.**
   - Teaching gap: edit the single teaching source, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`, and regenerate with `calor self-check docs --fix`. The primer (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md`, served as `calor://primer`), `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md` and the `calor init` templates are generated from it and are not edited by hand. State the tokens added and removed against `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs`. No budget constant changes under this issue; the primer measures 1,569 o200k tokens against a budget of 1,600, and the full reference 16,750 against a ratchet of 17,000 (`TokenBudgetTests.cs:50-54`, `:57-63`).
   - Lint gap (an overlap the grammar allows, such as an `if` chain where `match` is shorter): extend the canonical-usage lints (`LosslessSourceDocument.DiagnoseCanonicalUsage`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:446`).
   - Diagnostic wording: fix it here. A diagnostic that needs a fix patch goes to #1790. Guidance for retired `§` markers (Calor0006) goes to #1788.
   - #1603 and #1622, which this issue used to name as the homes for teaching bugs and lint gaps, were closed on 2026-11-22. That work is done here and in #1790.
3. **Refer form changes; do not make them here.** When the cause is the form itself, add a row to the breaking-window record (#1784) with the #1605 error count for the form and its token cost. This includes forms whose errors trace to C# habit (`var` for a mutable binding, `=>` as a postcondition header, `set`, `type:name`, `each`): under the amended rule (#1785) such a form may be respelled toward C# at a stated token cost. #1784 decides, and "keep the form" is a valid decision. The row is added before the window record's payload and cut-off dates; a form found later is recorded there as deferred to a future edition. No parser change and no repository rewrite happens under this issue: compiler support for a decided respelling is #1793.
4. **Re-run the affected pairs** after the fixes and after the 0.32 rewrite. This is a paid agent run. Filing or amending this issue does not authorise spend.
   - Authorisation. The run happens only under the 0.32 spend record (#1816). Before any run that record states, for this re-run separately from the seeded-defect replication: a numeric spend ceiling; the abort triggers (at least: cumulative spend reaches the ceiling, the dry pass fails on any pair and arm, an arm canary fails); the one pinned model; the arms and the runs per arm; and the reading for each outcome.
   - Commit. The Calor arm is pinned to the post-rewrite commit that #1807 posts on this issue. The fixes from step 2 and from #1790 merge before that commit; list any that did not as "fixed, not re-measured".
   - Pair set. The pairs #1605 filed as regressed, at most three as #1816 Part B registers (if more were filed, the three with the largest registered regression; the others are listed as "fixed, not re-measured"), plus the two modify-a-converted-module pairs (#1789).
   - Arms. All arms run in one epoch (one complete collection, one pinned model). For the regressed pairs: the Calor arm at the pinned commit, arm A (`run-pair.sh --arm-repo-root <pre-0.30 checkout>`, built by the procedure #1766 documents) and C#. If #1770 registered the reduced design, which has no arm A, the comparison is with C# only. For the #1789 pairs, which have no pre-0.30 form: the Calor arm at the pinned commit and C#. Numbers from the #1605 epoch are not reused as a comparator, because `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:12` requires simultaneous arm-against-arm comparison within one epoch.
   - Dry pass first, at zero cost: `run-pair.sh --null-agent` on every pair and arm in the set. It applies the reference solution instead of calling an agent.
   - Readings, proposed here and fixed in #1816 before any run. *Recovered*: on a regressed pair the Calor arm no longer meets the regression rule #1770 registered; the triage row closes as fixed and confirmed. *Not recovered*: the row closes as not recovered with the re-run numbers, the release notes list the construct as a known regression, and a follow-up issue is filed and named on the edition declaration (#1805) as a candidate exception. *Too few valid runs to decide* under the registered validity rule: the row reads "fixed, not confirmed". The #1789 pairs have none of these three readings: they are reported as ratios to C#, and the base-rate reading that #1816 Part B registers (the #1770 rule applied to this re-run) is taken on them, with the null, missing-case and resource defects per arm set against the opportunity counts #1789 records.
   - Sizing input for the ceiling, measured on pre-0.30 syntax with an unpinned model: mean cost per run was $0.50 to $0.60 for C#, $3.65 to $4.20 for Calor without an exemplar and $1.17 to $2.23 with one (measured by summing `total_cost_usd` over `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/*/*/run-*/agent.json`: 180 runs, $370.52).
   - Decline. If the maintainer declines the spend in #1816, the re-run does not happen. This issue then closes on steps 1 to 3 with the decline linked, and the release notes say "fixed, not re-measured".
5. **Green-field gate.** Work on green-field authoring cost beyond #1734 is funded only if #1605 shows a penalty on 0.30 with the shipped primer, under the close rule registered in #1770. Apply the reading the registration gives. *Closed*: record "green-field workstream closed by #1605" here and open nothing. *Open*: the follow-up is scoped from the triage table and filed as new issues; it is not done under this one. *Not resolved*: record the intervals here, file nothing, and read the rule again only if the re-run of step 4 contains both penalised pairs.
6. **If there is no report.** If #1605 recorded "no paid run authorised", steps 1 to 3 and 5 record "no report" and file nothing; step 4 covers the two #1789 pairs only, or is declined in #1816.

## Done when

- Every regression that #1605 filed, and every recurring wrong-form error found in step 1, has a row in the triage table with one outcome: fixed (pull request linked), referred to #1784 (row linked), or kept with a recorded reason. Or "no report" is recorded under step 6.
- No pull request under this issue changes an existing spelling or adds a hard error.
- Every pull request that changes teaching text passes `calor self-check docs` and `TokenBudgetTests` with no budget constant changed.
- The re-run is published in the 0.32 release notes and added to the evidence ledger (#1774), with, per pair and arm: runs, iterations, cost and tokens as ratios to the C# arm of the same epoch, the command lines, the commit and the model pin. Or the maintainer's decline is recorded in #1816 and linked here.
- The reading of the green-field gate (*Closed*, *Open*, *Not resolved* or "no report") is recorded in this issue.

Part of #1649. Depends on #1605, #1789, #1807, #1816: steps 1 to 3 need only #1605, and step 4 needs all four. Blocks #1805.
```

**Why.** After the amendment the issue is one closable unit. Respelling decisions move to the window record, where a familiar C# spelling can win at a stated token cost; the old text confined any revisit to the fewest-tokens rule. Fix patches move to #1790. The re-run is a paid collection and had no authorisation, commit pin, dry pass or advance reading. The old done-when, "No construct regresses against arm A", could not be met by any pull request, so the re-run reports the result instead. The old step 2 pointed at #1603 and #1622, which are closed, and `docs/benchmarking/results.md` is generated and marked do-not-edit, so the release notes and the evidence ledger are the report venue.

### #1646 · [0.32] Apply the amended fewest-tokens rule to every 0.30 spelling across the named tokenizer set

**Action:** rewrite · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1785, #1799 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

*The current body is stale: it says the 0.30 spellings were first estimated with the `o200k` proxy tokenizer, but the decision record counted them with Anthropic `count_tokens` (`docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:7`), and its token-ratio item now belongs to #1746 (reporting) and #1844 (target).*

#### Change

Re-check every 0.30 spelling choice against the amended fewest-tokens rule (#1785) with each tokenizer that rule names, and hand every choice that no longer holds to the breaking-window record (#1784). This issue measures and classifies. It changes no grammar.

1. **List the choices.** One row per row of the "Measured choices" table in `docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md` (36 rows, lines 21 to 56), plus the spellings that record lists as never measured (line 572: the `switch`, `case` and `default` heads, and the typed and declaration pattern spellings).
2. **Prepare payloads.** For each row, the shipped spelling and every alternative the record compared. A payload that shows the shipped spelling must be a program the 0.30 compiler accepts. The payloads join the single window manifest (#1784: one manifest, one token-count dispatch per tokenizer for the whole batch); this issue does not dispatch on its own. Prepared payloads are not stored on `main`: the maintainer publishes them in a payload commit (`.github/workflows/syntax-token-count.yml:10-11`).
3. **Count** with the tool from #1799, once per tokenizer in the #1785 set.
4. **Classify each row.** *Confirmed* when the shipped spelling satisfies the amended rule; otherwise record the clause it fails (below the minimum win, or ranking changed on a named tokenizer).
5. **Hand over.** Each row that is not confirmed becomes a row in #1784 with its counts, before that record's cut-off date. Nothing is decided here.
6. **Record** the result as `docs/design/0.32-spelling-confirmation.md`, linked from the 0.30 record.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The rule in force is "fewest tokens, not the most readable one", with the C# spelling only as the first tie-break (`docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:3`, `:5`).
- Every count in that record came from Anthropic `/v1/messages/count_tokens` for one model, Claude Opus 5.5 (`:7-11`). No other tokenizer was used for spellings.
- 13 of the 36 rows are marked *tie* in whole or in part (measured by counting `*tie*` in lines 21 to 56). The record names its own weak decisions: `→` and `func` each win by one token in one context (`:570`), and Run C's wins are one or two tokens except for quantifiers (`:572`).
- The non-baseline payloads were "speculative hand-written text, not compiler-accepted syntax", on "a hand-picked declaration-heavy convenience sample" (`:15`).
- `scripts/count_tokens.py` contacts only `api.anthropic.com` (`:5`) and accepts only a fixed population of one primer, 20 converted and 20 agent files (`:264-267`), with optional `probes` (`:269`). It runs from the manual workflow `.github/workflows/syntax-token-count.yml` for the one model in `TARGET_MODEL` (`:50`). Nothing in the repository counts spellings with another vendor's tokenizer.
- What runs on the `o200k` proxy is not a spelling choice: the CI token budgets (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:10-25`) and the hand-measured density figure in #1746.

#### Why

The 0.30 spellings were chosen for one model's tokenizer, on a small sample, several of them by a single token. #1651 runs Calor under other vendors' agents in 0.33, and the edition declared at the end of 0.32 freezes existing spellings. This is the last point at which a spelling that wins on one tokenizer only, or by one token only, can change without breaking the edition.

#### Done when

- `docs/design/0.32-spelling-confirmation.md` has one row per choice from step 1 and, for each tokenizer in the #1785 set, the count for the shipped spelling, the count for each alternative, and the classification.
- The record names the manifest commit, its `inputs_sha256`, and one run or command per tokenizer, as the 0.30 record does at lines 7 to 11.
- Every row not classified *confirmed* links to its row in #1784, and that row was added before the record's cut-off date. A row that missed the date is listed in #1784 as deferred to a future edition.
- Every payload that shows the shipped spelling compiles with the 0.30 compiler, and the record names the command or test that checks it.
- No file under `src/` changes in the pull request that closes this issue.

#### Not in scope

- The Calor-to-C# token ratio (#1746 reports it, #1844 targets it) and the teaching token budgets (#1734).
- Choosing the minimum win and the tokenizer set (#1785), building the counting tool (#1799), deciding a flip (#1784), rewriting source (#1806, #1803).

Part of #1649. Depends on #1785, #1799. Feeds #1784.

### #1647 · [0.32] Default-path converter regressions after 0.30

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** bug, p1 · **Depends on:** #1782, #1779 · **Blocks:** #1844, #1848, #1854, #1859, #1866 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

**Current.** The issue asks to fix converter regressions that appear after 0.30 on three paths: default-mode `calor migrate`, the round-trip harness legs (#1586) and the member-level oracle (#1587), triaged by construct. It is done when the native fraction per corpus project is "at or above the start-of-0.30 baseline" and no corpus project fails to build or pass its tests after migration. The baseline is not given in numbers, and the one cause the repository has already recorded is not mentioned.

**Amendment.** Title unchanged. Three edits.

(1) Append to the list in `## Change`:

```markdown
- **the recorded cause on the round-trip harness:** give the harness project context, so that a file with `namespace X;` and its own `using` directives converts natively again (the follow-up written in `docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:363-367`), then raise the lossy floors back;
- **what #1782 hands over:** every project-validation diagnostic id and build error that remains on the migrated corpus projects after the CS8669 fix, with the file counts #1782 lists. #1782 builds the migrated projects with `-p:CalorEnforceEffects=false`. This issue's criterion is the same build with effect enforcement at its default, which is on (`src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:10`).
```

(2) Insert three new sections after `## Change`:

```markdown
## Parts

An umbrella, delivered as ordered pull requests and closed by the one done-when below.

1. Harness project context on the round-trip legs, with the lossy floors raised in the same pull request.
2. The items handed over by #1782, until the migrated MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation libraries build with effect enforcement on.
3. One pull request per further regression that triage finds on default `calor migrate`, either round-trip leg or the member oracle.
4. The three decisions below, each recorded in the pull request that implements it.

Parts 1 and 3 need neither dependency. Parts 2 and 4 read the baseline #1782 writes to `bench/corpus/README.md` and the reports of the corpus report job (#1779).

## Decisions to record

Each is the maintainer's. The pull request states the option taken.

| Decision | Options and what each costs | Recommended |
|---|---|---|
| A lossy floor that cannot be restored | (a) Keep the issue open until 17 of 32, 96 of 139 and 54 of 112 are reached: this holds the 0.32 exit and the 0.34 entry on files the converter preserves on purpose. (b) Record a lower floor: the pull request lists each file still preserved with its recorded reason and edits `blocking_floors.lossy` and `tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/ProjectConfigs.cs` together, as `_update_workflow.lowering` in `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json` requires. (c) Convert those files natively by moving their imports: this changes name lookup, which 0.30 chose not to do. | (b), and only for files whose remaining loss is `namespace-topology` after part 1. It lets the issue close without a quiet re-baseline. |
| Blocking native floors on the corpus report job | (a) Report only, as #1779 ships it: a fall in project-mode native files is seen only by someone reading the report. (b) Per-project floors at the counts measured when this issue closes, lowered only by a reviewed pull request: a scheduled job can then fail on `main` after a merge and needs an owner. | (b). The round-trip floors protect the harness legs only, and nothing else protects the project-mode path. |
| The native-fraction column of `scripts/default_migrate_report.py` (`native_file_fraction`, `:101`, `:152-164`) | (a) Keep: every pull request summary shows a directory-mode fraction that #1746 reports as zero by construction, because the copies have no project file and no references. (b) Remove the column and keep the step, which still fails when a discovered input is not written (`:188-200`). | (b), once the corpus report job publishes native counts. |

## Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

Native files per corpus project on the round-trip harness, from `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json`:

| Project | High-water mark before 0.30, lossy leg (`projects`) | 0.30 lossy floor (`blocking_floors.lossy`) | 0.30 default floor (`blocking_floors.default`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| MediatR | 17 of 32 | 10 of 32 | 5 of 32 |
| FluentValidation | 96 of 139 | 58 of 139 | 5 of 139 |
| Serilog | 54 of 112 | 36 of 112 | 10 of 112 |

- The lossy floors were lowered to the 0.30 measurement by a maintainer decision on 2026-11-18. The recorded cause: 0.30 keeps a file with `namespace X;` and its own `using` directives whole as C#, 14 of 32 MediatR files and 76 of 139 FluentValidation files (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/ProjectConfigs.cs:134-137`, `:192-195`; `docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:363-367`). `ProjectConfigs.cs` was lowered from 0.40 and 0.48; Serilog's value there (0.25, `:173`) did not change.
- The default floors are the values CI enforces today. Whether they changed during 0.30 is not verified; the triage document records only the lossy floors as lowered.
- Why those files are preserved: the converter sends a namespace that declares its own `using` directives to whole-file preservation (`RequiresWholeFileNamespaceInterop`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:2107`), on purpose, "rather than moving its imports or silently changing name lookup" (`docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:104-106`). It keeps a native projection of those imports only when the generated C# compiles and declares the same types (`namespace-topology`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/FeatureSupport.cs:826-832`).
- Why the harness does not get that projection, by reading the code (not run). The harness converts one file at a time through `CSharpToCalorConverter.Convert` (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/RoundTripPipeline.cs:634`, `:1590`). The projection compiles the original together with the sibling syntax trees and metadata references carried in the conversion options (`CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:410-417`), and the harness options set neither (`RoundTripPipeline.cs:1631-1647` for the default leg, `:1649-1690` for the lossy leg), although the harness already resolves each file's project references for its report (`:559`). On the lossy leg two more conditions fail: the lossless projection refuses a conversion that is not lossless with all preprocessor branches (`CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:395-397`), and the lossy one is taken only when `ValidateRoundTripCSharp` is true (`:1306-1312`), which the harness sets to false (`RoundTripPipeline.cs:1687`).
- CI enforces the floors in the two `roundtrip-verification` jobs with `python3 scripts/roundtrip_baselines.py check` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:1045-1048`).
- Not part of this issue: CS8669. In a project with nullable annotations and warnings as errors, a whole-file preserved original that uses `?` reports CS8669 and the files are marked unverified (`CHANGELOG.md:120-124`); #1746 measured MediatR and Serilog entirely whole-file preserved at an earlier compiler commit. #1782 fixes that in 0.31 and hands over what remains. The migrator checks converted Calor with effect enforcement off (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/ProjectMigrator.cs:853-854`), so a project it reports as verified has not been built with enforcement on.
```

(3) Replace `## Done when` and the closing line with:

```markdown
## Done when

- On `roundtrip-verification (lossy)`, native files are at least 17 of 32 (MediatR), 96 of 139 (FluentValidation) and 54 of 112 (Serilog). The same pull request raises `blocking_floors.lossy` in `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json` to at least those counts, and `MinimumNativeFraction` in `tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/ProjectConfigs.cs` to at least its pre-0.30 values (0.40 for MediatR, 0.48 for FluentValidation). For a project that cannot reach its mark, the lower floor is recorded as the first decision requires: the pull request lists each file still preserved with its reason, and the maintainer approves it.
- On `roundtrip-verification (default)`, native files stay at or above 5 of 32, 5 of 139 and 10 of 112.
- Default project-mode `calor migrate` writes at least as many native files per project as the first project-mode baseline that #1782 records in `bench/corpus/README.md`, read from the corpus report job (#1779) or, if that job has not landed, from the hand corpus check #1782 records.
- No corpus project fails to build or pass its tests after migration:
  - on both round-trip legs `round_trip_passed` stays at or above the committed values (MediatR 155 of 157, FluentValidation 865 of 866, Serilog 811 of 811), and the `member-oracle` jobs pass. These are the test evidence: the subjects' own suites are not run against the project-mode migrated libraries under this issue unless the maintainer adds that as a part;
  - the migrated MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation libraries build with exit code 0 through `src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets` with `CalorEnforceEffects` left at its default, as shown by the build status in the corpus report job's three reports or, if that job has not landed, by the hand corpus check #1782 records. An error reported by effect enforcement (for example Calor0410) may remain only if the maintainer records it in this issue as an exception, by code and file, with the open epic #1808 issue that owns it; with no such record the criterion is exit code 0;
  - every diagnostic id and build error #1782 handed over is fixed, or listed in this issue with its file count and the reason it remains.
- The three decisions are recorded, each in a merged pull request linked from this issue.

Native coverage for constructs that were never supported belongs to 0.34, not here.

Part of #1649. Depends on #1782, #1779. Blocks #1844, #1848, #1854, #1859, #1866. Its closure is a 0.32 exit criterion and the 0.34 entry criterion.
```

**Why.** "Start-of-0.30 baseline" was undefined, and the one file that looks like its source holds the lowered floors, so the criterion was either already met or unreachable. The numbers and the recorded follow-up make it checkable, and the floor decision gives the issue a way to close when a file is preserved by design. CS8669 is named as out of scope because #1746 attributes it to this issue and it is a different defect with its own 0.31 fix; what that fix leaves over lands here, where the build is judged with effect enforcement on. The recorded exception for effect diagnostics exists because converted corpus code can fail on unknown or undeclared effects, which epic #1808 owns (#1731, #1802); without it the 0.32 exit would wait on that work whether or not the maintainer wants it to.

### #1790 · [0.32] Fix coverage for agent-hit diagnostics

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1605 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Give agents a fix patch where they actually hit errors. A *fix* is a `SuggestedFix` attached to a diagnostic (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1595-1621`); a *safe* fix has `isSafeToApply: true` and is the only kind applied without review.

1. **The three most frequent codes.** From the published #1605 report, take the three diagnostic codes the 0.30 Calor arms hit most often. If the report has no per-code counts, compute them from the archived loop telemetry (`journal.jsonl`, field `diagnostics[].code`) and commit the command. For each code, at the sites agents hit, add a fix or record why no deterministic fix exists. If #1605 recorded "no paid run authorised", record "no #1605 report" in `docs/cli/fix.md` and do steps 2 to 7.
2. **Every wrong-form error.** These are the 0.30 errors for a short or mis-cased visibility or modifier, which the release notes say suggest the C# word (`CHANGELOG.md:52-56`). First pin by test which error each such word produces in each header kind (class, member, module-level function), because the hint depends on where the word lands (see the baseline). The inventory is every error whose hint comes from `RemovedVisibilityReplacement` or `RetiredModifierReplacement`, or that suggests the lower-cased spelling of the word written, plus any site that test shows reporting such a word with no hint. Each gets a fix that replaces the word, or a recorded reason. At HEAD the hinting sites are three, in `src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs`, all plain errors with no fix:
   - Calor0122, unknown visibility (`ParseVisibility`, `:5604-5618`; table at `:5622-5631`);
   - Calor0107, unknown class modifier (`:6481-6492`) and unknown member modifier (`:7091-7099`; table at `:7109-7121`).

   Classify the parameter-modifier case error (`:7048-7053`) as well.
3. **Mark a fix safe only where the rewrite is syntactic and has one reading**, such as `pub` to `public` on a class header, `Static` to `static`, `virt` to `virtual`. A replacement with a second plausible reading stays a suggestion: `int` in the visibility slot maps to `internal`, but a C# author may have meant a return type.
4. **Never mark safe a fix that touches an effect row or a contract.** #1801 pins this with a test; if it has merged, that test passes unchanged. If Calor0410 is among the three codes, it already has an unsafe fix (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1068`, `:1084`) and nothing changes.
5. **Make the compound-visibility text agree with the parser.** The hints and the Calor0122 word list spell the two compound visibilities as in C#, `protected internal` and `private protected` (`Parser.cs:5576-5577`, `:5628-5629`), and a declaration header joins those two words (`:4231-4234`). The teaching source says "compound visibility uses protected-internal or private-protected" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json:926-927`, generated into `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:802-804`) and has no compiled example of either. Add a test that every replacement a wrong-form hint names parses in the slot it is suggested for, and correct whichever text names a spelling the parser rejects (by reading, the reference entry). Regenerate with `calor self-check docs --fix`. This removes no accepted spelling.
6. **Decide the rule for old-form errors.** The 0.32 window (#1784) may turn some 0.30 spellings into errors at the cut-over (#1803). The batch rewriter (#1806) migrates files, but its rules are a separate class: they are never marked safe and are never applied by `calor fix --apply` or the MCP tools. Record in `docs/cli/fix.md` whether the error for such an old form carries its own fix, and when that fix may be safe. Recommended: the same test as step 3, and never for an edit to an effect row or a contract. The patches themselves are added with each cut-over error, not here.
7. **Record reasons** in `docs/cli/fix.md`, which already says why Calor1306 has no patch, and in `website/content/cli/fix.mdx`.

Merge before the commit that #1807 records for the 0.32 paid runs, so that the #1645 re-run measures it, and never between a commit pin and the end of that run's collection: this changes what a measured arm receives.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- 248 diagnostic codes (constants in `Diagnostic.cs`) and 21 fix construction sites (measured by `grep -rn "new SuggestedFix(" src --include=*.cs | grep -v Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs`).
- Five sites pass `isSafeToApply`: tab indentation and indent width (Calor0008, Calor0009), each only when the file's indentation is unambiguous (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:398`, `:407`); trailing whitespace and the conditional-return lint (Calor1300, Calor1305; `src/Calor.Compiler/Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:441`, `:481`); and legacy closer removal, Calor0830 (`Parser.cs:621`).
- The Calor0830 fix cannot be produced from source text: the 0.30 lexer turns every `§/…` closer into a Calor0006 error token (`Lexer.cs:741-764`), and the test for the fix reaches it by reflection (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/AgentDiagnosticTests.cs:546-567`). Four safe sites are reachable, all whitespace or lint. Not verified by running.
- Only safe fixes are applied automatically: by `calor fix --apply`, by MCP `calor_check` when called with `apply: true`, and by MCP `calor_compile`, whose `autoFix` is on by default (`CHANGELOG.md:60`, `:88-91`; `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/CheckTool.cs:55-58`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/CompileTool.cs:189`). `DiagnosticFixes.Repair` refuses a patch that changes the generated C# of a file that compiled (`Diagnostic.cs:1701-1738`).
- Wrong-form errors are reported at the header's start token, not at the offending word (for example `Parser.cs:6474`, `:6491`), so a fix needs the word's own span.
- By reading, not run: a word written after a declaration name that is not a visibility is collected as a modifier (`Parser.cs:4235-4243`). A short visibility such as `pub` then reaches the class-modifier site, which consults the visibility table (`:6486-6487`), or the member-modifier site, which does not (`:7093-7097`), or the module-level function check, which gives no hint (`:1313-1318`). A test pins the class case for `pro` and `pri` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ParserTests.cs:45-50`).
- The two-word compound visibilities are pinned as accepted by tests (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ParserTests.cs:27-40`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NativeEffectHeaderTests.cs:193-194`). Whether a header accepts the hyphenated spelling the reference gives is not verified; the lexer scans `-` as an operator (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:523`), and the hyphenated strings in `ParserTests.cs:20-21` are aliases inside a retired `§CL` marker, which that test expects to be rejected.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` pins the test count of each test project (`expectedTotal`), and `scripts/check_trx.py` fails CI on any difference (`.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).

#### Why

A safe patch is applied inside the same tool call, so the agent spends no edit on it. Today that happens only for whitespace and one lint. 0.30's wrong-form errors already name the replacement, which is the class where a patch is sound. This work was step 2 of #1645 and is split out so that #1645 can close on regressions. It adds no error and changes no spelling, so it is outside the breaking window and the standing rule for new hard errors does not apply. No existing issue is displaced; the capacity is new, justified because these are the errors agents were measured hitting. Fix coverage is reported and never a target: it could be raised by marking effect-row edits safe, which would undo effect checking.

#### Done when

- `docs/cli/fix.md` has a table of the three most frequent agent-hit codes from #1605 with their counts and, for each, the test that covers its new fix or the reason no fix exists; or it says "no #1605 report".
- Each site in the wrong-form inventory has a test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/AgentDiagnosticTests.cs` showing its fix, or a recorded reason in `docs/cli/fix.md`.
- For every fix marked safe here, a test applies it through `DiagnosticFixes.Repair` or MCP `calor_check` with `apply: true` and asserts that the result compiles and equals the hand-corrected source. For every other fix added here, a test asserts `IsSafeToApply` is false.
- A test asserts that each replacement named by a wrong-form hint parses in the slot it is suggested for, and `calor self-check docs` passes with the compound-visibility text in agreement.
- `docs/cli/fix.md` states the rule for old-form errors from step 6.
- `DiagnosticTokenBudgetTests` and `TokenBudgetTests` pass with no constant changed, and `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated for the tests added.
- The pull request reports, from the #1605 data where it exists, the share of agent-hit diagnostic occurrences whose code carries a fix and the share that is safe, before and after.

#### Not in scope

- Calor0006 guidance for retired markers (#1788), respelling any form (#1784), the batch rewriter (#1806), and the never-safe pin itself (#1801).

Part of #1649. Depends on #1605.

### #1788 · [0.32] Calor0006 names the replacement for every retired § marker

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1605 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

Calor0006 is the lexer error for a retired `§` section marker. Generate its guidance from one table that covers every retired marker, in place of the hand-written `switch` in `Lexer.ScanRetiredSectionMarker` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:741-764`).

1. **First, text only.** The 0.30 release note says every `§` marker is the error Calor0006, "which names the native form to use" (`CHANGELOG.md:31-32`, copied at `website/content/changelog.mdx:37-38`). That holds for 15 markers. Correct both copies to say what the error does today. This step changes nothing an agent arm reads and may merge before #1605 publishes.
2. **Inventory.** List every `§` marker a pre-0.30 compiler lexed. Source: the lexer at the pre-0.30 commit `d82f3ae95175ecd09581c6922701706be72b0ca9`, the baseline that `docs/cli/fix.md:52` and `docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:7` already use, cross-checked against the markers that occur in `.calr` files in the tree.
3. **Table.** One entry per marker: the replacement in 0.30 words. Where a form has no replacement, the entry says so and says what to write instead: the Option and Result markers (`§SM`, `§NN`, `§OK`, `§ERR`) stay removed (#1617; use nullable types and exceptions), and source IDs are kept by tooling in `.calor/ids.json`.
4. **Message.** `Legacy section marker '<marker>' is removed. <entry>`, on one line. A marker outside the inventory keeps today's generic sentence.
5. **Longer help.** Add a Calor0006 entry to `DiagnosticText.Rules` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/DiagnosticFormatter.cs:47-73`) so that `calor_help` explains the code and the message can stay short.
6. **Order.** Write entries first for the markers #1605 reports agents writing, then by frequency in the tree.

No fix patch is added: retired syntax stays an error with zero edits applied, as two tests pin (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/AgentDiagnosticTests.cs:354`, `:385`). Whether a retired-to-current rewriter exists is decided in #1806.

Steps 2 to 6 change text a measured arm reads. Do not merge them between the commit pin and the end of collection of a registered paid run (#1605, or the runs under #1816).

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The `switch` names a replacement for 15 markers (`§M`, `§F`, `§MT`, `§AF`, `§AMT`, `§B`, `§E`, `§Q`, `§S`, `§SN`, `§DP`, `§BR`, `§RAW`, `§CS`, `§CSHARP`), gives one sentence for every closer (`§/…`), and gives every other marker "Use canonical lowercase words and ordinary C# expression punctuation." (`Lexer.cs:751-760`).
- In the tree, 853 of 1,823 `.calr` files contain `§`, with 89 distinct marker spellings: 12 of the 15 named ones, 11 closers and 66 that get only the generic sentence. Those 66 are 20,643 of 39,222 occurrences (53%), led by `§R` 5,896, `§A` 4,825, `§C` 2,837, `§IF` 2,176, `§EL` 1,013 and `§ASSIGN` 875 (measured by `grep -rhoE "§/?[A-Za-z0-9_?^]*" --include=*.calr . | sort | uniq -c`). These are legacy files, not agent output.
- Calor0006 has no entry in `DiagnosticText.Rules` and none in `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/error-suggestions.json`. `calor_help` reads its explanation from `DiagnosticText.Explanation` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/HelpTool.cs:466`).
- Existing tests: `CanonicalCutover_RejectsEveryLegacyMarkerBoundary` checks the code for 16 markers (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/LexerTests.cs:236-261`), and one test pins the generic sentence for `§NEW` (`tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/State/DocumentStateTests.cs:199`), so that expectation changes with this issue.
- Budget: the median rendered diagnostic must stay within 40 `o200k` tokens (measured 30 over 44 cases, three of them Calor0006), and every rendered diagnostic is one line (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/DiagnosticTokenBudgetTests.cs:25-32`, `:162-204`).
- Not verified: the pre-0.30 lexer's marker list. The checkout used to write this issue holds one commit.

#### Why

Nearly half the `.calr` files in the tree (853 of 1,823) are still in the retired dialect, and no rewriter for it shipped with 0.30. An agent that writes a retired marker gets, for 66 of the 89 spellings in the tree, one generic sentence. A specific replacement in the error turns a guess into one edit. The size of the problem is the Calor0006 hits per run that #1605 reports. If it reports none for the 0.30 arms, or recorded "no paid run authorised", only step 1 is required in 0.32 and the rest moves to 0.33 with that count recorded. No existing issue is displaced: the change is one lexer function, one table and its tests. It adds no error and tightens none. It is off the 0.32 exit chain and may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.

#### Done when

- `CHANGELOG.md` and `website/content/changelog.mdx` no longer say that Calor0006 names the native form for every marker.
- A test enumerates the inventory and asserts that each marker lexes to Calor0006 with guidance that is not the generic sentence. The 66 spellings measured above are in the inventory, or the pull request lists each one left out with the reason.
- A test asserts that the guidance for the Option and Result markers and for `§Q` does not suggest a removed form.
- `MedianDiagnostic_FitsBudget` and `EveryRenderedDiagnostic_IsOneLine` pass with no constant changed, and the pull request states the token count of the longest Calor0006 message.
- `calor_help` returns an explanation for `Calor0006`.
- The pinned `§NEW` message in `DocumentStateTests.cs` is updated, and `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated for the tests added (`scripts/check_trx.py` fails CI on a count that differs).
- The pull request lists the inventory with its source, and the markers #1605 reported agents writing.

#### Not in scope

- A fix patch or rewriter for retired syntax (#1806); the legacy-dialect allowlist and self-check (#1763); the other removed-form errors (Calor0003, Calor0007), which already name their replacement.

Part of #1649. Depends on #1605.

### #1789 · [0.32] Author two modify-a-converted-module pairs on one migrated corpus project

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Depends on:** #1782, #1766 · **Blocks:** #1645, #1816 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Author two benchmark pairs under `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/`, each a modification task on a slice of one corpus project as default migration leaves it. A *pair* is one task with a C# arm and a Calor arm that share a behavioural spec and one hidden test suite; `run-pair.sh` runs one arm of one pair.

1. **Project.** Choose one project under `bench/corpus/` (pins in `bench/corpus/README.md`) from the project-mode baseline table #1782 adds to that file: it must have native files. In the one measurement on record, FluentValidation was the only project with native files (#1746). If the chosen project's migrated slice does not build after #1782, the remaining build errors belong to #1647; add #1647 as a dependency of this issue and say so in a comment.
2. **Slice.** Choose source files that build on their own. The harness writes both arms' project files, with no package reference and no per-pair override (`run-pair.sh:729-778`), and the Calor arm builds with effect enforcement on (`templates/calor-arm/CalorArm.csproj.template`, `CalorEnforceEffects`), which the migrator's own check does not use.
3. **Starters from one commit.** `csharp/` is the slice's C# at the corpus pin. `calor/` is what default project-mode `calor migrate` writes for those files at one recorded compiler commit, unedited. The files the task changes must be native Calor in that output, not preserved C#. Record the project, the pin, the compiler commit and the command in `pair.json` under `parity.notes`, with any hand edit and its reason.
4. **Task.** `spec.md` states one arm-neutral change to existing behaviour and is written before the references. The starters fail the hidden suite; the references pass it.
5. **Hidden suite and shims.** One xUnit suite in `tests/`, bound through `tests/shims/TestShim.csharp.cs` and `tests/shims/TestShim.calor.cs`.
6. **References.** `reference/csharp/` and `reference/calor/`: the solved slice in each language.
7. **Manifest.** `pair.json` valid against `task-spec-schema.json`: category `N1` (neutral; the wedge categories require contracts that converted code does not carry), ids `N1-006-<slug>` and `N1-007-<slug>`, `iterationBudget` 10, and the `parity` counts, which must be within 30% across arms (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:49`).
8. **Opportunities.** An *opportunity* is a place in the code the agent must read or change where a defect of a class can occur: a nullable-typed value (null), a match or switch over a non-`bool` type (missing case), an `IDisposable` local (resource). Across the two pairs each class reaches the minimum that #1770 registers in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`; if the registration gives none, five. Count them on the references with the counter from #1760 if it has landed, otherwise by hand.
9. **Source location.** These pairs have no retired-syntax original, so their `.calr` files live in the pair directory and not in the `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax` mirror, which admits only copies of registered originals. If the 0.30 source-selection option that #1766 adds reads Calor sources only from the mirror, extend it to read a pair that has no mirror entry from its own directory, with a harness test.

If this merges before the 0.32 rewrite (#1803), the rewrite migrates the two pairs and #1807 validates them again. If it merges after, author the Calor files in the post-rewrite syntax and run the validation below at the commit #1807 records or later.

This issue runs no paid agent. Validation is `--null-agent`, which applies the reference instead of calling an agent.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

Paths without a directory are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

- The 50 `.calr` files of the 25 pairs translated to 0.30 syntax (`tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs`) contain no `match`, `switch`, `enum`, `using`, `null` or `?`-suffixed type (measured with `grep -rlE` for each). A count of null, missing-case or resource defects taken on them is zero by construction.
- The existing modification pairs are hand-written single-file fixtures: `pairs/W2-005-scheduler` is one file per arm, 12 functions in the starter and 13 in the reference.
- `run-pair.sh` copies the arm's fixture directory into the workspace (`:736`), writes the C# project inline (`:767-777`) or renders `templates/calor-arm/CalorArm.csproj.template`, which compiles `**/*.calr` with `CalorEnforceEffects` true, and copies `tests/*.cs` plus `tests/shims/TestShim.<arm>.cs` into the hidden project (`:781-782`). Both projects target `net10.0`.
- The migrator checks converted Calor with effect enforcement off (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/ProjectMigrator.cs:853-854`), so migrate output that it reports as verified has not been built the way the Calor arm builds it.
- `--null-agent` builds the starter, copies `reference/<arm>` over it, builds again and runs the hidden suite with no API call (`:1270-1293`). `result.json` records `nullAgent`, `taskSuccess` and `escapedBugs` (`:1648-1666`), and every observed build appends a `journal.jsonl` record with `heldout_fail` (`:1167`).
- Two 0.31 results are prerequisites. The Calor arm cannot run 0.30 source today, because the arm canary is retired syntax (`run-pair.sh:553`, `:575`; fixed by #1766). In a project with nullable annotations and warnings as errors, a preserved original that uses `?` reports CS8669 and is written but marked unverified (`CHANGELOG.md:120-124`); #1746 measured MediatR and Serilog with no native file at an earlier compiler commit (re-measured and fixed by #1782).
- `BenchCorpusCompilesTests` compiles each `pairs/*/reference/calor/*.calr` file on its own (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/BenchCorpusCompilesTests.cs:38-72`), so a multi-file reference with cross-file types fails it as written. It is a theory with one case per file, and `eng/test-manifest.json` pins the test count of the project (`scripts/check_trx.py` fails CI on a difference).
- Hidden suites must be shown deterministic by five consecutive green reference runs, committed under `epochs/authoring-validation-*/` (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:13`, `:52`; format in `epochs/authoring-validation-wave3/determinism-log.txt`).
- Not verified: which corpus slices build alone. The corpus submodules were not initialised in the checkout used to write this issue. Serilog is the only one of the three whose library targets `net10.0` at its pin (`bench/corpus/README.md:13-17`).

#### Why

The strategy's adoption path is per-module migration, where the Calor already in a module is the agent's reference; the pre-0.30 measurements put modification of existing Calor at parity with C# (`docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md:370-373`). No pair exercises that on converted code. The green-field pairs also cannot exhibit null, missing-case or resource defects, so a base-rate reading taken on them is "not measurable". These pairs run in the #1645 re-run, after the window cut-off, so their reading feeds the conditional 0.35 guarantees (#1869, #1871) and not the 0.32 window decision (#1800), which uses a static opportunity count. No existing issue is displaced: the capacity is new, justified because without these pairs the #1645 re-run and the base-rate reading have no task on which the three classes can occur.

#### Done when

- Both pair directories exist with the layout in steps 3 to 7, and `./run-pair.sh --check-pair-config <pair>/pair.json calor` and `... csharp` exit 0.
- For each pair and arm, `./run-pair.sh --pair pairs/<id> --arm <arm> --null-agent --out epochs/authoring-validation-converted-null` writes a `result.json` with `"nullAgent": true`, `"taskSuccess": true` and `"escapedBugs": 0`, and the first record of the run's `journal.jsonl` (the unmodified starter) has `heldout_fail` above 0.
- Five consecutive such runs per pair and arm are committed as a determinism log beside those results.
- `BenchCorpusCompilesTests` passes with the new references present, and `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated for the added cases. If a reference file cannot compile alone, extend the test to compile that pair's reference as one project; do not exclude the pair.
- The pull request lists the opportunity counts per class with file and line, each class reaches the minimum, and for each pair it states how many files in `calor/` are native and how many are preserved.

#### Not in scope

- Running an agent on the pairs (the #1645 re-run, under #1816).
- The opportunity counter (#1760), a third arm or analyzer packages in the C# project (#1815), and pairs on a second corpus project.

Part of #1649. Depends on #1782, #1766. Blocks #1645, #1816.

### #1746 · [0.32] Code density: migrated Calor token ratio reported per project

**Action:** move-in (from 0.31) · **Labels:** ci, enhancement · **Depends on:** #1779 · **Blocks:** #1844 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current.** A follow-up from #1604 and #1606. It records one hand measurement of 2026-11-20 with the `o200k` proxy tokenizer on project-mode default `calor migrate`: overall 1.016 times the C# tokens, 0.859 on the native subset, with MediatR and Serilog entirely whole-file preserved because of CS8669, which it says is tracked in #1647. It is done when CI reports the ratio per corpus project on each run and the overall ratio is below 1.0 or the target is revised.

**Amendment.** New title: `[0.32] Code density: migrated Calor token ratio reported per project`. Milestone 0.32. Labels `ci`, `enhancement` (the issue has none). Three edits to the body.

(1) In the paragraph under the table, replace "because of CS8669 (tracked in #1647)" with:

```markdown
because of CS8669 (measured at compiler 606dae6f2; re-measured and fixed under #1782. #1647 covers a different regression, on the round-trip harness, and takes over whatever #1782 leaves)
```

(2) Replace the `Done when:` list with:

```markdown
Done when:
1. The corpus report job (#1779) writes, in each project's JSON report (added the way that job documents for later columns) and in its step summary, for MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation:
   - the native file fraction, from the native and preserved classes the job already assigns from each file's `losses` list;
   - the token ratio overall: tokens of every written `.calr` file over tokens of the C# source file it replaces, summed per project;
   - the token ratio on native files and, separately, on preserved files, each with its file count;
   - a "with contracts" row: the ratio over native files in which the compiler reports at least one parameter refinement or postcondition, with the file count. It reads `n/a (0 files)` when there are none, so that the ratio of contract-free output is never presented as the density of code that carries contracts. The pull request names the compiler output used to detect them.
2. The per-run numbers are counted offline with `o200k_base`, and each column says "o200k proxy". The only o200k counter in the repository is `ProxyTokenizer` in the C# test project (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:10-25`; packages pinned at `Directory.Packages.props:21-22`). Use the offline counter from #1799 if it has landed; otherwise add a small counter on the same pinned packages. A test asserts that the counter returns the same count as `ProxyTokenizer.Count` on a checked-in `.calr` file and a checked-in `.cs` file. There is no fallback to a word-count estimate (`scripts/token_delta_spot.py` has one and is not used).
3. The numbers come from the job's buildable, default-migrated copies, never from source-only copies. (The existing step at `.github/workflows/test.yml:1028-1034` runs `scripts/default_migrate_report.py` on source-only copies and records `build: not-run`.)
4. The Anthropic `count_tokens` figure is produced once per release, not on every run: one dispatch of `.github/workflows/syntax-token-count.yml`, which needs a protected environment, a maintainer-published payload commit, a digest and a typed confirmation. The notes of the release in which this issue closes carry that figure with its run link, or the line "count_tokens density figure not produced" with the reason. `scripts/count_tokens.py:264-267` accepts only its fixed 41-file population, so corpus files go in as `probes` or through the tool from #1799; this issue does not change that script. Counting all 283 corpus files (32 + 139 + 112) in both languages needs `max_requests` above the workflow's default of 400 (`:34-37`).

The below-1.0× target is no longer part of this issue. It is #1844 (0.34), which depends on this report, on #1647 and on #1742.

Off the 0.32 exit chain: this issue may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.
```

(3) Append after the `Done when:` list:

```markdown
Hand method, for issues that need a density figure before the columns land: run default project-mode `calor migrate` on each restored library project as #1782 records in `bench/corpus/README.md`; count `o200k_base` tokens of every written `.calr` file and of the C# source file it replaces; report the two sums and their ratio per project, with the compiler commit.
```

**Why.** The 0.859 figure comes from one project, because the other two had no native file. The converter writes no contracts (by reading, `RoslynSyntaxVisitor` passes empty precondition and postcondition lists, for example `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/RoslynSyntaxVisitor.cs:3970-3971`), so that figure is the density of contract-free code and the "with contracts" row will read `n/a (0 files)` until something produces them. The issue itself says the existing CI report shows zero native files by construction. An Anthropic count cannot run on every CI run: the only path is a maintainer-dispatched workflow for one model. The job that produces buildable migrated copies is #1779. The target moves behind its prerequisites to #1844. The hand method is written down because the window record (#1784) and the effect work in 0.32 fall back to it if these columns slip.

**Moved from** 0.31 because it reports on output that exists only after #1782 and #1779, it is on no 0.31 exit criterion, and 0.31 is kept to making the instruments run.

### #909 · [0.32] DivisionByZeroChecker double-reports divisions nested under bound wrapper nodes; Calor0926 suggests over-strong contracts for deferred divisions

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** bug · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current.** The issue reports two analyzer defects found in a review of #908. A division nested under a wrapper expression is reported once per traversal level, so the `??` probe gives two Calor0920 against one for the plain division. The Calor0926 suggester proposes a function-level condition for a division that only runs conditionally, such as the fallback of `??`, and should consult `BoundChildren.DeferredOf()` first. The body names `DivisionByZeroChecker.CheckExpression` and `ContainsDivision`, is written in retired syntax, and has no done-when.

**Amendment.** The two paragraphs "Double-reporting" and "Over-strong suggestion" are replaced with current-syntax text by the tracker re-baseline (#1759). After that edit, append the following. If step 3 applies, retitle to `[0.32] Calor0926 suggests an over-strong refinement for a conditionally executed division`.

````markdown
## Re-test on 0.30 first

1. Compile this probe with `calor --input Probe.calr --output Probe.g.cs --analyze --all-findings` (Calor0926 is suppressed without `--all-findings`) and count the Calor0920 and Calor0926 diagnostics. It is a candidate in 0.30 syntax; confirm it compiles.

   ```calor
   module Probe
     func Fallback (int?:x, int:y) → int
       !
       return x ?? 10 / y
   ```

2. Do the same with the body `return 10 / y`.
3. If both forms report Calor0920 the same number of times, the double report is gone: strike the "Double-reporting" paragraph, post the two counts as a comment, and narrow this issue to the Calor0926 suggestion.
4. If the `??` form reports more, fix it in `src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/TypedBugPatternAnalysis.cs`, which now holds the traversal.

## Done when

- A test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Analysis/BugPatternTests.cs` compiles both forms and asserts the same number of Calor0920 for each.
- A test asserts that Calor0926 is not reported for a division that executes only conditionally (the right operand of `??`), and is still reported for the unconditional `10 / y`.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated for the tests added.
````

**Why.** The double-report half names code that no longer exists: `src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/Patterns/DivisionByZeroChecker.cs` is a 23-line wrapper over `TypedBugPatternAnalysis.Check`, which drops a diagnostic it has already reported for the same span, code and message (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/TypedBugPatternAnalysis.cs:38`, `:2733`). The Calor0926 half is still live by reading: `PreconditionSuggester.CheckExpression` recurses into every child without consulting `BoundChildren.DeferredOf` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/Patterns/PreconditionSuggester.cs:154-197`; `src/Calor.Compiler/Binding/BoundNodes.cs:1005`) and suggests the refinement `y | (# != 0)`. Neither was run. The issue stays in 0.32 as diagnostic-quality backlog; it is off the exit chain and may slip to 0.33, or be deferred with a reason, without holding the release.

**Action:** keep · **Labels:** bug · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

Delivers a correct diagnosis after a lossy partial-class merge: compiling one converted file whose code uses a type declared in another file must not report Calor0410 (undeclared effect); the cross-file type is resolved, or the message says the effect is unknown because of a cross-file reference. ### #1142 · [0.32] Delete the configured-vs-defaulted trap in EffectEnforcementPass's caps (follow-up to #1141)

**Action:** keep · **Labels:** enhancement · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

Delivers the removal of a trap in the effect pass: assigning the fixpoint iteration cap at all, even to its default value, silently switches off the size floor, because a private flag records that the cap was set. The issue replaces the flag with a nullable cap so that "not set" means the default, records three constructs of the same shape, and asks for the tightness of the floor to be proved or loosened. The trap is still present at HEAD `bc80daeb` (`_sccCapInjected`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:74-111`). It stays in 0.32 as internal hardening with no user-visible change, under this epic's done-when, which allows it to be deferred with a reason; it is off the exit chain and may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.
