## Epic #1787 · [0.32] Epic: last breaking window — the decisions

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Take every decision that would change a spelling the 0.30 compiler accepts, or reject a program that compiles today, once: under one amended spelling rule, measured from one payload manifest, by one dated cut-off. Anything undecided on that date keeps its 0.30 behaviour and waits for a future edition.

#### Why

The 0.30 syntax is still open on purpose. #1646 says "Flip any choice whose ranking changes ... a parse-error change plus a repo rewrite". #1645 says "If the form itself is the problem, revisit it". #1735 changes what compiled programs do at run time. The parser still accepts brace-header forms that the 0.30 cleanup left as "a separate decision" (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:542-544`). At the same time #1653 (0.33, agent reach) is premised on a stable language: "Launching earlier would teach agents a language that's about to change."

The last breaking change was expensive. 0.30 shipped "with no automatic rewriter for 0.29 source" (`CHANGELOG.md:12-13`), a full compiler-test run during its cut-over had 1,130 of 22,251 tests failing (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:9`), and the proof-soundness packet is still frozen in the retired syntax (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/templates.json`, 136 templates). If the pending decisions land one at a time, each one repeats part of that cost and each one invalidates the instruments that 0.31 restores. Taken as one batch, one rewriter (#1806) applies one rewrite (#1803), the instruments are re-registered once (#1807), and the edition can be declared (#1805).

The rule the decisions are taken under needs amending first. The 0.30 rule is "the variant with the fewest tokens, not the most readable one", with the C# spelling only as a tie-break, measured on one tokenizer (`docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:3-7`), and the record lists its own one-token wins as weak (`:570`).

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1784 | Breaking-window decision record: the batch table, the cut-off date, the staging rule, one payload manifest, the batch acceptance measurement, and the order of the steps that follow |
| #1785 | Amend the fewest-tokens rule: a minimum win, a named tokenizer set, and agent error counts as a ground for respelling toward C# |
| #1799 | Tooling that counts one payload manifest with each tokenizer the rule names |
| #1794 | Decide the brace-header forms the 0.30 parser still accepts |
| #1797 | Decide the union declaration spelling (additive unless it reserves a word; implemented by #1643 in 0.34) |
| #1796 | Decide whether a redundant `public` is canonical |
| #1800 | Decide every rejecting guarantee: null remainder, enum-match exhaustiveness, making the bool exhaustiveness check effective, obligation verification as a default-build check, and what counts as breaking |
| #1743 | The converter never emits an `else` whose body is only an `if`; the shipped error is recorded (not a breaking decision; may slip) |
| #1741 | Remove 0.30 residue: legacy `dbr`/`dbw` effect values, Lisp-era operator hints, inline-sibling counter, dead `AttributeHelper` code (not breaking; may slip) |
| #1735 | Inherited parameter refinements behave like direct ones |
| #1795 | Round-trip leg compiled with the default contract mode and with effects enforced, plus three compile-options canary tests |

#### Sequence

1. #1784 opens the record first (table, cut-off date, staging rule, order) and stays open until the rewrite has landed.
2. #1785 (the rule) before any spelling decision, because every spelling decision cites it.
3. #1799 (tooling) before the token count is dispatched, because the rule names tokenizers nothing in the repository can count with.
4. The spelling decisions share one payload manifest and one dispatch per tokenizer: the #1646 and #1645 rows (epic #1649), #1794, #1797, #1796 and the edition-marker spelling (#1084, epic #1792). The manifest is published on the record's payload date, which precedes the cut-off.
5. In parallel with 2 to 4: #1800, once the #1605 report (or its recorded "no paid run authorised"), the corpus report job (#1779) and the #1084 design record, which defines the opt-in mechanism, are available; #1735 together with #1795.
6. On the cut-off date the record is decided. #1793 and #1806 (epic #1792) start from the decided record. #1784 is closed after #1803 has landed, or after the rewrite inventory records that no rewrite was needed; its before and after acceptance measurement reads the corpus report job (#1779).
7. #1743 and #1741 are off this chain. Neither changes an accepted spelling; each can land at any time and may slip to 0.33.

#### Done when

- The amended rule is recorded (#1785) and one command per tokenizer counts the same manifest (#1799).
- Every row of the window record carries a decision dated on or before the cut-off, or reads "deferred to a future edition" with a reason.
- #1800 has recorded one outcome for each rejecting candidate.
- #1735 has landed and the strict round-trip leg (#1795) runs in CI.
- #1784 is closed: every row was decided or deferred by the cut-off date, every row decided "land" has merged, and the rewrite has landed or the inventory records that none was needed. This is the first 0.32 exit criterion.
- #1743 and #1741 are closed, or moved to 0.33 with their rows in the window record filled.

#### Not in this epic

- The per-module edition marker (#1084), compiler support for the decided forms (#1793), the rewriter (#1806), the rewrite itself (#1803), re-registering the instruments (#1807) and the edition declaration (#1805): epic #1792.
- Applying the amended rule to the 0.30 spellings (#1646) and acting on #1605 regressions (#1645): epic #1649. Their outcomes are rows in this epic's record.
- Sound effect contracts (#1731): epic #1808. Only a new effect spelling, or a program it newly rejects, is a row here.
- The guard-derived precondition decision (#1847): none of its options changes an existing spelling.
- Implementing unions (#1643, 0.34), null-gap checks (#1869) or enum exhaustiveness (#1871).
- Follow-up with no owning issue: reporting the static opportunity counts #1800 reads (enum-typed `match` targets, null-gap sites) as columns of the corpus report job (#1779). Until an issue owns that, #1800 takes the counts by commands written into its record.
- Option and Result stay removed (#1617). `requires` stays removed (#1618).

### #1784 · [0.32] Breaking-window decision record

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1779, #1785, #1799 · **Blocks:** #1793, #1806, #1805 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Create the decision record for the 0.32 breaking window at `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md` and keep it current until it is closed. This issue is a record. It changes no compiler code.

The breaking window is the 0.32 milestone: the last release in which an existing spelling may change. It ends when #1805 declares the edition, a written compatibility promise for the language as it then stands. "Breaking" is defined once, in the #1800 rows of this record: a removed or re-meant spelling, a change in what a compiling program does at run time, a newly reserved word, or a new default-on hard error.

The record has seven parts.

**1. The batch table.** One row per pending decision. Columns: item; owning issue; decision (land, keep as is, or defer to a future edition); date decided; changes `.calr` source (yes or no; yes means #1806 needs a rewriter rule); token cost per tokenizer (from part 4); forms used by the soundness packet (which templates of the registered proof-soundness packet contain the affected form, so that a respelling states how many hash-pinned cases must be re-frozen under #1807); landed in (pull request), or the reason for deferral.

Rows at filing:

| Row | Owner | Known at filing |
|---|---|---|
| Each 0.30 spelling whose ranking changes under the amended rule | #1646 | |
| Each respelling toward C# proposed from agent error counts (`var`, header `=>`, `set`, `type:name`, `each`), with its token cost | #1645 | |
| Brace-header forms | #1794 | |
| Union declaration spelling | #1797 | Additive. Changes `.calr` source: no, unless the chosen spelling reserves a word |
| Redundant `public` | #1796 | Enforced by lint only; neither form becomes an error |
| Edition-marker spelling | #1084 | |
| Any new effect spelling, and any program newly rejected, under sound effect contracts | #1731 | |
| Each rejecting guarantee: null remainder (one row per gap), enum-match exhaustiveness, making the bool exhaustiveness check effective, and whether obligation verification may become a default-build check | #1800 | |
| Inherited parameter refinements | #1735 | Changes `.calr` source: no. Changes emitted behaviour: yes |
| An `else` whose body is only an `if` | #1743 | The error shipped in 0.30. Changes `.calr` source: no |
| 0.30 residue removal | #1741 | Internal cleanup. Changes `.calr` source: no |

**2. The cut-off date.** A maintainer decision, recorded as one calendar date.

| Option | Cost |
|---|---|
| (a) A calendar date fixed when the record is created | A decision that is not ready is deferred for a whole edition. |
| (b) No date; the window closes when the last row is decided | The rewriter cannot be written against an open batch, so the marker, rewriter, rewrite, re-registration, both paid collections and the declaration all wait on the slowest decision. |

The plan recommends (a), set on the day the 0.32 milestone opens and set early, because every later step queues behind it. The date itself is the maintainer's. A row with no decision on that date is recorded as "deferred to a future edition": its 0.30 behaviour stays for the whole edition, and a deferred rejecting guarantee can later ship only in a form #1800 allows.

The record also carries a payload date, earlier than the cut-off: the manifest of part 4 is published and dispatched on that date. A spelling row whose payloads are not in that manifest is deferred to a future edition, unless the maintainer records a second dispatch with its own payload commit.

**3. The staging rule.** For every row marked "changes `.calr` source: yes", the old form stays accepted, with a warning at most, until #1803. That one pull request applies the rewrite and turns every old form of the batch into an error. No row makes an old form an error on its own, so the tree is migrated exactly once. A rejecting guarantee that #1800 decides to land follows the same rule: its check reports a warning at most until #1803. Compiler support for a decided form that has no owning implementation issue is carried by #1793. A row whose decision keeps the old form legal (#1796 is enforced by lint only) says so in its decision cell, and #1803 does not turn it into an error.

**4. One payload manifest, one dispatch per tokenizer.** A payload manifest is the JSON file `scripts/count_tokens.py` reads: it lists the text files to count and their variants. Every decision issue adds its candidate payload files to one manifest as `probes` (the validator still requires the fixed 41-file population; see baseline). On the payload date the maintainer publishes it as one payload commit and dispatches the count once per tokenizer in the #1785 set, using the #1799 tooling. The record links the payload commit, its `inputs_sha256` and each run.

**5. The standing acceptance rule, measured once for the batch.** One measurement before and one after, for the batch. If #1803 performs a rewrite, the two commits are its parent commit and its head, and the measurement is always taken, because the cut-over itself makes old forms errors. If #1803 records that no rewrite was needed, the two commits are the parent of the first merged pull request of a row that adds or tightens a hard error and the merge commit of the last such row. The measurement checks:

- the conversion scorecard's native count (the `conversion-scorecard` CI job, artifact `conversion-scorecard.json`) does not fall;
- the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens divided by original C# tokens, per corpus project) does not rise;
- the corpus files that newly fall to whole-file preservation (the converter keeping a C# file verbatim instead of writing native Calor) are counted and named.

The density ratio and the preserved-file list come from the corpus report job (#1779, with the token-ratio columns #1746 adds), dispatched on each of the two commits. If that job or its columns are not available, both values are measured by the hand method #1746 records (project-mode default `calor migrate` on MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation, counted with the o200k proxy tokenizer), and the record says which method was used.

**6. The order.** The #1084 design record merges before the cut-off (#1800 reads it and the marker spelling is a row). Decisions complete at the cut-off; then the #1084 implementation and #1793 (compiler support for the decided forms, old forms still accepted); #1806 (rewriter); #1803 (the rewrite lands once and performs the cut-over); #1807 (instruments re-registered); then #1816, #1810 and the #1645 re-run pin the post-rewrite commit; then #1805 declares the edition.

**7. Two states.** The record's first line carries its status.

- *Decided*: the cut-off date has passed and every row has a recorded outcome. #1806 and #1793 depend on this state, not on closure.
- *Closed*: #1803 has landed, or its inventory records that no rewrite was needed because no decision changed source.

The record also states that the guard-derived precondition decision (#1847) is not in the batch, because none of its options changes an existing spelling.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- No window record exists. The only spelling record is `docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md`.
- The pending decisions sit in separate issues: #1646 ("Flip any choice whose ranking changes"), #1645 ("If the form itself is the problem, revisit it"), #1743, #1741, #1735, and the brace-header forms (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:542-544`).
- A token count is a maintainer action: `.github/workflows/syntax-token-count.yml` runs only on `workflow_dispatch` (`:21-22`), takes a `payload_commit` and an `inputs_sha256` (`:24-33`), needs a typed confirmation (`:70`) and the pre-existing environment `anthropic-count-tokens` (`:121`), and counts for one fixed model (`TARGET_MODEL: "Claude Opus 5.5"`, `:50`). Payloads "are not stored on main" (`:10`).
- `scripts/count_tokens.py` rejects a manifest whose population is not one primer, 20 converted files and 20 agent files (`:264-267`); additional payloads are optional `probes` (`:269`), reported separately.
- The soundness packet at HEAD is in the retired syntax and frozen: 136 templates in `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/templates.json`, `"status": "FROZEN-AT-MERGE"` (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/registration.json:9`), "Do not edit the fixture to make the job green" (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:69`). The packet column is therefore filled from the 0.30-syntax successor packet that #1778 registers.
- The conversion scorecard is the `conversion-scorecard` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:672`). Its test pins `BASELINE_FULLY_CONVERTED = 100` (`tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/ConversionScorecardTests.cs:31`); `CHANGELOG.md:166-168` reports 101 of 106 scorecard inputs native. The two figures are not reconciled here: the "before" value is whatever the job reports on the pre-rewrite commit.
- The density ratio was 1.016 overall on 2026-11-20, measured by hand with the o200k proxy tokenizer on 0.30 syntax (#1746). No CI job reports it per project yet; #1779 and #1746 add that.

#### Why

The first 0.32 exit criterion is that this record is closed. Without it the pending decisions land separately, each with its own repository rewrite, its own token dispatch and its own re-freeze of the soundness packet, and the edition would be declared over spellings that were still moving. With it the rewriter has a fixed input, each respelling states what it costs in tokens and in re-frozen cases before it is chosen, and a decision that is not ready by the date is deferred in writing.

#### Done when

- `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md` exists with the seven parts; the cut-off and the payload date are calendar dates, and the payload date is the earlier.
- **Decided state.** In a commit made on or after the cut-off date the record's status line reads "decided", and every row has a decision and a date, or reads "deferred to a future edition" with a one-line reason.
- The record links one payload commit, one `inputs_sha256` and one run per tokenizer, and every token cost in the table cites those runs. If the maintainer recorded a second dispatch, the record links its payload commit, digest and runs as well, names the rows it served, and gives the reason.
- Every row marked "changes `.calr` source: yes" names its rewriter rule in #1806 and the pull request that added compiler support for the new form (its owning issue, or #1793). The #1743 and #1741 rows read "no". Neither changes what the compiler accepts, so those two rows may link their open issue in place of a pull request and do not hold the record open if the work slips.
- **Closed state.** The status line reads "closed" and all of the following hold:
  - #1803 has merged, or its inventory records that no rewrite was needed.
  - Every other row marked "land" links the pull request it landed in.
  - The record holds the before and after measurement of part 5 with both commit hashes and the method used. The native count is not lower and the density ratio is not higher, or the row responsible has been reverted or deferred. The list of newly preserved files is present, even if empty. Only if no rewrite was performed and no row added or tightened a hard error does the record say so in place of the measurement.

#### Not in scope

Implementing any row, the rewriter, the rewrite, the re-registration and the edition declaration. Each has its own issue.

Part of epic #1787. Depends on #1779 (the corpus report job the acceptance measurement reads; the hand method in part 5 is the fallback if it slips), #1785 (the rule every spelling row cites) and #1799 (the counters the dispatch uses). Blocks #1806 and #1793, both from the decided state, not from closure.

### #1785 · [0.32] Amend the fewest-tokens rule: a minimum win and a named tokenizer set

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** documentation · **Depends on:** #1770 · **Blocks:** #1646, #1784, #1799, #1794, #1797, #1796 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Record an amended spelling rule as the first section of `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md` (create the file with only this section if #1784 has not created it yet), and add a one-line pointer to it at the top of `docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md`. This is a maintainer decision in three parts. Each part lists its options; the maintainer picks one and the record states the choice.

**A. Minimum win.**

| Option | Effect | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| A1. No minimum (today) | Any strict win decides. | One-token wins in one context keep deciding spellings. |
| A2. An absolute minimum of M tokens per probe; a smaller difference is a tie, resolved by the existing tie order (C# spelling, then probe baseline, then fewest UTF-8 bytes) | With M = 2 the one-token wins the 0.30 record calls weak become ties. | #1646 must re-rank every row; some C#-vs-non-C# rows flip to C#. |
| A3. A relative minimum (percent of the probe) | The bar scales with probe size. | The 0.30 record uses absolute deltas because fixed API framing dilutes percentages on small probes (`:7`). |

The plan recommends A2 with M = 2, plus one clause: where neither spelling is the C# spelling, the shipped 0.30 spelling stays unless the challenger wins by at least M. Without that clause `→` (22) against `->` (23, row 8) would become a tie, the rest of the tie order (probe baseline, then fewest UTF-8 bytes) could hand it to `->`, and every function header would be rewritten for no familiarity gain. For scale, at M = 2 the recorded counts make `yield v` against C# `yield return v` a tie (68 against 69, row 33), which the C# spelling would win. Listing every affected row is #1646's work.

**B. Named tokenizer set.** #1651 (0.33) extends the agent evaluation harness to Codex, Gemini and Copilot in addition to Claude Code.

| Option | Cost |
|---|---|
| B1. Anthropic `count_tokens` for one Claude model (today) | Spellings are tuned to one vendor. |
| B2. Three members: Anthropic `count_tokens` for the pinned Claude model (primary); OpenAI `o200k_base`, offline; Google's token counter for a Gemini model named in this record on the decision date. Copilot is counted with the tokenizer of a Copilot model named in this record on the decision date | Two more counters to build (#1799) and one more key-holding environment. |
| B3. Every model each agent can select | Unbounded. |

The plan recommends B2 with this combination rule: a candidate wins only if it clears M on the primary tokenizer and is not worse on any other member. Which encoding the Codex and Gemini models actually use is not verified in the repository; #1799 records it per tool. If #1832 (the 0.33 per-agent registration) later pins a different model, no window decision is reopened; the difference is a finding for #1846.

**C. Agent error counts as a ground for respelling toward C#.**

| Option | Cost |
|---|---|
| C1. Not allowed (today: #1645 confines a revisit to "the one-way and fewest-tokens rules") | A form agents keep writing the C# way cannot be respelled unless it also wins on tokens. |
| C2. Allowed when a stated threshold is met: the form's wrong-form error count in the #1605 report, with its denominator, and a maximum token cost per occurrence. The cap is a number of tokens per occurrence on the primary tokenizer, and the cost on every other member is recorded beside it | The language may get longer by a stated amount. |

If #1605 recorded "no paid run authorised", or its reading for a form is "not measurable", C2 cannot admit that form in this window and its row is "keep" or "deferred". The combination rule in B cannot admit a respelling toward C#, which costs tokens by design; the plan recommends that the record state that a C2 respelling is bounded by the cap in place of that rule.

The plan recommends C2. The threshold on #1605 wrong-form error counts is the one that #1770 (the 0.31 evaluation registration) registers in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` before the #1605 data exists. This record copies those numbers and cites the annex entry. If #1770 registered no such threshold, this record sets one and states in words that it was set after the #1605 stage 1 report was read.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The rule: "every 0.30 spelling choice goes to the variant with the fewest tokens, not the most readable one" (`docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:3`); ties go to the C# spelling, then the probe baseline, then fewest UTF-8 bytes (`:5`).
- One tokenizer: Claude Opus 5.5 through `count_tokens` (`:7`).
- The sample was "a hand-picked declaration-heavy convenience sample" and non-baseline payloads were "speculative hand-written text, not compiler-accepted syntax" (`:15`).
- Weak decisions by the record's own account: "`→` and `func` win by 1 token each, in one context only" (`:570`); the pattern spellings are "a provisional inventory gate, not a count result or human selection" (`:572`).
- Two 0.30 forms look like C# and mean something else: `var` is "mutability, not a type-inference keyword" (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:59`), and a header `=>` is a postcondition (`:403`).
- The offline tokenizer CI uses is o200k_base, described in the code as a proxy: "o200k counts are not Claude counts" (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:11-17`).
- The 0.30 release "publishes no new benchmark results" (`CHANGELOG.md:15`); #1605 is the first agent measurement on the 0.30 spellings.
- Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` (`:100`) is held append-only in CI by `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py`, run by the `annex-freeze` job (`.github/workflows/experiment-registry-tamper-check.yml:98`).

#### Why

Every spelling decision in the window cites this rule, so it has to exist before they are taken. As written, the 0.30 rule lets a one-token difference on one vendor's tokenizer override the spelling C# programmers and models already know, and it gives familiarity no way to win a revisit. Agent reach in 0.33 targets three other agents (Codex, Gemini and Copilot) whose tokenizers the rule never consulted.

#### Done when

- The record states, for each of A, B and C: the option chosen, the date, and "maintainer" as the decider, in the style of the 0.30 rule line.
- A: the minimum is a number with its unit, the tie order is stated, and the both-non-C# clause is adopted or declined in words.
- B: each member is named with its model or encoding and whether it is offline; the combination rule is stated.
- C: the threshold numbers and the token cap with the tokenizer it is counted on are present, with the Annex A entry the threshold was copied from or with the sentence that it was set after the #1605 stage 1 report was read; or C1 is recorded.
- `docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md` points to the amendment in its first ten lines.
- The pull request that closes this issue changes only those two documents. No spelling is decided in it.

#### Not in scope

Re-ranking the 0.30 spellings (#1646); building counters (#1799); any individual respelling (#1645).

Part of epic #1787. Depends on #1770 (the registration that holds the part C threshold). Blocks #1799, #1794, #1797, #1646.

### #1799 · [0.32] Tokenizer-set tooling: count the same payloads with each tokenizer named by the rule

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1785 · **Blocks:** #1646, #1784, #1797, #1796, #1650 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Deliver tooling that counts one payload manifest with each tokenizer in the set #1785 names, and says for each whether it runs offline or needs a key.

1. **One manifest for all.** Keep the manifest format `scripts/count_tokens.py` reads (schema version 1: samples, variants, optional probes) and its `inputs_sha256` digest unchanged, so the same reviewed bytes are counted by every tokenizer. The manifest's `target_model` field names the Anthropic model; each sibling counter takes its own model or encoding as an argument and writes it into its results.
2. **One counter per additional tokenizer.** Do not add other providers to `scripts/count_tokens.py`: its header states that only `api.anthropic.com` is used, and `scripts/test_count_tokens.py:44` pins the request URL. Add sibling counters that reuse its manifest validation.
   - o200k_base, offline: use the pinned `Microsoft.ML.Tokenizers` packages so no dependency is added. Where it lives (a small `tools/` console project or a mode of an existing tool) is the implementer's choice.
   - Each key-requiring member (expected: the Gemini model): a script plus a manual workflow modelled on `.github/workflows/syntax-token-count.yml`, with the same guards.
3. **Same output shape.** Each counter writes a results file in the shape `count_tokens.py --output` writes, and one command merges them into a table of payload by tokenizer.
4. **A tokenizer table**, in the tooling's documentation and copied into the window record (#1784): name, model or encoding, offline or key-required, the command or workflow that runs it, and the tool commit.

Counting is not a paid agent run: no completion or generation request is made. The 0.30 record states that Anthropic token counting is free of token charges (`docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:13`); charges for other providers' counting endpoints are not verified and are stated in the tokenizer table.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `scripts/count_tokens.py`: "Only api.anthropic.com is used" (`:5`); "No offline counts exist" (`:24`). It requires a population of one primer, 20 converted files and 20 agent files (`:264-267`) and accepts further payloads as optional `probes` (`:269`).
- `.github/workflows/syntax-token-count.yml`: manual dispatch only (`:21-22`), dispatch from main with the typed confirmation `count-tokens-only` (`:69-70`), pre-existing environment `anthropic-count-tokens` (`:121`), one fixed model (`:50`), "there are no offline or proxy counts" (`:8`).
- `scripts/test_count_tokens.py` holds the offline controls for that script and workflow and runs in CI (`.github/workflows/test.yml:109`).
- The only offline tokenizer is a test helper: `internal static class ProxyTokenizer` wraps `TiktokenTokenizer.CreateForModel("gpt-4o")` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:19-25`). It cannot be run on a manifest. The packages are pinned at `Directory.Packages.props:21-22` (version 2.0.1).
- Nothing under `scripts/`, `.github/`, `tools/` or `bench/` calls an OpenAI or Google token counter (grep for `api.openai.com`, `generativelanguage`, `countTokens`). `scripts/token_delta_spot.py` uses `tiktoken` with `cl100k_base` when installed, for a retired-syntax estimate; it does not read manifests.

#### Why

#1785 names a tokenizer set covering the agents #1651 adds to the evaluation harness, and nothing in the repository can count with any member except one Claude model. Without this tool #1646 and #1797 cannot apply the amended rule, and the per-agent token counts #1650 needs in 0.33 have no counter. The capacity is new, and it is the measurement half of #1646 moved here so that #1646 becomes application only: the additional work is the non-Anthropic counters and their dispatch workflows. Sharing one manifest also means the whole window needs one maintainer dispatch per tokenizer, where separate issues would each need their own.

#### Done when

- For every tokenizer in the #1785 set, one documented command takes a manifest and writes a results file; the merged table is produced by one further command.
- The offline counter, run twice on the same manifest, writes identical output and opens no network connection (test).
- Each key-requiring counter runs only from a manual workflow that requires a payload commit, the inputs digest, a typed confirmation and a pre-existing protected environment; an offline control test in the style of `scripts/test_count_tokens.py` checks those invariants and runs in the same CI step (`.github/workflows/test.yml:109`).
- A missing key, a failed request or a digest mismatch writes no results file (test per counter).
- `python3 scripts/test_count_tokens.py` exits 0 with its existing assertions unedited, so `scripts/count_tokens.py` still sends requests only to `api.anthropic.com`.
- If tests are added to a .NET test project, its `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated.
- The tokenizer table exists with one row per member.
- If a member cannot be counted (no counter exists for the pinned model), the table says so and #1785's set is amended in the record; the member is not silently skipped.

#### Not in scope

Preparing the window's payloads or dispatching the count (#1784); deciding any spelling.

Part of epic #1787. Depends on #1785. Blocks #1797, #1646, #1650.

### #1794 · [0.32] Decide the brace-header forms accepted "pending a separate decision"

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1785 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Decide, and record as a row of the window record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`), what happens to each brace-header form the 0.30 parser still accepts. A brace header is a `{...}` group written directly after a keyword, the way the retired syntax wrote `§F{id:Name:pub}`. This issue decides; it changes no parser code.

First complete the inventory in the baseline by compiling one probe per form (`calor --input probe.calr --output probe.g.cs`) and recording accepted or rejected. The starting list is the 31 call sites of `ParseAttributes` in `src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs`. The baseline below comes from reading the parser; nothing was run.

The forms fall into three classes:

| Class | Forms | Used today |
|---|---|---|
| A. Undocumented sections | `in{type:name}` and `out{type}` lines after a function, method, constructor or operator header; `where{T:constraint}`; a group after `invariant` | By nothing in the repository |
| B. Emitted by the converter | `proof{"description"} condition`; `assume{category} "text"` | Emitter and tests; `proof` is also documented |
| C. Inert groups | An empty group after `if` or `match`; a group after a block end | By nothing |

Options:

| Option | Cost |
|---|---|
| 1. Keep everything | Class A stays as a second way to declare parameters and a return type, against "one way to write each thing" (`CHANGELOG.md:9`). No rewrite. |
| 2. Reject classes A and C with an error that names the replacement; keep class B as the canonical spelling | Parser deletion and tests. No rewriter rule, because no file uses A or C. |
| 3. Reject every brace header and respell class B | Two documented forms change: a spelling chosen under #1785 with counts from #1799, a rewriter rule in #1806, changes to the emitter, `docs/syntax-reference/contracts.md` and 14 test files. |

The plan recommends option 2. Class A has no user, so rejecting it costs no rewrite and removes the duplicate declaration path. Class B is documented and emitted, and no measurement shows agents failing on it; respell it only if the #1605 report does.

If the decision rejects or respells any form, the parser change is implemented under #1793, the conditional issue that carries compiler changes for decided rows of the window record. Under the window's staging rule the old form produces at most a warning there, and becomes an error only in #1803. The standing acceptance rule applies to that change: the conversion scorecard's native count and the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens over original C# tokens) do not regress, and the number of files that newly fall to whole-file preservation (kept verbatim as C#) is reported.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The deferral is recorded in `docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:542-544`: "Not removed, because input still reaches it: the brace-header forms behind live keywords (`in{...}`, `out{...}` declaration sections and `ParseValue`). Rejecting them changes what the language accepts (#1600 territory), so that is a separate decision." Neither the `CHANGELOG.md` 0.30 section nor `docs/syntax-reference/` mentions it.
- The brace reader `ParseAttributes` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:4315-4341`) has 31 call sites (grep for `ParseAttributes(`, less the definition); its value reader is `ParseValue` (`:4472`).
- Class A: the function section loop accepts `in` and `out` lines (`Parser.cs:1355-1362`), read by `ParseParameter` (`:1413-1416`) and `ParseOutput` (`:1492-1495`) with no diagnostic for the form itself; the same loop exists for method signatures (`:6363`), method definitions (`:6975`), constructors (`:7933`) and operators (`:8020`). Braced `where` is at `:5837-5840`; `invariant` consumes a group at `:1039-1047`.
- Class B: `proof{"description"} condition` is documented (`docs/syntax-reference/contracts.md:262-264`), parsed at `Parser.cs:1831-1835` and emitted at `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:4045-4053`. `assume{category} "text"` is parsed at `Parser.cs:9126-9129`, emitted at `CalorEmitter.cs:3159-3164`, and used in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ExtendedFeaturesTests.cs:57` and `tests/TestData/QueryCorpus/project/contracts.calr:2`. It is not in `docs/syntax-reference/`.
- Class C: `if` and `match` read a group and report an error only when it has content (`Parser.cs:3989-3990`, `:3382-3383`, via `ValidateIdentityFreeHeader`, `:4343-4357`); block ends read a group at, for example, `:1396` and `:4151`.
- Use: no `.calr` file, no C# test literal and no page under `docs/` or `website/content/` has a line starting with `in{`, `out{`, `where{` or `invariant{` (grep for `^\s*(in|out|where|invariant)\{`). `assume{` or `proof{` appears in 14 C# test files and one `.calr` file.

#### Why

The edition freezes what the compiler accepts. A form that is accepted, undocumented and untested at the freeze either becomes part of the promise by accident or has to be removed later as a breaking change. The 0.30 cleanup left this decision open in writing, and the window is the last point at which rejecting a form is cheap.

#### Done when

- The window record has one row per class with the option chosen, the date, "changes `.calr` source" answered, and the probe result for every form in the inventory. Each of the 31 `ParseAttributes` call sites is assigned to a class or listed as reading no user-written group.
- If option 2 or 3: the row lists the forms #1793 must warn on and #1803 must reject, with the replacement each error message names. For option 3 it also names the rewriter rule in #1806 and the chosen replacement spellings with their token counts.
- If option 1: the record says which of the kept forms are documented in `docs/syntax-reference/` and which stay undocumented, with the reason.
- The pull request that closes this issue changes no file under `src/`.

Part of epic #1787. Depends on #1785.

### #1797 · [0.32] Union declaration spelling: token-measured decision before the freeze

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1785, #1799 · **Blocks:** #1643 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Choose the one Calor spelling for "one of a closed set of types" that #1643 (0.34) will implement, and record it as a row of the window record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`). The spelling adds a form: it rewrites no existing declaration. It changes existing `.calr` source only if it reserves a word (step 1). It is decided inside the window so that the edition is not followed at once by a new spelling debate.

1. **List the candidates.** At least: the C# 15 declaration carried over with Calor's existing conventions (lowercase keyword, indentation, `type:name`), and one or more shorter alternatives. State for each whether C# 15 unions and closed hierarchies share it, as #1643 requires ("map both ... to it, or say why they must stay distinct"). The repository does not spell the C# 15 declaration; take it from the C# 15 language reference and cite the page and date in the record. State for each candidate whether it needs a word that programs may use as an identifier today. Such a candidate is either recognised by position, or its word is reserved inside the window: the row is then marked "changes `.calr` source: yes", #1793 carries the reservation and #1803 switches it on.
2. **Write payloads** for each candidate and add them as probes to the window's single manifest: a declaration with three cases; the same declaration followed by a `match` that covers every case; and a closed hierarchy if it shares the spelling. This follows the three-probe pattern the 0.30 record used for `record` (row 22).
3. **Apply the amended rule** (#1785) to the counts from every tokenizer in the set (#1799). This is a maintainer decision with a default: the C# spelling is chosen unless an alternative clears the rule's minimum win.
4. **Record** the candidates, the counts per tokenizer, the margin, the clause of the rule that decided, and the date.

Do not write the candidates in `calor` code fences: they are not compiler-accepted syntax until #1643 lands. Use `text` fences, as the 0.30 record does.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- No parser path builds a union declaration: `UnionTypeDefinitionNode` is referenced in seven files under `src/Calor.Compiler/` and `Parsing/Parser.cs` is not one of them.
- The converter cannot write one: `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:2838-2839` throws "UnionTypeDefinitionNode has no 0.30 source form."
- The checker's union type is never constructed: `UnionType` is declared at `src/Calor.Compiler/TypeChecking/CalorType.cs:410` and `new UnionType(` appears nowhere in `src/`. The union arm of the exhaustiveness switch (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/PatternChecker.cs:261`) therefore cannot be reached from source, and 0.30 checks exhaustiveness for `bool` only (`:258-263`).
- #1643 asks for "exactly one Calor spelling ... chosen by fewest tokens (#1597's tool)". Its title and body still use the retired `§W`.
- The typed patterns a `match` arm would use (`case string text` and similar) are recorded as "a provisional inventory gate, not a count result or human selection" (`docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md:572`).

#### Why

#1643 must pick a new spelling, and it is scheduled after the edition is declared. A spelling that reserves no word does not break the edition, but one that does must be reserved inside the window, and choosing either in 0.34 would reopen the token debate on a language that has just been frozen and would need another maintainer token dispatch. Deciding it now uses the window's one manifest and one dispatch, and applies the amended rule while it is being applied to everything else. Exhaustive unions are parity with C# 15's compiler-checked `switch`, not an advantage over it.

#### Done when

- The window record row is filled as item 4 describes, with "changes `.calr` source" answered on that basis: "no" for a spelling recognised by position, "yes" with the reserved word named if one is reserved.
- The row states whether unions and closed hierarchies share the spelling.
- Every count in the row cites the window's payload commit and runs.
- The pull request that closes this issue changes only the record: no file under `src/` and no page under `docs/syntax-reference/`.

#### Not in scope

Implementing unions, their exhaustiveness check, or the `net10.0` lowering question (#1643). Confirming the typed-pattern spellings (#1646). Option and Result stay removed (#1617).

Part of epic #1787. Depends on #1785, #1799. Blocks #1643.

### #1796 · [0.32] Decide whether a redundant public is canonical

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1785, #1799 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Decide whether writing `public` where it is already the default is canonical Calor, and record the decision as a row of the window record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`). For example, `func Main public () → void` and `func Main () → void` declare the same function. This issue decides; it changes no compiler, lint or sample file.

| Option | Cost |
|---|---|
| 1. Canonical form omits the default `public`; the explicit form stays legal | A new `calor lint` finding with a safe fix, and a formatter rule, implemented under #1793 (the conditional issue that carries compiler and tool changes for decided rows of the window record). The rewrite of existing material (counts below) is a rule of the rewriter (#1806), applied by the single rewrite (#1803). |
| 2. Both are canonical (today) | Public material keeps two spellings; `samples/` disagrees with the generated reference. |
| 3. Canonical form writes `public` | Contradicts the 0.30 decision (row 4) and the converter's output; the generated reference, the primer and the emitter would all change. |

The choice is made under the amended fewest-tokens rule (#1785), on the counts from every tokenizer in its set; the row names the clause that decided.

The plan recommends option 1, for every declaration kind whose default visibility is public. Whatever is chosen, enforcement is a `calor lint` finding and a formatter rule only, never a compile error. The non-canonical form stays legal after the cut-over in #1803, and the row says so, so the decision cannot break a program under the edition. Add payloads with and without the keyword to the window's manifest: the 0.30 measurement compared `pub`, not `public`, against the default (31 against 27 tokens, row 4).

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `samples/`: 27 of 31 `func` headers write `public` (grep over the 11 `.calr` files); two write `private`, two write nothing.
- The generated reference and the primer never write it: zero matches for a `func` header with `public` in `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md` and `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md`. The reference says "public is the default" (`current-language.md:14`).
- Hand-written reference pages do write it: `docs/syntax-reference/types.md:55-57`, `:183`, `:244`; `structure-tags.md:56`; `effects.md:287`, `:329`; four more lines under `docs/semantics/`; 36 lines in 18 files under `website/content/`.
- Whole tree, by `grep -rIE 'func \S+ public\b'`: 402 `.calr` files contain 2,148 such headers, 390 of the files under `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/`; 129 C# files under `tests/`, `src/`, `tools/` and `bench/` embed 1,830 more lines in string literals. None of the 50 translated benchmark pair files under `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/` has one.
- The converter omits the default: `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:475` and ten further sites emit visibility only when it is not public.
- No lint flags it: the lint findings are Calor1300, 1301, 1305 and 1306 (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1196-1235`); Calor1307 to Calor1309 are unused. `calor lint --fix` exists (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/LintCommand.cs:27-28`).
- The source formatter keeps every token today: `FormatSource` "Formats source while preserving all non-trivia source text" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:45-48`). A formatter rule that deletes a keyword is a new kind of rule.

#### Why

Agents and people write Calor from the examples in front of them, and those examples are not uniform: the samples write a keyword that the generated reference, the primer and the converter never write. After the edition is declared, a canonical-form decision that rewrites samples, documentation and fixtures would need a second repository-wide rewrite, so the decision belongs in the window even though it is enforced only by lint.

#### Done when

- The window record row states the option chosen, the date, the declaration kinds covered, "changes `.calr` source: yes" (option 1 or 3) or "no" (option 2), and that the non-canonical form stays legal after the cut-over.
- For option 1 or 3: the row names the rewriter rule in #1806, lists the lint finding, its fix and the formatter rule as work for #1793, and states that the finding is a warning.
- The token counts with and without `public` are in the row and cite the window's runs.
- The pull request that closes this issue changes only the record.

#### Not in scope

Running the canonical-usage lint on converted output (#1747, 0.34). The admission rule for public Calor (#1839).

Part of epic #1787. Depends on #1785, #1799.

### #1800 · [0.32] Decide every rejecting guarantee inside the window: null remainder, enum-match exhaustiveness, and what counts as breaking

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1605, #1779, #1084 · **Blocks:** #1868, #1864, #1869, #1871 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Record one decision for each candidate guarantee that would reject a program the compiler accepts at the end of 0.32. This is a maintainer decision record, taken in one sitting with #1735 and any effect spelling #1731 needs. Its outcomes are rows of the window record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`).

**Definition to record.** Under the edition (the compatibility promise #1805 declares at the end of 0.32), a breaking change is any of: removing an accepted spelling or giving it a new meaning; changing what a program that compiles does at run time; reserving a word that could be used as an identifier; a new default-on hard error on a program that compiles under the edition. This is the wording #1805 points 1 to 3 publish; if the two differ, #1805 wins. A new warning is not breaking. A check that runs only when asked for is not breaking. #1805 may also adopt a soundness exemption, under which a fix that withdraws an unsound acceptance may reject programs within an edition; the candidates below are new guarantees, not such fixes, so their outcomes hold whether or not the exemption is adopted.

**Outcomes.** Each candidate gets exactly one.

| Outcome | Meaning | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Land in the window | A default-on error from the cut-over. The check is implemented in 0.32 and reports a warning at most until #1803, which turns it into the error with the rest of the batch. Its row in the window record is marked "changes `.calr` source: yes, hand-migrated" if any tree file is reported. The decision files the implementation issue in 0.32. | Implementation and corpus fallout inside 0.32. |
| Warn in this edition | May ship later as a warning, never as an error, for edition modules. | A weaker guarantee. |
| Opt-in | May ship later as an error only in modules that opt in through the per-module edition marker, using the opt-in mechanism #1084 records (its first pull request, the design record `docs/design/0.32-edition-marker.md`, merged; not its closure), with a `calor fix` rule; the default is unchanged. | Needs the marker; two behaviours to test. |

A candidate with no measured defect rate (its #1605 reading is "not measurable on this pair set", or #1605 recorded "no paid run authorised") cannot be given "land". It is held for the next measurement, and the record states which of the other two forms it may take if it is built. Held is not dropped.

**Candidates.**

1. The rejecting remainder of null enforcement, one decision per documented gap: later assignment and rebinding; member writes; constructor inputs; generic outer-container nullability and arbitrary generic payloads.
2. Exhaustiveness over enum matches.
3. Checks that are not default-build breaks. Record, with the code references below, that call-site refinement obligations and bug-pattern checkers do not run in a default build, so #1864 and #1870 need no window decision, and that making either default-on later would be breaking.
4. Whether refinement (obligation) verification may ever become a default-build check. A failed obligation is an error by policy, so turning the check on by default rejects programs that compile today.

   | Option | Cost |
   |---|---|
   | (a) Opt-in only for the whole edition | Obligation results stay invisible to default builds; #1868 chooses the opt-in surface. |
   | (b) Default-on allowed only in modules that opt in through the #1084 mechanism | Needs the marker; two behaviours to test. |
   | (c) Default-on allowed for every edition module | A breaking change under the definition above, so it needs a carve-out in the promise #1805 writes. |

   The plan recommends (a), with (b) as the only route to default-on. #1868 then decides the opt-in surface without reopening this.
5. Making the existing bool exhaustiveness check effective. In a default build the pattern checker has no type environment (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:913`), so Calor0500 is inert for variable targets, class members and expression matches; turning it on rejects programs that compile today. One outcome from the table.

**Inputs.**

- From the #1605 report, for candidates 1 and 2: the per-class count, the opportunity count and the reading (above threshold, below threshold, not measurable) against the thresholds registered before the run (#1770). Expect "not measurable": the #1605 pair files contain no `match`, no `enum` and no nullable type (see baseline).
- The modify-a-converted-module pairs (#1789) can exhibit these classes, but they first run in the #1645 re-run on the post-rewrite commit, after the cut-off. They are not an input here.
- A static opportunity count, taken before the cut-off from the migrated output of the corpus report job (#1779: MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation migrated by default `calor migrate`). For enum exhaustiveness: the number of `match` statements and expressions whose target is an enum type, and how many of them have no catch-all arm. For each null gap: the number of sites of that shape (assignments to an existing non-nullable reference binding, writes to non-nullable reference members, constructor arguments of reference type, generic instantiations with reference-type arguments). No corpus-job column reports these counts today, so the record carries the commands or script that produced them and the job run they read; a syntactic approximation is acceptable if labelled as one. If the job reports them as columns by then, the record cites the columns. If the corpus job is not available, the count is taken from a hand run of default `calor migrate` on the same three projects, and the record says so.

The static count measures exposure: how many sites a check would examine and could reject. It does not measure how often agents introduce the defect, so it cannot by itself justify "land".

**Recommendation.** The evidence available before the cut-off cannot show a defect rate for either class, so the expected outcomes are "warn" or "opt-in". The plan recommends opt-in for the null gaps, because the strategy already says "The null ban rides an edition" (`docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md:210`) and #875 says a sound analysis "will reject code that is fine today"; and warning for enum exhaustiveness, which is what C# gives a non-exhaustive `switch` expression (warning CS8509; C# compiler behaviour, not stated in the repository). Candidate 5 has the same evidence gap, and the plan recommends the same two forms for it. "Land in the window" needs evidence this plan does not expect to have by the cut-off: a class that reads "above threshold" in the #1605 report, and an implementation that meets the standing acceptance rule, shown on the warning form before the cut-off.

Anything given "land" carries the standing acceptance rule: the conversion scorecard's native count and the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens over original C# tokens) do not regress, and the number of files that newly fall to whole-file preservation (kept verbatim as C#) is reported.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Null is enforced at three boundaries since 0.22.0: initialization (Calor0272), native return (Calor0273), resolved method input (Calor0274) (`CHANGELOG.md:1871-1876`). The documented limits: "bounded receiving-boundary enforcement, not whole-program null safety. General assignment/rebinding, member writes, and constructor inputs remain outside its scope. Generic outer-container nullability and arbitrary generic payloads are not actively checked" (`CHANGELOG.md:1910-1913`). The same paragraph adds that newly allocated reference array slots can hold null (`:1913-1914`); decide whether that is a fifth row.
- Strictness has a recorded price: relaxing nullability for `nullable oblivious` modules gained 8 native conversions (`CHANGELOG.md:159-168`).
- Enum matches: the exhaustiveness switch handles `bool` and a union type and returns exhaustive for everything else, "Unknown types are considered exhaustive" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/PatternChecker.cs:258-263`). Calor0500 is reported as an error (`:236-240`) and the checker runs on every compile (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:911-915`), but it is built as `new PatternChecker(diagnostics)` with no type environment (`Program.cs:913`; the constructor then makes an empty one, `PatternChecker.cs:16-20`). A variable target therefore resolves to the error type (`:97-109`) and the check returns without a verdict (`:223`): through `Program.Compile` only a literal target reaches a verdict.
- Refinement obligations: generated only when `VerifyRefinements` is set (`Program.cs:1074`); it defaults to false (`:1546`); the only product code that sets it is the MCP refine tool (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/RefineTool.cs:104`, `:190`, `:255`, `:364`, `:434`). A failed obligation is an error by policy (`src/Calor.Compiler/Verification/Obligations/ObligationPolicy.cs:33`).
- Bug-pattern checkers: run only when `EnableVerificationAnalyses` is set (`Program.cs:1175`); it defaults to false (`:1487`) and is set by `--analyze` (`:152`, `:532`) and by the MCP compile and analyze tools. `calor check` sets only `VerifyContracts` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:181`).
- The benchmark pairs cannot exhibit either class: the 50 translated pair files under `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/` contain no `match`, no `enum` and no nullable type or `null` (grep).

#### Why

After the edition is declared, a new default-on error on a working program is a breaking change. Both candidate guarantees are scheduled in 0.35, after the freeze, so the form in which each may ever ship has to be fixed now. Building either without a measured count of the defect class in agent output would repeat the checker that was built and reverted in April 2026, whose post-mortem asks for a corpus base-rate check before building (`docs/design/tier1a-postmortem.md:256`).

#### Done when

- The record states the definition of breaking.
- Each null gap and enum exhaustiveness has one outcome, the #1605 reading with its counts, the static opportunity count with its method and source run, and the date; a held candidate names the form it may later take.
- Every "opt-in" outcome names the #1084 mechanism it relies on.
- Candidate 3 is recorded with the code references, and the sentence that turning either check on by default would be breaking.
- Candidate 4 has one option recorded with its date.
- Candidate 5 has one outcome from the table, with its date.
- For every "land" outcome an implementation issue exists in 0.32, carries the standing acceptance rule, and is a row of the window record.
- The record states the two non-goals: no null ban without an edition mechanism, and no return of Option and Result (#1617).

Part of epic #1787. Depends on #1605 (stage 1 report), #1779, and #1084 (its first pull request, the design record `docs/design/0.32-edition-marker.md`, merged; not its closure). Blocks #1864, #1869, #1871.

### #1743 · [0.32] Converter never emits an else whose body is only an if; record the decision

**Action:** move-in (from 0.31) · **Labels:** bug · **Displaces:** n/a · **May slip:** yes

**Current.** The body asks for an `else` whose body is only an `if` to be an error with a machine-applicable fix, so that an else-if chain has one spelling, and says no such diagnostic exists in 0.30. It is done when the diagnostic exists with a fix patch, a parser test, and a converter that never emits the nested form; or when the decision to keep both spellings is recorded in the #1597 decision record.

**Moved from** 0.31 because the 0.31 exit does not need it, and its outcome is recorded in the 0.32 window record (#1784) beside the other decisions about what the compiler accepts. It is not a breaking decision: the error shipped in 0.30, and relaxing it later would break no program.

**Amendment.** Change the title to `[0.32] Converter never emits an else whose body is only an if; record the decision` and add the label `bug`. Replace the second paragraph of the body (the one beginning "#1599 asked for") with:

````markdown
## Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

The error and its fix already exist. `src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:4127-4147` reports Calor0100, "An else body containing only an if is removed; use an 'else if' clause.", with a suggested fix that flattens the nested form, and `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/CanonicalControlFlowTests.cs:1705` (`ElseOnlyIf_FixPreservesCommentRawPayloadAndFollowingSibling`) pins the fix. The fix is not marked safe to apply (`SuggestedFix` defaults `isSafeToApply` to false, `src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1610`), so `calor fix --apply` skips it.

Three things are not established:

1. The converter can still write the rejected form. For a C# `else { if (...) ... }` whose block holds only the `if`, the else body is converted as a statement list holding one `if` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/RoslynSyntaxVisitor.cs:9894`) and `EmitIfChain` writes `else` followed by an indented `if` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:1725-1732`). A second path can give the same shape: when the nested condition hoists only pattern bindings, `SplitConditionHoists` leaves nothing before the `if`, so the else body is again one `if` (`RoslynSyntaxVisitor.cs:9874-9881`, `:9819-9834`). No test that converts either input was found. Both were found by reading; neither has been run.
2. Neither `CHANGELOG.md` nor the `if statement` entry of the teaching reference (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:229-233`, generated from `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`) mentions the error.
3. No decision is recorded. The 0.30 record measured `else if` against `elif` (row 34 of `docs/design/0.30-syntax-token-decision.md`), not the nested form.
````

Replace the "Done when" paragraph with:

````markdown
## Done when

- A conversion test (for example in `tests/Calor.Conversion.Tests/RoundTripTests.cs`) feeds C# with `else { if (a) { ... } }` and with `else { if (a) { ... } else { ... } }` and asserts that the emitted Calor compiles with no Calor0100 and that the compiled result returns the same value as the C# for an input reaching each branch. A third input reaches the hoisted path (a nested condition that declares a pattern variable, for example `else { if (o is string s) { ... } }`; confirm it reaches that path). The emitter writes an `else if` clause for an else body that is exactly one `if`.
- `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json` states the rule in the `if statement` entry and the generated reference is refreshed with `calor self-check docs --fix`; `CHANGELOG.md` has an entry.
- The 0.32 window record (`docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`) has this issue's row: the error is kept as shipped in 0.30, "changes `.calr` source: no", and whether the fix is marked safe to apply, with the reason.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has the new `expectedTotal` for each test project that gained a test.
- Standing acceptance rule: the pull request reports that the conversion scorecard's native count and the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens over original C# tokens) did not regress, and how many corpus files newly fall to whole-file preservation (kept verbatim as C#).

Accepting the nested form again would be a relaxation, which breaks no program and can be decided in any release. If the maintainer chooses that instead, the parser error at `Parser.cs:4127-4147` and its test are removed in one change and the record's row gives the reason.
````

**Why.** The body's premise is stale at HEAD bc80daeb: the error it asks for shipped with 0.30, so nothing here changes what the compiler accepts. What remains is a converter that can still write the form the parser rejects, the missing documentation, and a recorded decision. This issue is off the 0.32 exit chain and may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.


**Why.** The body describes `dbr` and `dbw` as codes listed in `KnownCompactCodes`; at HEAD they are internal registry values that source cannot reach, so the removal is cleanup and not a change to the language. The two `AttributeHelper` methods are residue of the same kind, kept alive only by their tests. This issue is off the 0.32 exit chain and may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.

### #1735 · [0.32] Inherited parameter refinements: same exception and contract-mode behavior as direct ones

**Action:** move-in (from 0.31) · **Labels:** bug, p1 · **Blocks:** #1805, #1866 · **Displaces:** n/a

**Current.** A refinement on a method's own parameter throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` in every contract mode, while the same refinement inherited from an interface throws `ContractViolationException` and is dropped under `--contract-mode off`. The body asks for inherited refinements to be emitted through the refinement guard path, with tests for both cases including `off`, and for the contracts documentation to mention inheritance.

**Moved from** 0.31 because it changes the run-time behaviour of programs that compile today, so it is decided and landed in the 0.32 window, before the edition is declared, and because the opt-in guard lift (#1866) depends on it.

**Amendment.** Change the title prefix to `[0.32]` and add the labels `bug` and `p1`. Add this section before "Done when":

````markdown
## Breaking-window status (HEAD bc80daeb)

This is a row of the 0.32 window record (`docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`): "changes `.calr` source: no; changes emitted behaviour: yes". After the change an inherited refinement throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` where it threw `ContractViolationException`, and is checked under `--contract-mode off` where it was dropped. A program that catches `ContractViolationException` from an inherited refinement, or relies on `off` removing the check, behaves differently. It lands in 0.32 so that the edition is declared over one behaviour for refinements, not two.

Code today: the inherited path is `CSharpEmitter.Visit(RequiresNode)` (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:5262-5302`), which emits nothing under `Off` (`:5265-5268`); the direct path is `:6496-6497`. `docs/syntax-reference/contracts.md:37-41` describes the direct case only. `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NestedContractInheritanceRuntimeTests.cs` asserts `ContractViolationException` at ten sites (first at `:201`); which of them exercise an inherited refinement is for the implementer to establish.

No round-trip job can show this change: the round-trip harness compiles with contracts off, and the converter writes no contracts, so no inherited refinement reaches those compiles. The behaviour is pinned instead by a canary test in `tests/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness.Tests` that #1795 adds: an interface refinement inherited by an implementer, compiled with the default contract mode, asserting the exception type the emitted check throws.
````

Append to "Done when":

````markdown
- The inherited-refinement canary in `tests/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness.Tests` asserts `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. If #1795 has not landed when this does, #1795 writes the canary with the new type. Both ship in the same release.
- `CHANGELOG.md` states the exception-type and contract-mode change under a breaking-change heading.
- The window record row carries the decision date and the pull request.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has the new `expectedTotal` for each test project whose test count changed.
````

**Why.** The change alters the exception type and the contract-mode behaviour of programs that compile today, so it lands before the edition is declared and the compatibility promise is written over one behaviour for refinements. A guard lift (#1866) turns a body guard into a parameter refinement, and any type that inherits that refinement would otherwise get the second behaviour; #1866 keeps virtual, abstract, override and interface-implementing members excluded whether or not this lands.

### #1795 · [0.32] Round-trip leg compiled with the default contract mode and with effects enforced

**Action:** new (from n/a) · **Labels:** tests, ci · **Blocks:** #1866 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

The round-trip harness converts C# to Calor, compiles the Calor back to C#, swaps the result into the project and runs the project's own tests. Add a leg in which that compile uses the compiler's default contract mode and enforces effects.

1. **Harness option.** Add an option to `tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness` `run` (the name is the implementer's choice) under which the per-file compile in `ConvertAndReplaceAsync` builds its `CompilationOptions` without overriding `EnforceEffects` or `ContractMode`. Without the option the harness behaves exactly as today. State in the pull request whether the option also reaches the other two permissive compile sites listed in the baseline.
2. **CI leg.** Run the in-repository subject `Synthetic` with that option in default conversion mode (`run Synthetic --mode default` plus the new option) as its own matrix entry or job in `.github/workflows/test.yml`, uploading its report under its own artifact name. It needs no submodules.
3. **Floors.** The leg blocks on its own floors in `tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/ProjectConfigs.cs`, set to what it measures on main when it lands, as the default-mode leg did (`:20-26`), and enforced by the harness exit code. Do not pass its report to `scripts/roundtrip_baselines.py check`, which accepts only the two existing modes and requires all five projects (see baseline), unless that script and `scripts/test_roundtrip_baselines.py` are extended in the same pull request. List in the pull request every file that passes the default leg and is rejected in this one, with its diagnostic codes.
4. **Canary.** SyntheticLib converts to Calor with no contracts, so on that subject the contract half of the leg has nothing to observe yet. Add three tests in `tests/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness.Tests` that compile fixed Calor text twice, once with exactly the options object the leg uses and once with the permissive options:
   - (a) a public function with the pure row `!` that calls `Console.WriteLine` fails with an effect error under the leg's options and compiles under the permissive ones;
   - (b) a function with a postcondition, written like `=> result >= 1`, produces C# that contains the postcondition check under the leg's options and not under the permissive ones;
   - (c) an interface method with a refined parameter, written like `func Use (int:x | (# > 0))`, and an implementing class with no refinement of its own produce C# that contains the inherited check under the leg's options. The test pins the exception type that check throws: `ContractViolationException` today, `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` once #1735 lands.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Three compile sites in the harness set `EnforceEffects = false` and `ContractMode = Compiler.ContractMode.Off`: `ConvertAndReplaceAsync` (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/RoundTripPipeline.cs:677-683`), `ConvertSourceToRoundTripCSharp` (`:1594-1601`) and the member oracle (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/Oracle/OracleRunner.cs:301-309`).
- The compiler defaults are `EnforceEffects = true` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:1385`) and `ContractMode.Debug` (`:1400`).
- What the permissive options hide: the effect pass does not run at all (`Program.cs:1007`), and under `Off` a postcondition and an inherited precondition emit no check (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:5306-5310`, `:5265-5268`) while a direct refinement emits in every mode (`:6496-6497`). They do not hide contract-inheritance errors: `ContractInheritanceChecker` runs on every compile (`Program.cs:979-985`).
- CI runs two legs, `lossy` and `default`, both with `run --all` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:949`, `:982-985`, `:1023`); `--mode` accepts only those two values (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/Program.cs:264-277`). Each candidate's options are recorded in the report (`RoundTripPipeline.cs:684`).
- Floors are enforced twice: by the harness from `ProjectConfigs.cs`, and by `scripts/roundtrip_baselines.py check` against `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:1045-1048`). That script knows the modes `lossy` and `default` and the five projects only (`scripts/roundtrip_baselines.py:19-20`).
- `tests/Calor.RoundTrip.Synthetic/SyntheticLib` has six `throw` statements (`Calculator.cs:23`, `:30`; `DateHelper.cs:19`, `:41`; `FizzBuzz.cs:19`; `StringUtils.cs:8`), pinned by exact-type assertions in `SyntheticLib.Tests`. `tests/Calor.RoundTrip.Synthetic2/GeoLib` has none. The three open-source corpus projects are git submodules under `bench/corpus/`.
- The converter writes no contracts: `preconditions: Array.Empty<RequiresNode>()` at 16 sites in `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/RoslynSyntaxVisitor.cs` (first at `:2887`).
- `tests/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness.Tests` has 472 tests by `eng/test-manifest.json`, which `scripts/check_trx.py` enforces in CI.
- Whether Synthetic passes with the default options is not verified: no .NET SDK was available when this was written.

#### Why

Every round-trip verdict in CI comes from a compile with contracts off and effects unenforced. A converter change that writes a wrong effect row, or a compiler change that alters how an inherited contract is emitted (#1735), cannot fail any round-trip job. The 0.32 window changes exactly those things, and a later guard lift (#1866) is to be accepted on unchanged round-trip floors and pass counts, which says nothing about contracts while no leg emits them. The capacity is new and small: one harness option, one CI entry on an in-repository subject, three tests.

#### Done when

- A CI job runs the Synthetic subject with the new option and uploads a report in which each candidate's recorded compiler options show effects enforced and contract mode `Debug`.
- The job fails when the leg's floors are not met; the floors and the measured values are stated in the pull request.
- The three canary tests pass; (a) fails if the leg's options object is changed back to `EnforceEffects = false`, and (b) fails if it is changed back to `ContractMode.Off`.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` records the new `expectedTotal` for `tests/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness.Tests` (475 if only the three canary tests are added).
- The reports of the existing `lossy` and `default` legs for Synthetic are unchanged by this pull request (same pass counts and fractions), and `python3 scripts/test_roundtrip_baselines.py` exits 0.
- `tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/Program.cs` usage text documents the option.

#### Not in scope

Running the strict option on the corpus projects; changing the options of the existing legs; changing how inherited refinements are emitted (#1735).

Part of epic #1787.

## Epic #1792 · [0.32] Epic: one rewrite, instruments re-registered, then the edition

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Land every source-changing decision of the 0.32 breaking window in the repository in one change made by a shipped rewriter, re-register the instruments on the result, and only then declare the language an edition, with a per-module marker and a written compatibility promise.

#### Why

The breaking window is the 0.32 milestone: the last release in which an existing spelling may change. An edition is a named set of accepted spellings and default-on errors that later compilers keep accepting.

0.30 changed the whole syntax with no rewriter (`CHANGELOG.md:13`, `:36`). A week after the cut-over, a full local run of the compiler tests on the milestone branch had 1,130 failures out of 22,251, and roughly 70% of all failures triaged that day were stale tests or fixtures that still asserted the retired syntax or behaviour (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:9`, `:13`, `:29`). The instruments (the soundness-sweep packet, the benchmark pairs and the end-to-end agent tasks) were left behind as well. At HEAD `bc80daeb` all 136 soundness-sweep templates (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/templates.json`), the 102 `.calr` files under `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs` and 63 live end-to-end task files (the count in #1736) are still in the retired syntax.

0.31 restores those instruments on the 0.30 spellings. The 0.32 window (epic #1787) may then respell forms they use. Without a fixed order the second rewrite would repeat the first: hash-pinned cases orphaned again, paid agent runs taken on spellings that are about to change, and an edition declared on forms no agent has run.

The strategy committed to "Syntax editions before any further breaking change" (`docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md:210`), and 0.30 went ahead without one. This epic supplies the mechanism (the marker and the rewriter), does the rewrite once, and puts the declaration last.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1084 | Per-module edition marker: design record and implementation (rewritten from the v0.14 self-migration residue) |
| #1793 | Conditional: compiler support for each form the window decided that has no implementing issue, with the old form still accepted |
| #1806 | `calor fix` rewriter for the breaking-window batch, with tests; the decision on a rewriter for pre-0.30 source; the standing rule for later breaking changes |
| #1803 | Apply the rewrite once across the inventory; the old forms become errors in the same change |
| #1807 | Re-register the instruments on the post-rewrite commit: soundness packet, benchmark pairs, arm canary, end-to-end suite |
| #1805 | Decision: declare the edition and publish the compatibility promise, or record the deferral |

#### Sequence

1. #1084, first pull request (the design record), before the cut-off date of the window record (#1784). #1800 reads it, the marker's spelling is a row of the record's batch table, and whether the marker is required decides whether the rewrite inserts it into every file.
2. When the record is decided (its cut-off date has passed and every row has an outcome): #1084, second pull request (implementation), and #1793. Both leave every old form accepted. #1793 is closed as not planned if no decided row needs it.
3. #1806. A rule can only be written for a decided row whose new form the compiler accepts.
4. #1803, once, after the legacy allowlist (#1763) exists. Until it merges, no window row turns an accepted form into an error.
5. #1807, directly after. The soundness packet pins a hash per case, and the green-run count for 0.35 entry restarts at any re-registration (#1783).
6. The paid runs (#1810 in epic #1812, and the #1645 re-run in epic #1649) pin the commit that #1807 posts.
7. #1805, after each of those runs has published or its decline is recorded (#1816), so that the edition is not declared on spellings no agent has run, and after the window record is closed with every "land" row inside the commit #1807 posted.

#### Done when

- The compiler parses and validates a per-module edition marker, and `docs/design/0.32-edition-marker.md` records the design, including what a module writes to opt in to a rejecting check (#1084).
- Every decided row of the window record that needs a compiler change and has no implementing issue links a merged pull request, or #1793 is closed as not planned with the record linked.
- `.calr` files outside the legacy allowlist were migrated by the shipped rewriter (#1806) in one merged change, and `docs/plans/0.32-rewrite-inventory.md` lists every class of embedded Calor text (test literals, sweep templates, documentation fences) with how it was migrated; or that file records that no rewrite was needed because no decision changed source (#1803).
- `docs/plans/0.32-instrument-re-registration.md` records the instruments validated again after the rewrite, with the soundness packet re-registered if any case changed, and the commit for the paid runs to pin is posted (#1807).
- `docs/plans/0.32-edition-declaration.md` records the edition declaration, or the decision to defer with its effect on 0.33 (#1805).

#### Not in this epic

- Which spellings flip and which guarantees reject: epic #1787, recorded in #1784.
- Compiler work for window rows that have their own issue: #1731, #1735 and any implementation child #1800 creates.
- Authorising and running the paid collections: #1816, #1810, #1645.
- Restoring the instruments on 0.30 syntax: #1766, #1781, #1778, #1736 (0.31).
- The legacy-dialect allowlist and its self-check: #1763.
- Re-affirming the edition after the per-agent results: #1846.
- A second edition. #1084 delivers the marker for the first edition only.
- Follow-up with no issue of its own: implementing the per-module opt-in.
