The #1084 design record states the mechanism (what a module writes); the first issue that ships an opt-in check implements it (the candidates are an implementation child of #1800, or the 0.35 issues that put a rejecting check behind an opt-in).

### #1084 · [0.32] Per-module edition marker

**Action:** rewrite (from none) · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Blocks:** #1800, #1806, #1697 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

*The old body tracks leftovers of the v0.14 self-migration plan. Its first item has shipped, and the other three concern a 1.x-to-2.0.0 semantics migration of `§SEMVER`-declared source that the 0.30 rewrite made moot.*

#### Change

Design and implement a per-module edition marker: a declaration by which a `.calr` module states which edition of the language it is written in. An edition is a named set of accepted spellings and default-on hard errors. This issue delivers the mechanism for the first edition only.

The work is two ordered pull requests. The first carries parts 1 and 2 and merges before the cut-off date of the 0.32 breaking-window decision record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`, the list of every breaking decision 0.32 may take): #1800 reads the design, and the marker's spelling is a row of that record. The second carries part 3 and merges once that row holds a decided spelling, before the rewriter (#1806) starts.

**1. Close the old scope.** Post one comment on this issue that records:

- Item 1 (refuse an older `semver` major) is done; see Baseline.
- Item 2 (a CHANGELOG discontinuity note about benchmark numbers measured under 2.0.0 semantics) is closed as obsolete. The note was never written, the numbers it would have qualified were measured on the retired syntax, and 0.30 "publishes no new benchmark results" (`CHANGELOG.md:15`).
- Item 3 (a demand-driven 1.x-to-2.0.0 migrator) is closed as obsolete. A `§SEMVER{...}` declaration is itself an error since 0.30, so a 1.x-declared file is rejected before any version check. Whether a rewriter for pre-0.30 source is ever built is decided in #1806.
- Item 4 (confirm no golden file depends on 1.x semantics) is closed with the check recorded: 0 of 1,823 tracked `.calr` files declare a version.

The Calor0701 message, `docs/semantics/versioning.md` and `website/content/semantics/versioning.mdx` send users to this issue as the migration tracker. Repoint them at section 8 ("Migration Guidance") of `docs/semantics/versioning.md`, and update the three test assertions that mention the link (Baseline).

**2. Write the design record** `docs/design/0.32-edition-marker.md`. Each question below is a maintainer decision. The record states the option taken, the date and the reason. The spelling is the one exception: this record proposes it and the window record decides it.

*Relation to the existing `semver` line.*

- (a) A separate marker; `semver` unchanged. Cost: two optional version lines to teach.
- (b) `semver` becomes the edition marker. Cost: `semver` requires an exact major and the documentation says there is no dual-semantics mode, which is the opposite of an edition. Every later semantics major would force an edition.
- (c) `semver` is retired inside the window and the marker replaces it. Cost: it removes an accepted spelling, so it needs its own row in the window record and a rewriter rule. No `.calr` file uses `semver`; six documentation pages and four test files do (Baseline).
- Recommended: (a). `semver` answers "which evaluation rules" and fails closed. An edition answers "which spellings and default-on errors" and exists so that two answers can coexist in one build.

*Optional or required.*

- Optional: a module with no marker is read as the first edition, permanently. Cost: the default can never move, so later editions reach new code only when `calor init`, the converter and the templates write the marker. Cost also: if any window row gives an existing spelling a new meaning, the rewriter (#1806) cannot tell a migrated module from a 0.30 module by its text. Decide this question once the window record lists its candidate rows; if one of them would give an existing spelling a new meaning, the design record states the answer for both outcomes of that row.
- Required: every module declares it. Cost: #1803 inserts it into every 0.30-syntax `.calr` file (970 today) and every embedded module (at least 351 C# test files); the converter must emit it or converted files stop compiling; one more line in each of the 87 teaching programs against 250 tokens of headroom in the full reference; a new way for an agent's first build to fail.
- Recommended: optional.

*Spelling and position.* The design record proposes candidate spellings and a position. The choice is made with the rest of the batch: it is recorded in the "Edition-marker spelling" row of the window record, under the fewest-tokens rule as #1785 amends it, with token counts from the window's single dispatch per tokenizer. Add the candidates to the window's payload manifest (the list of text files that `scripts/count_tokens.py` counts) so they are counted in that dispatch. Keywords are reserved globally in the lexer, so a new reserved word rejects every program that uses it as an identifier. Recommended: a word recognised only by position, as a module-body line, the way the parser already treats `global` and `namespace` there. If a reserved word is chosen, the row is marked as changing `.calr` source and gets a rewriter rule.

*Name of the first edition.* The record names it. #1805 declares under that name.

*What the marker gates, and how a module opts in.* The marker gates accepted spellings and default-on hard errors only. #1800's "opt-in" outcome, and later issues that put a rejecting check behind a per-module opt-in, need to know what a module writes to opt in.

- (i) Opting in means declaring a later edition. Cost: an opt-in check waits for a second edition, which needs a new marker value, rewriter rules and a compiler that accepts both editions.
- (ii) The marker line can carry named opt-ins inside one edition. Cost: one more form to teach, and each named opt-in is a second behaviour to test. The design record states the proposed spelling and adds it to the window's payload manifest as an additive form, so it is counted and decided with the marker spelling.
- Recommended: (ii), because the opt-in checks this plan anticipates come before any second edition is planned. #1800 and #1805 are written to hold under either option.

The record states which option was taken, that the mechanism is not implemented here, and that the first issue which ships an opt-in check implements it. No second edition is implemented here.

**3. Implement the record.**

- Parser (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs`, beside the `semver` handling at `:953-985`): accept the marker at most once per module. An unknown edition, a malformed value and a duplicate are errors, with new codes from Calor0706 to Calor0709. Calor0703 to Calor0705 are unassigned today but meant verification results before they were renumbered, so leave them unused. Report the errors from the parser so that compile, the language server, self-check and MCP agree, as `semver` does.
- `ModuleNode` carries the declared edition; `CalorEmitter` writes it back; `calor format` leaves it unchanged.
- Documentation: a page `docs/semantics/editions.md` and a matching page under `website/content/semantics/` (both authored by hand), and one row in the syntax-reference index (authored in `website/content/syntax-reference/index.mdx`; the copy in `docs/syntax-reference/` is regenerated with `calor self-check docs --fix`).

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Item 1 is done. The parser calls `SemanticsVersion.ReportDeclaredVersion` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:980`), which reports Calor0701 for any declared major other than 2 (`src/Calor.Compiler/SemanticsVersion.cs:51-52`, `:107-119`). It is called from the parser so that compile, the language server, self-check and MCP see one verdict (`:79-80`). `NativeSemanticsVersion_EnforcesCompatibility` covers it (`tests/Calor.Semantics.Tests/VersioningTests.cs:390`).
- `semver "MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH"` is an optional module-body line (`docs/syntax-reference/index.md:29`, `:80`; `docs/semantics/versioning.md:113`, `:120-122`). The parser reads it at `src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:953-985`, stores it on `ModuleNode.DeclaredSemanticsVersion` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Ast/ModuleNode.cs:85`), and the emitter writes it back (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:251-254`). "There is no dual-semantics mode" (`docs/semantics/versioning.md:321`).
- No `.calr` file declares a version: 0 of 1,823, measured by `git ls-files -z '*.calr' | xargs -0 grep -lE '^\s*semver\b'`. Six current documentation pages describe the line (`docs/semantics/versioning.md`, `docs/semantics/README.md`, `docs/syntax-reference/index.md`, `website/content/semantics/versioning.mdx`, `website/content/semantics/index.mdx`, `website/content/syntax-reference/index.mdx`), and four test files use it (`git grep -l semver -- 'tests/**/*.cs'`). The teaching reference (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`) does not mention it.
- 970 of the 1,823 `.calr` files contain no `§` and are taken as 0.30 syntax; the other 853 contain `§` (same listing, scanned for `§`).
- Nothing under `src/` implements or mentions an edition (`git grep -i edition -- src` prints nothing).
- Keywords are one global table (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:22-82`, lookup at `:944`), so `semver` is reserved everywhere and an identifier that collides is written `@semver` (`tests/Calor.Semantics.Tests/VersioningTests.cs:477-491`). A word outside the table can be recognised by position: the module body treats `global` and `namespace` that way (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:986-989`).
- Calor0700 to Calor0702 are the only codes assigned between 0700 and 0709 (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:616-636`). Calor0703 to Calor0705 carried verification results until they were renumbered to Calor0713 to Calor0715 (`:666-679`).
- The Calor0701 hint for an older major links to `issues/1084 (migration)` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SemanticsVersion.cs:70`). Two tests assert that the link is present (`tests/Calor.Semantics.Tests/VersioningTests.cs:114`, `tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/State/DocumentStateTests.cs:151`) and one asserts that it is absent for a newer major (`tests/Calor.Semantics.Tests/VersioningTests.cs:175`). `docs/semantics/versioning.md` cites this issue at `:204`, `:244`, `:400` and `:424`; `website/content/semantics/versioning.mdx` at `:181` and `:197`.
- The old body's second item was never done: `grep -i discontinuity CHANGELOG.md` prints nothing.
- The full teaching reference measured 16,750 tokens on 2026-11-20 against a ratchet of 17,000 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:55-62`), which leaves 250. It contains 87 complete programs (`grep -c '^module ' docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md`). The token cost of a marker line has not been counted.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` pins `expectedTotal` per test project and CI enforces it with `scripts/check_trx.py` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).

#### Why

After the 0.32 window closes, the compatibility promise (#1805) says a breaking change needs a new edition, and #1800 lets a rejecting guarantee wait behind a per-module opt-in. Both need a place in the source where a module says which rule set it was written for. Nothing in the compiler provides one: `semver` gates evaluation semantics by exact major and has never selected a syntax. The marker is ordered before the rewriter (#1806) because the optional-or-required decision determines whether the single rewrite touches every file. The Calor0701 message is repointed because this issue stops being the migration tracker it names.

#### Done when

- `docs/design/0.32-edition-marker.md` exists and states, for each of the five questions, the option taken (for the spelling: the candidates and the recommendation), the date and the reason. It merged before the cut-off date written in `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`, and the "Edition-marker spelling" row of that record holds the decided spelling with its token counts.
- The design record states the opt-in mechanism in enough detail for #1800 to cite: what a module writes, and which issue implements it.
- Tests in `tests/Calor.Semantics.Tests` pass for each of these: a module carrying the first-edition marker generates the same C# as the same module without it (if optional); an unknown edition is an error and no C# is generated; a malformed value is an error; a duplicate is an error; the marker survives a round trip through `CalorEmitter`; `calor format --check` exits 0 on a file that carries it.
- If the word is contextual: a test compiles a module that uses the same word as a parameter name.
- A test in `tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/State/DocumentStateTests.cs` reports the same error code for an unknown edition as the compiler does.
- `git grep -n 'issues/1084' -- src tests` and `git grep -n '1084' -- docs/semantics website/content/semantics` both print nothing, and the three assertions named in Baseline check the new pointer.
- `calor self-check docs` exits 0 and `TokenBudgetTests` pass with no budget constant raised.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has the new `expectedTotal` for each test project whose test count changed.
- If "required" or a reserved word was chosen, the change rejects programs that compile today. Then the rejection takes effect only in #1803, the row is marked as changing `.calr` source in the window record, and the standing acceptance rule applies to it: the conversion scorecard's fully converted count (the `conversion-scorecard` CI job) and the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens over original C# tokens, which #1746 reports per corpus project) do not regress, and the number of files that newly fall to whole-file preservation (kept verbatim as C#) is reported.
- The closing comment for old items 1 to 4 is posted.

#### Not in scope

- A second edition, and implementing the opt-in mechanism the design record states.
- The rewriter (#1806), and inserting the marker across the tree (#1803, only if "required" is chosen).
- The declaration and the promise (#1805).
- Changing `SemanticsVersion.Major`.

Part of epic #1792. Blocks #1800 (which reads the design record) and #1806.

### #1793 · [0.32] Compiler support for the forms the window decided (conditional)

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1784 · **Blocks:** #1806 · **Displaces:** conditional

#### Change

Make the compiler and its tools accept each new form that the 0.32 breaking window decided and that no other issue implements, while the old form keeps compiling. The breaking window is the 0.32 milestone, the last release in which an existing spelling may change. Its decisions are rows of the window record, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md` (#1784).

**Gate.** This issue is conditional.

- Source: the window record in its *decided* state, which it reaches when its cut-off date has passed and every row has a recorded outcome.
- Open: at least one row is decided "land", needs a change under `src/`, and has no implementing issue of its own. The candidates are the spelling flips from #1646, the respellings from #1645, and the outcomes of #1794 (brace-header forms) and #1796 (a redundant `public`). There is no numeric threshold; one such row opens the gate.
- Held: until the record is decided the gate is neither open nor shut, and no work starts here.
- Closed: the decided record has no such row. Close this issue as not planned with a link to the record. #1806 then proceeds without it.

Rows that have an implementing issue stay there: #1084 (edition marker), #1731 (effect contracts), #1735 (inherited refinements) and any implementation child that #1800 creates.

**Work, one pull request per row.** When the gate opens, post the list of rows on this issue. Then, for each row:

1. Parser (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs`): accept the new form. The old form compiles as before. It reports a warning only if the row lists one, and never an error; the switch to an error happens once, in #1803.
2. Where the new form cannot be accepted beside the old one (the new spelling already means something else, or it needs a word that programs may use as an identifier today), put the new parse behind an internal compiler option that defaults to off. #1803 turns it on. A new reserved word enters the keyword table only in #1803.
3. `CalorEmitter` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs`), through which `calor convert` and `calor migrate` write Calor: it can emit the new form under an internal option, with the default unchanged, so that converter output and its snapshots do not move before the rewrite.
4. `CalorFormatter` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs`): it formats a file in either form and never turns one form into the other. Migration is the rewriter's job (#1806).
5. Whatever else the row lists: a `calor lint` finding with its fix, a formatter rule, the text of a warning. A fix that edits an effect row or a contract is not marked `isSafeToApply` (#1801 pins this by test).
6. Tests in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests`. Register any new `.calr` fixture in `eng/tier2-fixture-expectations.json`.
7. Link the pull request from the row in the window record.

No pull request here changes an existing `.calr` file, test literal, documentation example or teaching text to the new form. The single rewrite (#1803) does that, and the teaching reference keeps one way to write each thing until then.

**Standing acceptance rule.** No pull request here adds a hard error, but each changes the parser or the emitter that conversion depends on. Each therefore reports, before and after:

- the conversion scorecard's fully converted count (the `conversion-scorecard` CI job), which does not fall;
- the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens divided by original C# tokens) per corpus project, which does not rise;
- the number of corpus files that newly fall to whole-file preservation (the converter keeping a C# file verbatim instead of writing native Calor), with their names.

The ratio and the preserved-file list come from the corpus report job (#1779, with the ratio columns #1746 adds). If that job or its columns are not available, use the hand method #1746 records (project-mode default `calor migrate` on MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation, counted with the o200k proxy tokenizer) and say so.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- No open issue implements a decided spelling. The body of #1646 says a flip is "a parse-error change plus a repo rewrite", and the body of #1645 says "If the form itself is the problem, revisit it"; neither names an implementation issue.
- Keywords are one reserved table (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:22-82`, lookup at `:944`), so a new keyword rejects every program that uses the word as an identifier. The parser can also recognise a word by position, as it does for `global` and `namespace` in a module body (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:986-989`).
- The converter writes Calor through `CalorEmitter` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:990`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/ProjectMigrator.cs:490`).
- `CalorFormatter.FormatSource` "Formats source while preserving all non-trivia source text" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:45-48`). The formatter write-path gate forbids rewriting identifiers by regular expression and requires byte-identical generated C# (`docs/release-policy.md:132-154`).
- The lint findings are Calor1300, Calor1301, Calor1305 and Calor1306 (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1196-1235`), and `calor lint --fix` exists (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/LintCommand.cs:27-29`).
- A fix marked `isSafeToApply` is applied by `DiagnosticFixes.Repair` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1701`), which `calor fix --apply`, `calor lint --fix` and the MCP check, compile, fix and migrate tools all call (`git grep -n 'DiagnosticFixes.Repair' -- src` lists eight call sites).
- The scorecard test asserts exactly 100 of 100 snippets fully converted (`tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/ConversionScorecardTests.cs:31`, `:66`) and runs in the `conversion-scorecard` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:672`).
- Every tracked `.calr` file under `samples/` and `tests/` must be registered exactly once in `eng/tier2-fixture-expectations.json`; `test_every_tracked_fixture_is_registered_exactly_once` (`scripts/test_tier2_verification.py:582`) runs on every pull request (`.github/workflows/test.yml:103`).
- `eng/test-manifest.json` pins `expectedTotal` per test project, enforced by `scripts/check_trx.py` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).

#### Why

The window's spelling decisions come from issues that record a row and do not implement it (#1646, #1645, #1794, #1796). The rewriter (#1806) can migrate a file only to a form the compiler already accepts, and the window's staging rule keeps every old form legal until the single rewrite. Without this issue, either the rewriter issue absorbs an unknown number of parser changes, or each flip lands with its own cut-over and the tree is migrated more than once.

The issue is conditional because nobody can know before the cut-off whether any such row will exist. It takes capacity only if the gate opens.

#### Done when

- Gate closed: this issue is closed as not planned with a link to the decided record, whose rows show that none meets the gate.
- Gate open: a comment on this issue lists every row that meets the gate, and each row of the record links its merged pull request.
- For each row, tests show all of the following.
  - The new form parses. For a pure respelling it generates the same C# as the old form of the same program.
  - The old form still compiles with no error, and with exactly the warning the row lists, if any.
  - `CalorEmitter` output with the internal option on compiles and generates the same C# as its default output for the same module.
  - `CalorFormatter.FormatSource` returns a canonical file in each form unchanged (with the internal option on where the new form needs it).
- Each pull request modifies no existing `.calr` file (`git diff --diff-filter=M --name-only <base> -- '*.calr'` prints nothing) and leaves `tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/baseline.json` untouched.
- Each pull request states the three standing-acceptance figures before and after, with the method used. The fully converted count is not lower, no project's ratio is higher, and the list of newly preserved files is present, even if empty.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has the new `expectedTotal` for each test project whose test count changed, `calor self-check docs` exits 0, and the required checks in `.github/workflows/test.yml` pass.

#### Not in scope

- Deciding any row (#1784 and the decision issues that feed it).
- Turning an old form into an error, changing what the emitter or formatter writes by default, or rewriting the tree (#1803).
- The rewriter's rules (#1806).
- Rows that have their own implementing issue.

Part of epic #1792. Depends on #1784 (its decided state, not its closure). Blocks #1806.

### #1806 · [0.32] calor fix: a rewriter for the breaking-window batch, with tests

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1784, #1084, #1793 · **Blocks:** #1803 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Add a batch-migration mode to `calor fix` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/FixCommand.cs`) that rewrites 0.30-syntax Calor to the forms decided in the 0.32 breaking window, the last release in which existing spellings may change. It has one rule for each row of the window's decision record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`) that is marked as changing `.calr` source. The option name is this issue's to choose. Work starts when the record is in its decided state: its cut-off date has passed and every row has an outcome. The record itself closes only after #1803.

**1. Staging.** A rule migrates a file to a form the compiler already accepts. Compiler support for each new form lands before this issue: with the row's own issue where one exists (#1084, #1731), and under #1793 for a row that has none (a #1646 flip, a #1645 respelling, an outcome of #1794 or #1796). If #1793 was closed as not planned because no such row exists, that dependency is met. Until #1803 the old form of every row stays accepted, with a warning at most, and a new form that cannot coexist with the old one sits behind the internal compiler option that #1793 adds; the batch mode sets that option when it checks its own output. Nothing in the tree is rewritten here. #1803 runs the tool and turns the old forms into errors. The rules must still migrate a 0.30-syntax file after that cut-over, because that is when users run them, so the mode keeps its own way to read each old form once the default parser rejects it.

**2. Rule contract.** Each rule is tested for all of the following.

- It is deterministic and idempotent.
- For a file that compiled before, the generated C# is byte-identical afterwards, unless the record marks the row as changing generated code.
- For a file that parses but does not compile for an unrelated reason, the rule still applies and the file's error diagnostics (code and message) are unchanged. The starter files of the W5A and W5C seeded-defect benchmark pairs are built to fail with Calor0410 (`bench/phase0-agent-native/check-probe-pair.sh:16-18`).
- Comments, blank lines, encoding, BOM and line endings are preserved, and no regular expression rewrites identifiers, as the formatter write-path gate requires (`docs/release-policy.md:132-154`).
- A row that cannot be migrated mechanically (a rejecting guarantee that #1800 lands in the window) gets a report-only rule: it lists each site and edits nothing. The record lists that row as hand-migrated.
- A row that gives an existing spelling a new meaning cannot be detected from the text. Its rule runs only when the file is stated to be 0.30 source (an explicit option, or the absence of the edition marker if #1084 made the marker required), and it refuses a file it cannot classify. The pull request lists such rows, and the idempotence test for them runs with that option.

**3. What a run does.**

- It applies only the batch's rules. It does not apply the existing safe patches (indentation, trailing whitespace, the Calor1305 lint), so the rewrite diff contains only decided changes.
- Rules are applied only by this mode. No rule's edit is marked `isSafeToApply`, so plain `calor fix --apply`, `calor lint --fix` and the MCP tools, which apply every safe patch, never apply one. A rule may edit an effect row (for example, if #1731 decides that existing rows must add a new effect code), and #1801 pins by test that such an edit is never marked safe. Whether an old-form error later carries a safe patch is #1790's decision.
- It runs to a fixed point, or fails naming the file.
- It skips every path on the legacy-dialect allowlist (#1763, the list of retained files that must stay byte-identical; read if it has merged, and required by #1803) and in an exclusion file given on the command line. It never writes a file that fails to lex or parse before the rewrite. It prints each skipped file with the reason.
- It writes through the path `calor fix` already uses (`SafeSourceFile.WriteParsedAsync`), which preserves encoding, BOM and permissions.
- `--dry-run` prints edit counts per rule and per file.
- A text mode reads one complete module on standard input and writes the rewritten module to standard output, so that #1803 can pass documentation fences and test literals through the same rules.
- If #1084 decided that the marker is required, one rule inserts it.

If the record has no source-changing row at its cut-off, the mode ships with an empty rule table and the contract tests run against a test-only rule.

**4. Decide whether the tool also reads pre-0.30 (`§`) source.** This is a maintainer decision, recorded in `docs/cli/fix.md` with the date, the reason and what would reopen it.

- (a) No. Retired source is rewritten by hand or regenerated with `calor convert`, as the 0.30 release notes say (`CHANGELOG.md:36-40`). Cost: none now. A 0.29 codebase outside this repository still has no path.
- (b) Yes. Cost: a lexer and parser for the retired syntax return to the shipped tool, and several translations are not mechanical (preconditions became parameter refinements, Option and Result were removed, declaration IDs moved to `.calor/ids.json`).
- Recommended: (a). Of the 853 retired-syntax files in this tree, 746 already have a 0.30 copy, and the other 107 are archived run output (54), the 52 `W-00*` pair files whose fate #1648 decides, and one harness canary for which #1766 adds a 0.30 counterpart (Baseline). A report of 0.29 source outside this repository is what would reopen the decision.

**5. Set the standing rule.** Add a "Breaking changes" section to `docs/release-policy.md`: a change that removes or changes an accepted spelling, changes what a compiling program does, reserves a word, or adds a default-on hard error adds a rule with fixtures to this mode and runs it over the repository in the same change. Once an edition is declared (#1805) such a change is also a new edition. A soundness fix admitted under the promise's exemption, if one is adopted, follows the terms written there and adds at least a report-only rule.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `calor fix` requires `--apply` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/FixCommand.cs:51-58`), has `--dry-run` and `--format json` (`:29-36`), accepts only `.calr` files or directories (`:99-102`), and walks every `.calr` under a directory except `bin` and `obj`, with no exclusion list (`:148-153`). It writes with `SafeSourceFile.WriteParsedAsync` (`:114`).
- It applies only patches marked safe, through `DiagnosticFixes.Repair` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1701`). `Repair` stops after three passes (`:1709`) and refuses a patch after which a file that compiled generates different C# (`:1733`). `calor lint --fix` and the MCP check, compile, fix and migrate tools call the same method (`git grep -n 'DiagnosticFixes.Repair' -- src`).
- Safe patches are produced at five sites under `src/Calor.Compiler/`: `Parsing/Parser.cs:621`, `Parsing/Lexer.cs:398` and `:407`, `Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:441` and `:481`. They cover a legacy closer, indentation, trailing whitespace and the Calor1305 lint. None implements a spelling decision.
- The bulk migrations `calor fix` once had (`--elide-call-closers`, `--heal-closers`, `--drop-structural-ids`, `--compact-ids`, with `--revert --log`) were removed in 0.30 (`docs/cli/fix.md:42-45`).
- 0.30 shipped no rewriter (`CHANGELOG.md:13`, `:36`).
- The lexer rejects every `§` marker with Calor0006 (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:741-764`), so the tool cannot read the 853 retired-syntax `.calr` files. 746 of them are registered in `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/inputs.json` (`originalPaths`) as having a 0.30 copy. The other 107 are 54 files under `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/w-rows-dry-001` and `w-rows-dry-002`, 52 under `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/W-00*`, and `bench/phase0-agent-native/templates/calor-arm/permissive-canary.calr` (`git ls-files '*.calr'`, scanned for `§` and compared with `originalPaths`).
- `docs/release-policy.md` has no section on breaking changes (its two top-level sections start at `:9` and `:132`).
- Every tracked `.calr` under `samples/` and `tests/` needs exactly one entry in `eng/tier2-fixture-expectations.json` (`scripts/fixture_compile_check.py:13-19`); `test_every_tracked_fixture_is_registered_exactly_once` (`scripts/test_tier2_verification.py:582`) enforces it on every pull request (`.github/workflows/test.yml:103`). Rule fixtures stored as `.calr` need an entry, and the "before" files become rejects after #1803.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` pins `expectedTotal` per test project, enforced by `scripts/check_trx.py` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).
- `tools/Calor.IndentMigrator` is an older bulk migrator that re-emits whole files through `CalorEmitter` (`tools/Calor.IndentMigrator/Program.cs:147`). It is not in `Calor.sln` or any workflow. Whether it meets the write-path gate is not verified.

#### Why

The window's decisions change spellings that the tree uses in thousands of places. In 0.30 that cost was paid by hand, after the fact. With a rewriter, each decision carries its own migration, and the tool that migrates this repository is the tool a user runs on theirs.

This is new capacity and displaces no existing issue. The work was implicit in the bodies of #1646 ("a parse-error change plus a repo rewrite") and #1645 ("revisit it"), which record decisions and carry no migration of their own.

This issue adds no hard error: every old form stays accepted until #1803.

#### Done when

- The mode exists. On a fixture directory it rewrites the files, a second run reports zero edits, and `--dry-run` writes nothing. Tests cover all three.
- Each rule has before and after fixtures and tests for every point of the rule contract.
- Each rule names the #1784 row it implements. The pull request lists every source-changing row of the record against its rule, or against "hand-migrated", and marks each row that gives an existing spelling a new meaning. A test fails if a rule has no before and after fixtures.
- For each row that gives an existing spelling a new meaning, tests show that its rule edits nothing without the 0.30-source statement, refuses a file it cannot classify, and is idempotent when run with that statement.
- For each rule, a test runs it with the old form rejected by the default parser (the state after #1803) and the "before" fixture is still migrated.
- A test shows that excluded paths are byte-identical after a run.
- A test shows that plain `calor fix --apply` applies none of the batch rules and that no rule's edit is marked `isSafeToApply`.
- A dry run over this repository's 0.30-syntax `.calr` files is attached to the pull request: edit counts per rule, and every skipped file with its reason.
- The pull request changes no spelling outside the tool, its tests and its documentation, and the required checks in `.github/workflows/test.yml` pass.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has the new `expectedTotal` for each test project whose test count changed, and every `.calr` fixture added under `tests/` is registered in `eng/tier2-fixture-expectations.json`.
- `docs/cli/fix.md` documents the mode and records decision 4 with its date, reason and reopening condition. `docs/release-policy.md` has the "Breaking changes" section with the standing rule of part 5, including its edition and soundness-fix sentences. `calor self-check docs` exits 0.
- If the record has no source-changing row: the mode ships with an empty rule table, the contract tests pass against the test-only rule, and the pull request says so.

#### Not in scope

- Compiler support for the new forms (the row's own issue, or #1793).
- Running the tool over the repository and switching the old forms to errors (#1803).
- Deciding any row (#1784).
- Calor0006 guidance (#1788) and fix coverage for agent-hit diagnostics (#1790).

Part of epic #1792. Depends on #1784 (its decided state, not its closure), #1084 and #1793 (merged, or closed as not planned). Blocks #1803.

### #1803 · [0.32] Apply the rewrite once across the inventory

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1806, #1763 · **Blocks:** #1807 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

One pull request moves the repository from the 0.30 spellings to the batch decided in the 0.32 breaking-window record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`), using the batch mode of `calor fix` from #1806. After it merges, the old forms are errors. Step 1 may merge first as its own pull request; steps 2 to 8 are one pull request.

1. **Protect the evidence first.** The legacy-dialect allowlist (#1763) lists the retained files that must stay byte-identical, each entry with a `dialect` (`pre-0.30` or `0.30`). Its entries at filing are in the retired `§` syntax. After this change the 0.30 spellings are a second retired dialect. Add an entry with `dialect: 0.30` for every 0.30-syntax file that is kept as evidence: at minimum any outputs of the #1605 runs archived in the tree. The allowlist's self-check accepts a marker-free file listed with that dialect. #1763 may merge after the 0.31 release but must have merged before this step; it may list the 52 `W-00x` pair files as pending #1648, so an open #1648 does not hold the rewrite.
2. **Run the rewriter** over every `.calr` file outside the allowlist.
3. **Migrate embedded Calor text.** Complete modules go through the rewriter's text mode. Fragments and templates with holes are edited by hand.
4. **Cut over in the same change.** Each old form becomes an error that names its replacement, and the rewriter still migrates it. The exception is a row whose decision keeps the old form legal (the record says so in its decision cell, as for a redundant `public` under #1796). Turn on every internal option that #1793 put a new form behind, and remove the option. `CalorEmitter` and `CalorFormatter` emit only the new forms. Generated mirrors are regenerated with `calor self-check docs --fix`, not edited. Snapshots, goldens and registered error signatures are updated. Extend `SourceLiteralSyntaxGuardTests` to the old forms that can be recognised lexically, so that compiler messages and hints cannot keep teaching them. The same change applies every #1731 contract change recorded as outcome (b), and turns any check #1800 recorded as "land" from a warning into the error. Each cut-over error carries a fix patch or not, as the rule #1790 records in `docs/cli/fix.md` says; if that rule is not recorded yet, the errors carry no safe patch.
5. **Insert the marker** here if #1084 chose "required".
6. **Carry the sweep packet's pinned files.** The soundness sweep is the daily job that compares the verifier's "proven" claims with an independent oracle, over a packet of generated cases that pins a SHA-256 per case (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep`). If any rule changes the text of a case, this pull request includes the edited templates, the regenerated case index (a compiler test fails on any difference) and any harness literal that matches the old form. Apply the respelling to the templates by the procedure in the "Successor soundness packet: design" section of `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` (#1769), and add whatever amendment entry the packet's registration requires for a template change, marked as awaiting classification by #1807. If that section states no procedure for a template change, get one recorded there before editing a template; the 1.0.0 registration's own rule is a versioned amendment (Baseline). Do not edit `expected-verdicts.json`. The full sweep that CI runs on a pull request touching the packet must pass against the existing expected verdicts. A changed verdict blocks the merge: a respelling must not change what the verifier concludes.
7. **Write the inventory** `docs/plans/0.32-rewrite-inventory.md`: one row per item with the path or glob, the file count, how it was migrated (rewriter, text mode, by hand, regenerated, or untouched on the allowlist) and the check that proves it. Embedded Calor text (test literals, sweep templates, documentation fences) has its own rows. A row with no automatic check, such as documentation fragments, says who reviewed it.
8. **Record the standing acceptance rule.** The cut-over adds hard errors, so the window record holds one measurement on the parent commit and one on this pull request's head, as part 5 of that record defines: the conversion scorecard's fully converted count (the `conversion-scorecard` CI job) does not fall; the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens over original C# tokens, per corpus project, from the corpus report job #1779 with the columns #1746 adds, or the hand method #1746 records) does not rise; and the corpus files that newly fall to whole-file preservation (kept verbatim as C#) are counted and named. The converter emits Calor through `CalorEmitter`, so a form it still writes in the old spelling shows up here as preserved files.

If the record has no source-changing row, no rewrite happens. This issue then closes with an inventory file that says no rewrite was needed and names the decided record and the commit at which that was established.

Starting inventory, with counts at HEAD `bc80daeb`. Recount when the work starts.

| Item | Where | Count | Expected method |
|---|---|---|---|
| ActiveSyntax mirror | `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/**/*.calr` | 767 | rewriter |
| Other mirror inputs | the `.txt` (4), `.json` (16) and `.py` (3) files in the mirror that `inputs.json` registers as text, structured, transcript, identity-sidecar and executable entries | 23 | text mode or by hand, where they embed Calor text |
| Pair fixtures | `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs` (inside the 767), plus the #1789 pairs and the 0.30 arm canary from #1766 once they exist | 50 | rewriter; the W5A and W5C seeded-defect starters must still fail with Calor0410 |
| Other 0.30 `.calr` | `samples` (11), `tests/E2E` (58), `tests/TestData/LintScenarios` (58), `bench/mcp/tasks` (19), `tests/Calor.Enforcement.Tests` (13), `tests/TestData/Verification` (11), `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/SelfTest` (10), `tests/TestData/RenameScripts` (9), `bench/phase0-agent-native/fixtures` (6), `tests/TestData/QueryCorpus` (6), `tests/SdkConsumer` (1), `tools/calor-allowlist-audit` (1) | 203 | rewriter |
| Soundness packet | `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/templates.json`; module header in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/SweepCaseGenerator.cs:152`; probe in `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Program.cs:301`; literal matches in `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/CaseExecutor.cs:172` and `Program.cs:418`. All are retired syntax at HEAD; #1781 ports them first, so locate them again | 136 templates | by hand, or generated cases through text mode; see step 6 |
| C# test literals | `tests/**/*.cs` | 351 files, 5,439 lines that begin with `func` | text mode for complete modules, by hand otherwise |
| End-to-end tasks | `tests/E2E/agent-tasks` (prompts, fixture `CLAUDE.md` files, acceptance scripts); `tests/E2E/scenarios` (11 scenario directories, 10 with an `output.g.cs` golden) | 58 `.calr` (in the row above) | rewriter for `.calr`, by hand for prompts and scripts, goldens regenerated |
| Teaching source | `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json` (83 constructs, 87 programs, the primer) | 1 | edit the source, regenerate the mirrors |
| Syntax reference | `website/content/syntax-reference/*.mdx` (authored); `docs/syntax-reference/*.md` (generated) | 13 pages each | text mode, then regenerate |
| Other documentation | `calor` fences in 34 `.md` files (415 fences) and 49 `.mdx` files (313 fences), including `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`. The counts include generated pages and dated records | | text mode for complete programs, by hand for fragments; dated records under `docs/plans`, `docs/design`, `docs/experiments`, `docs/process` and earlier CHANGELOG entries stay as written |
| Product strings | `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/error-suggestions.json`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/calor-syntax-documentation.json`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md`, `src/Calor.LanguageServer/Handlers/CompletionHandler.cs`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools` | | by hand, or regenerated where the file is a generated mirror |
| Registries | `eng/tier2-fixture-expectations.json`, `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/inputs.json`, `tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/baseline.json`, `eng/test-manifest.json` | | updated only where an outcome or a test count legitimately changes |
| Frozen evidence | the 853 retired-syntax `.calr` files, and the files added in step 1 | 853 or more | untouched |

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- 1,823 `.calr` files are tracked; 853 contain `§` and 970 do not (`git ls-files '*.calr'`, scanned for `§`).
- The mirror is registered in `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/inputs.json` (767 `originalPaths`) and parsed by `ActiveInput_ParsesWithTheCurrentCompiler` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ActiveSyntaxInputTests.cs:133`).
- Every tracked `.calr` under `samples/` and `tests/` has a compile expectation in `eng/tier2-fixture-expectations.json` (1,268 files: 1,125 `compile`, 99 `reject`, 44 `known-failure`, by a count over `groups[].files`). Reject entries pin the error code and message. On a pull request to `main`, registration is checked for both roots (`scripts/test_tier2_verification.py:582`, run at `.github/workflows/test.yml:103`), but outcomes are checked for `samples/` only, by the `verify-phase1` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:1560`, `scripts/verify_phase1.py:11`). Outcomes under `tests/` are checked by the Tier 2 verification workflow, which runs on manual dispatch or on pull requests to `phase-*` and `release/**` branches (`.github/workflows/tier2.yml:10-18`) and stays red while `known-failure` entries exist (`:8`).
- 351 C# files under `tests/` contain a line that begins with `func`, 5,439 such lines in all (`git grep -E '^\s*func [A-Za-z_@]' -- 'tests/**/*.cs'`). Single-line literals are not counted, so this is a lower bound.
- `CaseIndexMatchesTheGenerator` fails when `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/cases-index.json` differs from the generator's output (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:33-62`). A pull request that changes the packet, the generator or the harness also runs the full sweep on `macos-15` (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:71-91`). The expected verdicts are keyed by case id and hold no hash (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/expected-verdicts.json`), so a respelled case can pass against them unchanged. The 1.0.0 registration allows a template change only by a versioned amendment (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/registration.json:3059`). All 136 templates are retired syntax today; #1781 ports them and #1778 registers the successor packet.
- `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md` is generated from `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json` (`:9` of that page). The syntax-reference pages are authored on the website and mirrored into `docs/` (`docs/cli/self-check.md:13-16`).
- `calor self-check docs` runs in the `test` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:267`). In covered documentation it parses every `calor` fence whose first content line starts with `module`; fences that are fragments are skipped (`docs/cli/self-check.md:88-95`).
- `SourceLiteralSyntaxGuardTests` scans string literals under `src/` for retired `§` markers only (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SourceLiteralSyntaxGuardTests.cs:42`).
- The conversion scorecard runs in the `conversion-scorecard` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:672`). Its test asserts exactly 100 of 100 snippets fully converted and 100 passing round trip (`tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/ConversionScorecardTests.cs:31-32`, `:66`, `:86`). The committed `tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/baseline.json` still records 96 fully converted, with a timestamp of 2026-10-30.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` pins `expectedTotal` per test project, enforced by `scripts/check_trx.py` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).
- A push to `main` that touches `tests/E2E/agent-tasks/**` or `tests/Calor.Evaluation/**` starts the Benchmarks workflow (`.github/workflows/benchmark.yml:36-41`). Its paid steps run only on a manual dispatch (`:121`, `:389`), so merging the rewrite spends nothing.
- A week after the 0.30 cut-over, a full local run had 1,130 failing compiler tests of 22,251, and roughly 70% of the failures triaged were stale tests or fixtures (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:9`, `:29`).

#### Why

A single change gives one commit that every later step can name: the instruments are re-registered on it, the paid runs pin it, and the edition is declared at it. Spreading the rewrite over several changes would leave `main` accepting a mixture, and each instrument would need registering more than once.

The cut-over belongs in the same change because a green CI run proves the migration only when the old forms are no longer accepted.

This is new capacity and displaces no existing issue. It is the repository rewrite that the old body of #1646 assumed would follow each flip.

#### Done when

If a rewrite was needed:

- One merged pull request contains the rewriter's edits, the hand edits, the cut-over and the regenerated mirrors, and every required check passed on its head commit.
- On the merged commit, the rewriter's dry run over the repository reports zero edits.
- For each source-changing row, a test shows that the old form is an error that names the replacement, and a test shows that the rewriter still migrates the rule's "before" fixture. Where the record gives an old spelling a new meaning, a test pins the new meaning instead. Where the row keeps the old form legal, a test shows that it still compiles.
- For each #1731 (b) group and each landed guarantee, a test shows the program that compiled before is now rejected.
- `git diff --name-only <parent> <merge commit> -- <allowlisted paths>` prints nothing, and the allowlist self-check from #1763 passes with the 0.30-dialect entries added in step 1.
- `expected-verdicts.json` is unchanged by the pull request. If the pull request touched a sweep input, its sweep run passed; if it did not, `git diff --name-only <parent> <merge commit> -- tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep` prints nothing.
- `docs/plans/0.32-rewrite-inventory.md` exists with the columns of step 7, one row per item, rows for test literals, sweep templates and documentation fences, and the commit hash.
- `calor self-check docs` exits 0, the `verify-phase1` job passes, and `ActiveInput_ParsesWithTheCurrentCompiler` passes.
- A manual dispatch of the Tier 2 verification workflow (`.github/workflows/tier2.yml`) on the pull request's branch reports no fixture in a failing status other than `known-failure` entries that were already registered on the parent commit. The run is linked in the inventory.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has the new `expectedTotal` for each test project whose test count changed.
- The window record holds the before and after values for the standing acceptance rule with both commit hashes and the method used. The fully converted count is not lower and no project's density ratio is higher; the list of newly preserved files is present, even if empty.
- The merged commit hash is posted on #1807.

If no rewrite was needed:

- `docs/plans/0.32-rewrite-inventory.md` exists, says that no row of the decided record changes `.calr` source, links the record and names the commit. The same statement is posted on #1807 with that commit.

#### Not in scope

- Re-freezing expected verdicts, re-validating pairs, the arm canary and the end-to-end dry run (#1807).
- Translating retired `§` files (0.31: #1766, #1781, #1736).
- Any decision about which forms change (#1784), and compiler support for the new forms before the cut-over (#1793 and the rows' own issues).

Part of epic #1792. Depends on #1806 and #1763 (the allowlist must exist before anything is rewritten). Blocks #1807.

### #1807 · [0.32] Re-register the instruments after the rewrite

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Depends on:** #1803, #1778, #1766, #1736 · **Blocks:** #1645, #1805, #1810, #1662 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

After #1803 has merged (the single repository rewrite of the 0.32 breaking window, or its record that no rewrite was needed), bring every instrument restored in 0.31 back to a registered, validated state in one pull request, and post the commit that the 0.32 paid runs pin. Nothing here invokes an agent or spends money.

**1. Soundness packet.** The soundness sweep is the daily job that compares the verifier's "proven" claims with an independent oracle, over a packet of generated cases that pins a SHA-256 per case. Compare the case index before and after the rewrite: `git diff <parent of the #1803 merge> <#1803 merge> -- tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/cases-index.json`.

- If no case hash changed, record that with the command used. The registered packet stands and the green-run count continues.
- If any hash changed, re-register the packet by the procedure #1778 used.
  - Get a run of the changed cases on `main`: dispatch the Soundness sweep workflow, or take the first scheduled run after the #1803 merge. Both sweep `main` HEAD on `macos-15`.
  - Download the run's artifact `soundness-sweep-<run id>-<attempt>` and run `python3 scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py freeze --out <artifact directory> --commit <the commit that run swept> --runner "macos-15, GitHub Actions run <URL>"`, so that the fixture's `recordedFrom` names a commit at or after the rewrite. The command refuses a run with any failing check.
  - In the pull request, list every case whose hash changed and classify it with #1778's terms: *unchanged* (the hash changed, the verdict did not), *port-only* (the verdict moved and a named respelling rule of the window record accounts for it) or *real* (any other verdict change). #1803 merged only with every verdict unchanged, so a port-only or real entry means something moved since. A real entry is treated as a compiler finding: it needs a linked issue and the maintainer's sign-off before the freeze merges.
  - Amend the packet's registration, completing the entry #1803 opened if there is one, as the "Successor soundness packet: design" section of `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` (#1769) prescribes.
- When the packet is re-registered, the count of consecutive daily green runs required for 0.35 entry (#1783) restarts at that merge. State the date of the first scheduled run that counts.

**2. Benchmark pairs.** A pair is one task in a C# version and a Calor version (the two arms), graded by a shared held-out test suite. A seeded-defect pair ships a starter with a planted defect. No agent is invoked.

- `bench/phase0-agent-native/check-probe-pair.sh` exits 0 on the nine seeded-defect pairs (W5A-001 to 003, W5B-001 to 003, W5C-001 to 003), in the 0.30-syntax copies that #1766 wired into the harness, as rewritten by #1803.
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh --null-agent` passes for both arms on each pair registered for #1605 (#1770), on the nine pairs above, and on the #1789 pairs if they have merged. `--null-agent` applies the reference solution in place of the agent.

**3. Arm canary.** `run-pair.sh --pair <dir> --arm calor --canary-only` prints a JSON line whose `armCanary` is `strict-ok` for the strict Calor arm, and `permissive-ok` for a permissive control arm, for every Calor arm configuration that #1816 registers. The canary is a small program that prints under an empty effect row; it proves the arm enforces what its configuration claims. Use the 0.30 canary that #1766 added, as rewritten. If #1816 has not registered its arm configurations when this runs, run the canary for the configurations #1770 registered for #1605 and say so in the record; #1810 and the #1645 re-run then repeat the canary for any configuration added later, in their own dry pass.

**4. End-to-end agent-task suite.** The zero-cost dry run that #1736 defines passes: each task's setup and acceptance on its `expected/` fixture.

**5. Commit to pin.** The commit the paid runs pin is the merge commit of this issue's pull request. Steps 2 to 4 run on the pull request's head, which is based on `main` after the #1803 merge. Before merging, check that `main` contains every fix the #1645 re-run is meant to measure: the fixes merged under #1645 and #1790. List them in the record with their commits. A fix that merges later is listed on #1645 as fixed and not re-measured. Also check that `main` contains every row of the window record marked "land" that changes what compiles or what a compiled program does: #1735, the #1084 implementation, every #1731 group with outcome (b), and any implementation child of #1800. List each with its commit. A row that is not merged blocks this pull request, or is re-recorded in the window record as deferred to a future edition before this pull request merges.

**6. Record.** Write `docs/plans/0.32-instrument-re-registration.md`: the head commit the commands ran on, each command with its exit code, the classification table from step 1, the two lists from step 5, and the date the count restarts or the statement that it continues. After the merge, post the merge commit on #1816, #1810 and #1645 as the commit to pin, with the result of `git merge-base --is-ancestor <commit> <merge commit>` for each fix and each "land" row listed in step 5.

If #1803 recorded that no rewrite was needed, steps 1 to 4 still run, so that the paid runs pin a commit on which every instrument has been checked; step 1 is then expected to find no changed hash.

A failure in steps 2 to 4 is fixed in this pull request only if the fix is to a fixture or script. A compiler defect is filed as its own issue and blocks this one.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/cases-index.json` lists 710 cases, each with a `calorSha256` (first at `:13`). `registration.json` in the same directory has status `FROZEN-AT-MERGE` (`:9`), states that a test regenerates the cases and fails on any byte difference (`:2858`), and allows a template or case to change only by a versioned amendment (`:3059`; its `amendmentLog` at `:3068` is empty).
- The expected verdicts are keyed by case id, hold no hash, and record the commit and runner they were frozen from (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/expected-verdicts.json:5-8`).
- The sweep README says "Do not edit the fixture to make the job green" (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:69`) and documents the freeze command, which refuses a run with any failing check (`:77-91`; implemented at `scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:302-335`).
- The harness runs on macOS ARM64 only (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:14`). The sweep is scheduled once a day (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:26`) on `macos-15` (`:91`). Scheduled and dispatched runs always sweep `main` HEAD (`:100-105`) and upload the run directory as the artifact `soundness-sweep-<run id>-<attempt>` (`:137-146`).
- All 136 templates are in the retired syntax today. 0.31 ports them and registers a successor packet (#1781, #1778). This issue assumes that packet exists.
- `check-probe-pair.sh` accepts pair directories and checks, per defect class, that the C# starter builds, both references pass the held-out suite, and the Calor starter fails in the registered way (`bench/phase0-agent-native/check-probe-pair.sh:1-26`).
- `--null-agent` is documented at `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:13-15` and `--canary-only` at `:197-202`; the canary's passing statuses, `permissive-ok` and `strict-ok`, are set at `:618-633`. The canary sources are retired syntax today (`bench/phase0-agent-native/templates/calor-arm/permissive-canary.calr`, and the inline text at `run-pair.sh:575`); #1766 adds a 0.30 canary.
- The end-to-end agent-task suite is not part of pull-request CI: `.github/workflows/test.yml` does not reference `tests/E2E`. It runs only from a manual dispatch of `.github/workflows/benchmark.yml` with `agent_refactoring=true` (`:389`), which is a paid agent run. Its only dry mode today skips the agent and emits a synthetic record (`tests/E2E/agent-tasks/run.sh:211-217`). The dry run this issue uses is the one #1736 adds: "A dry run (no paid agent) of each task's setup and acceptance on its `expected/` fixture passes."

#### Why

The soundness packet pins a hash per case and the fixture may not be edited to go green, so a respelled template means the packet must be registered again. #1803 changes the pinned files and proves that no verdict moved. This issue makes that a registration: reviewed, classified, frozen from a run on a commit that contains the rewrite, and counted from. The pairs, the canary and the end-to-end suite are validated per file, and a paid run on an unvalidated fixture measures the fixture.

The posted commit also fixes what the 0.32 paid runs measure. A fix for a regression found by the 0.30 agent evaluation (#1605) is re-measured by the #1645 re-run only if it is in that commit, so the record lists which fixes are.

This is new capacity and displaces no existing issue. Without it the 0.32 rewrite would undo the 0.31 work on the instruments, and the 0.35 entry criterion would count green runs of a packet that no longer matches the language.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/0.32-instrument-re-registration.md` exists with the head commit the commands ran on, the commands and exit codes for steps 1 to 4, the classification table, the lists of fixes and of "land" rows from step 5, and either the restart date or the statement that the count continues.
- Either the record shows the `git diff` of the case index printing nothing, or the re-registration is merged through a reviewed pull request whose sweep run passed, with `recordedFrom` in `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/expected-verdicts.json` naming a commit that contains the #1803 merge, every changed case classified as unchanged, port-only or real, and no real entry without a linked issue.
- The first scheduled sweep on `main` after this issue's pull request merged passed its gate. A comment on this issue links the run.
- `check-probe-pair.sh` exits 0 on the nine pairs, and every `--null-agent` run in step 2 passes its held-out suite.
- `--canary-only` prints `strict-ok` or `permissive-ok`, as its configuration claims, for each Calor arm configuration registered at that time; the record names the registration they were read from.
- The #1736 dry run passes on every task.
- The merge commit is posted on #1816, #1810 and #1645 as the commit to pin, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor` exits 0 against it for every fix listed in step 5.
- Every "land" row of the window record that changes what compiles or what a compiled program does is listed with a commit that is an ancestor of the merge commit, or the window record shows it re-recorded as deferred to a future edition.
- If the pull request changed a test count, `eng/test-manifest.json` has the new `expectedTotal`.

#### Not in scope

- Any paid run (#1816, #1810, #1645).
- Adding rows or controls to the packet (0.35).
- Changing the run counts (#1783).
- A continuous-integration job that runs `run-pair.sh` against a built compiler.

Part of epic #1792. Depends on #1803, and on the 0.31 work it validates again: the registered successor packet (#1778), the 0.30 pairs and canary wired into the harness (#1766) and the end-to-end dry run (#1736). Blocks #1805, #1810 and the re-run step of #1645.

### #1805 · [0.32] Decision: declare the edition and publish the compatibility promise

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1807, #1810, #1645, #1816, #1784, #1735 · **Blocks:** #1846, #1697, #1701 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Take and record one maintainer decision: is the language at the post-rewrite commit declared the first edition? An edition is a named set of accepted spellings and default-on hard errors that later compilers keep accepting. This issue produces a decision record and, on a declaration, the published compatibility promise. It changes no compiler behaviour. The per-module marker is #1084 and the rewriter is #1806. Filing this issue decides nothing.

**Gate.** 0.32 has two paid agent collections: the seeded-defect replication (#1810) and the re-run of the 0.30 agent evaluation's regressed pairs (#1645). Both are registered and authorised, or declined, in `docs/plans/0.32-paid-run-registration.md` (#1816). This issue runs no agent and spends nothing.

- Source of the gate values: the #1816 record, the published results of #1810 and #1645, the window record (#1784), and the commit and record #1807 posted.
- Open, when all four hold: #1807 is closed, so the instruments are registered on the post-rewrite commit; the seeded-defect replication has published on that commit, or the #1816 record carries the maintainer's decline of it; the #1645 re-run has published on that commit, or the #1816 record carries its decline; the window record (#1784) is closed, and the #1807 record lists every "land" row (#1735 among them, if it landed) as an ancestor of the posted commit.
- Held: a collection is authorised and has not published, #1807 is open, or the window record is not closed. No declaration is made and this issue stays open.
- If the gate is still held when the maintainer wants to release 0.32, the only option available is C. If the cause is an authorised collection that has not published, the record also says that the 0.32 exit criterion for the replication is carried to 0.33.

**Options.**

| Option | Meaning | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| A. Declare | The spellings and default-on errors at the commit #1807 posted are the first edition. The promise is in force from the 0.32 release. | Every later respelling or new default-on error needs a new edition: a marker value, a rewriter rule and a compiler that accepts both. Choices not flipped in the window stay. |
| B. Declare with listed exceptions | As A, but named forms are outside the promise until a stated milestone. | A weaker promise. An agent result measured on an excepted form may have to be re-run. |
| C. Defer | No edition at 0.32. | 0.33 enters through "deferral recorded", so the per-agent work (#1650, #1651) is built and measured on a language that may still change. 0.36 entry requires the edition in force, so the external pilot stays blocked until a later declaration. |

**Recommendation.**

- A, when both paid collections published and neither reports a regression traced to a form that the window changed or confirmed.
- B, only for a form whose #1645 triage row closed "not recovered" and that has the follow-up issue #1645 filed. Each exception names the form, the evidence, the resolving issue (that follow-up) and an expiry no later than #1846.
- C, when a paid collection was authorised but has not published, or #1807 is open.
- If both collections were declined, A is still available. The record must then say that the edition is declared without an agent run on its spellings, and name the last agent measurement (#1605) and its commit.

**The promise.** Text to adopt or amend, published as a section of `docs/semantics/editions.md` (the page #1084 creates) and the matching page under `website/content/semantics/`:

1. A module that compiles under the edition keeps compiling, with the same meaning, under every later compiler that supports the edition. No accepted spelling is removed or given a new meaning.
2. Additive syntax is allowed when no module that compiled before stops compiling or changes meaning. Reserving a word that could be used as an identifier is not additive.
3. A new default-on hard error on a module that compiles under the edition is a breaking change. New checks arrive as warnings, through the per-module opt-in the #1084 design record defines, or in a new edition.
4. Any later breaking change is a new edition and ships with a `calor fix` rule that migrates fixtures, benchmark pairs and documentation in the same change.

The maintainer also settles five points that the promise must state. Each has a recommended wording; none is decided by this text.

5. *Older editions.* How long a compiler keeps accepting an older edition's modules once a newer one exists. Recommended: removal needs one full minor release of notice and a shipped rewriter.
6. *Soundness fixes.* Whether a fix that withdraws an unsound acceptance, such as a false proof or an unsound built-in effect contract, may reject programs within an edition. Recommended: yes, with a release note and a suggested fix for the affected programs. The cost of "no" is that every such fix waits for a new edition. The unsound contracts known today are decided before the declaration by #1731: fixed (its outcomes a and b) or kept as assumptions listed in the effects reference (outcome c). The wording settled here must say whether the exemption may later be used to tighten a listed assumption, or only an unsoundness found after the declaration. Other 0.32 issues are written to hold under either answer.
7. *Tool layout.* Whether a change to how the tools treat files, without a change to the language, is covered. The known case is #1825, after which `calor test` and `calor check` treat a `tests/*.calr` file as a test, where today `calor test`, `calor check` and `calor run` compile it as library source; `dotnet build` of a `Calor.Sdk` project is unchanged. Recommended: not covered; such changes are listed in the release notes, and #1846 lists this one.
8. *Additive spellings to expect.* The promise says that new spellings for the C# constructs the converter learns in 0.34 are expected after the declaration. They are additive under point 2 and are chosen under the fewest-tokens rule as #1785 amends it.
9. *What is not promised.* Recommended: the text of generated C#, diagnostic wording, warnings, and `calor convert` output.

**What is recorded.** `docs/plans/0.32-edition-declaration.md` holds the date, the option, the edition's name and marker spelling (from #1084), the commit (the one #1807 posted), links to the published runs or the declines, the exceptions (B) or the deferral terms (C), the wording settled for points 5 to 9 (for point 6, including the answer on listed assumptions), and any language-surface change merged after the posted commit, with its class as #1846 part 2 defines the classes.

- On A or B: publish the promise, add a CHANGELOG entry for 0.32, replace the sentence at `website/content/guides/adoption-playbook.mdx:19-20`, and post the link on #1701, whose sign-off lists the promise. #1846 re-affirms the declaration at the close of 0.33.
- On C: the record names each form still open, which may only be a form with a #1645 "not recovered" row or a window row the maintainer reopens by name, and for each the issue that decides it. Any change to such a form follows the standing rule in `docs/release-policy.md` (#1806) and repeats the #1807 procedure in the same change, before any paid run pins a commit. Rows deferred at the cut-off stay deferred. The record also names the milestone at which the declaration is retaken, the effect on the 0.33 and 0.36 entry criteria, and whether #1650 and #1651 proceed or hold. #1846 is amended from "re-affirm" to "declare".

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- There is no edition mechanism: `git grep -i edition -- src` prints nothing.
- The adoption guide says "Syntax and semantics are versioned, but there is no broader compatibility promise yet" (`website/content/guides/adoption-playbook.mdx:19-20`).
- `docs/release-policy.md` has no section on compatibility or breaking changes (`grep -n -i 'compatib\|breaking' docs/release-policy.md` prints nothing).
- The strategy lists "Syntax editions before any further breaking change (Rust-editions precedent; per-module)" (`docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md:210`). 0.30 was a no-compatibility rewrite (`CHANGELOG.md:13`, `:36`) that "publishes no new benchmark results" (`:15`); the first agent evaluation of its spellings is #1605, in 0.31.
- `semver` is a semantics gate that requires an exact major (`src/Calor.Compiler/SemanticsVersion.cs:51-52`). It promises nothing about spellings.
- `ExecutionWorkspace.ResolveSources` takes every `*.calr` file under the target except `bin/`, `obj/` and `reference/` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ExecutionWorkspace.cs:189`, `:222-231`), which is the behaviour point 7 refers to.
- The epic for agent reach, #1653, gives this as its reason for following 0.32: "Launching earlier would teach agents a language that's about to change."

#### Why

Agent reach in 0.33 and the external pilot in 0.36 both need a language that will not move under them, and nothing in the project says it will not. The declaration comes after the paid runs on the post-rewrite commit because the 0.30 spellings were chosen by token count and had not been run by an agent when they shipped. Declaring first would freeze forms that have never been measured.

It is filed as a decision because only the maintainer can bind the project to it, and because deferring is a legitimate outcome with a stated price.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/0.32-edition-declaration.md` exists with every field listed under "What is recorded", and links, for each of the two paid collections, either its published result or the dated decline line in `docs/plans/0.32-paid-run-registration.md`.
- On A or B: `docs/semantics/editions.md` and the matching website page carry the promise with points 1 to 9; `calor self-check docs` exits 0; the CHANGELOG entry is added; `website/content/guides/adoption-playbook.mdx` no longer says there is no compatibility promise and links the page; the link is posted on #1701.
- On B: each exception has a form, an evidence link (the #1645 "not recovered" row), a resolving issue and an expiry.
- On C: the record states the effect on the 0.33 and 0.36 entry criteria, names for each form still open the issue that decides it, says whether the replication criterion is carried to 0.33, and #1846 is amended.
- The record links the closed window record and the #1807 list of "land" rows, and lists any language-surface change merged after the posted commit with its class, or says there is none.
- The pull request changes no file under `src/`.

#### Not in scope

- The marker (#1084), the rewriter (#1806), the rewrite (#1803) and the re-registration (#1807).
- Authorising or running the paid collections (#1816, #1810, #1645).
- Re-affirming the edition after the per-agent results (#1846).
- Defining a second edition.

Part of epic #1792. Depends on #1807, #1816, #1810 and #1645; for each paid collection the dependency is met by its published result or by its recorded decline. Also depends on #1784 (closed) and #1735 (merged inside the posted commit, or re-recorded as deferred). Blocks #1846, #1697 and #1701.

---

## Epic #1808 · [0.32] Epic: effect precision on everyday .NET code

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Before the edition is declared, make it known and checkable how much everyday .NET code the effect system really covers. Decide the built-in contracts that are known to be unsound inside the breaking window (#1731). Report how much converted and agent-written code ends up `unknown` (#1802). Tie every teaching statement about what is pure or unknown to a compile check (#1798). Pin that no tool applies an effect-row or contract edit on its own (#1801). Stop `calor effects suggest --merge` from writing unresolved calls into the project manifest as pure (#1814).

#### Why

Effect rows are a feature that neither C# nor Rust has. At HEAD `bc80daeb` five facts limit what they can claim.

- The built-in manifest gives an empty effect list to members that can run code the effect system does not see: every `System.String` method through a wildcard, `Parse`, `TryParse` and `ToString` on `int`, `long`, `double` and `bool`, 61 LINQ operators, `Object.Equals` and `GetHashCode`, and the methods and properties of `Nullable<T>` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Manifests/bcl-system.calor-effects.json:43-47`, `:66-107`, `:461-526`, `:541-554`). These contracts were withdrawn and then restored during 0.30; tests name "the restored 0.29 BCL manifest" (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/StringInterpolationTests.cs:270-272`). #1731 tracks them and has no milestone.
- Since 0.30 a member that the framework and ecosystem manifests do not list is charged `unknown` (`CHANGELOG.md:64`). A callable that declares `! unknown` is not checked and forces the same row on every caller, and `calor convert` writes that row itself (`docs/syntax-reference/effects.md:292-302`). No report counts how much code carries it.
- The teaching reference tells agents that `Contains(string)`, `Substring`, trimming, `Enumerable.Where` and `ToArray` "remain unknown under strict checking" (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:580`, `:1153`). The manifest lists them as pure, and a benchmark reference solution that calls `Contains(string)` under a pure row is compiled in CI (`tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/N1-003-csv-row/reference/calor/CsvRow.calr:3-5`).
- The compiler's suggested fix for an undeclared effect is to add the effect to the row (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1068`, `:1084`). One test covers one of the two shapes of that fix. The language server marks every fix it offers as the preferred action, safe or not (`src/Calor.LanguageServer/Handlers/CodeActionHandler.cs:77`), and the workflow catalog and the `calor_compile` tool description tell agents that effect errors are fixed automatically.
- `calor effects suggest --merge` writes an empty effect list, which a manifest reads as pure, for every unresolved call into the project's live `.calor-effects.json` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/EffectsCommand.cs:623-631`, `:703-722`). The `Calor0411` message tells the user to add a manifest entry (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:5952`). One command turns `unknown` into pure with no review.

The timing follows from the edition. Once #1805 declares it, a tightened built-in contract is a new hard error on programs that compile. The planned compatibility promise treats that as a breaking change unless the maintainer adopts the soundness exemption that #1805 puts to them. These contracts are known to be unsound now, so #1731 is decided in this window either way. Replacing an `unknown` charge with an exact contract only accepts more programs; that work continues in 0.33 (#1841) and is sized by the report #1802 produces.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1731 | Sound contracts for the built-in members known to run unseen code: one recorded outcome per member group, decided inside the breaking window |
| #1802 | Unknown-effect share per corpus project and per agent run, from one measurement in the effect pass |
| #1798 | A compile check behind every "pure" or "unknown" statement in the teaching text, the primer's `[needs ...]` annotations and the effects reference |
| #1801 | Pin: no effect-row or contract edit is applied automatically, by `calor fix`, the MCP tools or the language server |
| #1814 | `calor effects suggest --merge` never writes an unresolved call into the project manifest as pure |

#### Sequence

1. #1802, part 1 (the corpus columns), as soon as the corpus report job (#1779) exists. It gives #1731 its before figure and #1841 its sizing input. Part 2, the agent-run table, may follow.
2. #1731 records its breaking outcomes in the window record (#1784) before that record's cut-off date.
3. The contract changes of #1731 land. An outcome that changes `.calr` source gives its rule to the rewriter (#1806) and starts rejecting programs only in the single rewrite (#1803).
4. #1798 and #1801 pin the result. #1798 follows #1772 and #1745; whichever of #1798 and a #1731 contract change merges second updates the affected claims in its own pull request. #1801 has no dependency and should merge before #1790 starts marking new fixes safe.
5. #1814 has no ordering.

Calor text that these issues embed in tests or in the teaching source before #1803 merges is part of that rewrite's inventory.

#### Done when
