- The corpus report shows the unknown-effect share for MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation (#1802, part 1). This is the 0.32 exit criterion "Unknown-effect share reported per corpus project"; the agent-run table of part 2 may follow.
- #1731 is closed: every member group has a recorded outcome in the window record (#1784), the landed ones carry the standing acceptance figures, and the deferred ones are listed as assumptions in the effects reference (`website/content/syntax-reference/effects.mdx`, generated into `docs/syntax-reference/effects.md`).
- `calor self-check docs` exits non-zero when a teaching statement about a member's effects no longer matches the compiler (#1798).
- The safe-fix pin runs in pull-request CI, and the language server marks a fix as preferred only when it is safe to apply (#1801).
- After `calor effects suggest --merge`, a pure function that calls an unresolved member still reports `Calor0410` for `unknown` (#1814).

#### Not in this epic

- Correcting the teaching statements themselves: #1772 (0.31), before the #1605 pin.
- Exact contracts for the callee families behind most unknown charges: #1841 (0.33).
- Which diagnostics get fixes and which fixes are marked safe: #1790 (epic #1649).
- A round-trip leg compiled with effects enforced: #1795 (epic #1787).
- The legacy `dbr`/`dbw` effect codes (#1741, epic #1787) and the iteration caps of the effect pass (#1142, epic #1649).
- Whole-file preserved C#, which gets no effect checking at all. Raising the native fraction is #1647 and epic #1659.
- Rejecting guarantees other than effects (null remainder, enum exhaustiveness): #1800.
- Effect diagnostics in the editor. The language server does not run the effect pass, so no effect-row fix reaches an editor and the preferred-flag change in #1801 is preventive. No issue tracks running the pass there; it is a named follow-up.

### #1731 · [0.32] Sound effect contracts for culture-sensitive BCL members, LINQ and Object dispatch

**Action:** move-in (from none) · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1802 · **Blocks:** #1841, #1711 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

**Current**

The issue lists four groups of BCL members that the built-in manifests declare pure although they can run code the effect system does not see: culture-sensitive formatting and parsing, `System.Linq.Enumerable` operators, `Object.Equals` and `GetHashCode`, and `Nullable<T>` members. It records that these contracts were withdrawn during 0.30, that `n.ToString()` then stopped compiling and such files were preserved as C#, and that the 0.29 contracts were restored on 2026-11-14. It asks for an exact contract or a declarable effect per member with no native conversions lost on the 100-input scorecard, and it has no milestone and no labels.

**Moved from** no milestone because after the edition is declared at the end of 0.32 (#1805), tightening a built-in contract is a new hard error on programs that compile, which the planned compatibility promise treats as a breaking change. 0.32 is the last release in which these contracts can change without an edition mechanism or a soundness exemption written into that promise, and the holes are known now.

**Amendment**

Set the milestone to 0.32 and the labels to `enhancement`, `p1`. The title becomes `[0.32] Sound effect contracts for culture-sensitive BCL members, LINQ and Object dispatch`. Five text changes follow.

1. In `## Problem`, add a fifth bullet:

```markdown
- culture-sensitive `System.String` members, which a wildcard contract admits: `ToUpper()`, `ToLower()` and their `CultureInfo` overloads dispatch to the culture's `TextInfo`, and the `string`-argument overloads of `StartsWith`, `EndsWith` and `IndexOf` that take no comparison argument use the current culture. This issue enumerates the other `System.String` members that read the current culture or format an arbitrary object.
```

2. Insert after `## History`:

```markdown
## Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `src/Calor.Compiler/Manifests/bcl-system.calor-effects.json` gives an empty effect list to: every `System.String` method, through the wildcard `"*": []` (:43-47); `Parse`, `TryParse` and `ToString` on `System.Int32` (:66-73), `System.Int64` (:75-82), `System.Double` (:90-98) and `System.Boolean` (:100-107); 61 `System.Linq.Enumerable` operators (:461-526); `Equals` and `GetHashCode` on `System.Object` (:541-550); and every method, accessor and constructor of `System.Nullable<T>`, through `defaultEffects: []` (:552-554; a member with no entry of its own falls back to the type default, `src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectResolver.cs:229-233`, `:276-280`, `:327-331`).
- The same file holds a worked precedent for a sound split. Each `System.Convert` method that can reach user code is named `["unknown"]` and its primitive overloads are listed as exact pure entries (from :115); an exact entry wins over the name entry, which wins over the wildcard (`tests/Calor.Enforcement.Tests/ConvertUserCodeEffectTests.cs:7-19`).
- Tests pin the restored contracts and change with them: `StrictFormatting_ManifestContractIsCharged` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/StringInterpolationTests.cs:270-290`); `StringContracts_CultureDispatchMutatesOrThrowsInOriginalAndIsAdmittedWithParity` (`tests/Calor.Enforcement.Tests/EffectResolverTests.cs:386-394`), which shows `ToUpper()` running a culture callback and being admitted; and the cases commented "restored 0.29" at `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NullableCastTests.cs:160` and `:202-203`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ComputedCallExpressionTests.cs:71` and `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/InteropScope/RefOutImplementationTests.cs:791`. `grep -rn "restored 0.29" tests` lists the rest.
- Formatting is already split. Interpolating a `decimal`, `float`, `uint`, `object` or type-parameter value is `unknown` (`StrictInterpolation_UnprovedFormattingIsUnknown`, `StringInterpolationTests.cs:229-239`); `int`, `long` and `double` are pure.
- The effect codes a contract may use are the registry at `src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectTypes.cs:65-104`, plus the manifest-only code `unknown` (`EffectResolver.cs:517-524`). No code stands for reading the ambient culture or for dispatch to an override.
- Since 0.30 `calor convert` writes `! unknown` on a callable whose effects it cannot resolve, where it used to preserve the file as C# (`docs/syntax-reference/effects.md:301-302`; `DeclareUnknownRows`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1376`). A withdrawn contract can therefore appear as more `! unknown` rows while the native file count stays the same.
- The effects reference does not say that any built-in contract is an assumption: `docs/syntax-reference/effects.md` has no mention of LINQ, `ToString`, `GetHashCode` or `Nullable` (checked with `grep -i`). That file is generated; its source is `website/content/syntax-reference/effects.mdx`, and `calor self-check docs --fix` regenerates the copy.
- Not verified when this baseline was written: the commit hashes under History (checked against a shallow clone with no history). `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NativeMetadataClosureTests.cs:774` names `12b1adb3e` as the restoring commit. `CHANGELOG.md` does not record the withdrawal or the restoration.
```

3. Insert before `## Done when` (two sections):

```markdown
## Breaking window

This issue is a row of the 0.32 breaking-window decision record (#1784, `docs/design/0.32-breaking-window.md`). Two kinds of change here break programs that compile today: a new effect code that existing rows must add, and any contract change that makes such a program report `Calor0410`. The five member groups are: `System.String`; numeric and `bool` formatting and parsing; `System.Linq.Enumerable`; `Object.Equals` and `GetHashCode`; `Nullable<T>`.

In every group, an overload shown to run no culture, provider or override code gets its own exact pure entry, in the pattern `System.Convert` uses; start from the overloads the withdrawn 0.30 commits and their tests already analysed (see History). That part rejects nothing. For the members that remain, the maintainer records one outcome per group in the window record before its cut-off date:

- (a) nothing remains: every member of the group has an exact entry and today's programs keep compiling. Cost: per-overload analysis and tests.
- (b) an explicit effect that callers declare with one row entry: an existing code, or a new one whose spelling is decided in the window record under the amended rule (#1785): its candidates join the window's payload manifest before the payload date and are counted in the single dispatch per tokenizer. Cost: every function that reaches such a member, and each of its callers, gains a row entry, and programs that compile today are rejected. Compiler support for a new code lands with this issue while the old contract stays in force, the rewrite rule goes to the batch rewriter (#1806), and the contract change that rejects today's programs takes effect in the single repository rewrite (#1803), not before.
- (c) the contract stays for this edition, is listed in the effects reference as an assumption with the reason, and the sound contract waits for a future edition, a per-module opt-in under the edition marker (#1084), or a soundness exemption if the edition declaration (#1805) adopts one. Cost: a documented hole; #1711 leaves these paths out of effect evidence or discloses them as assumed.

Charging the remaining members `unknown` is the 0.30 change that was reverted on 2026-11-14. It is not among the outcomes, because it fails the second bullet of Done when: every caller would have to declare `! unknown`.

Recommended, not decided: (c) for the remaining members of each group, unless the acceptance figures under Done when show that (b) loses no native conversion and does not raise the density ratio. A member group with no recorded outcome at the cut-off takes (c). Replacing an `unknown` charge with an exact contract accepts more programs, is not breaking, and may land at any time.

## Order and delivery

- The before figure for each group is the unknown-effect share of the corpus report (#1802). If that report has not landed, use the count of `! unknown` rows in the migrated corpus output (`grep -rc '! unknown'` over the `.calr` files of the corpus report artifact) and say so.
- Outcomes are recorded before the cut-off date of the window record. A contract change that keeps today's programs compiling may merge at any time; one that rejects them merges only as part of #1803.
- This issue is closed by the one Done when below. It may be delivered as one pull request per member group; each pull request names its group and posts that group's before and after figures here. If any group takes (b), the issue closes only after #1803 has merged.
```

4. Replace `## Done when` with:

```markdown
## Done when

- Every member group has one recorded outcome, (a), (b) or (c), in the window record, and the row says whether `.calr` source changes. The exact entries and every (b) outcome are implemented, and every (c) group is listed as an assumption in `website/content/syntax-reference/effects.mdx`, with `docs/syntax-reference/effects.md` regenerated and `calor self-check docs` reporting no drift.
- `n.ToString()`, `"${n}"` and a LINQ `Where`/`Select` with pure lambdas still compile in a pure function, or the replacement spelling is one short row.
- For a group with outcome (b): a test shows that the code is accepted in a row, and that until #1803 merges a program that calls the member without it still compiles, with a warning at most. The rejection is part of the #1803 pull request.
- The standing acceptance rule for a tightened hard error is met, with the figures posted on this issue before and after each contract change:
  - the conversion scorecard's fully-converted count stays at its baseline (100 of 100, `tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/ConversionScorecardTests.cs:31`; CI job `conversion-scorecard`);
  - the density ratio (migrated Calor tokens divided by original C# tokens) per corpus project does not rise (the #1746 columns on the corpus report job, #1779; if those columns have not landed, the hand procedure recorded in #1746);
  - the number of files that newly fall to whole-file preservation is reported per corpus project (#1779);
  - the unknown-effect share per corpus project is reported (#1802, or the fallback count above), and any rise is explained.
- `CHANGELOG.md` has an entry for each contract that changed and for each group left as an assumption. It records neither the 0.30 withdrawal nor the restoration today.
- The tests listed under Baseline are updated in the same change as the contract they pin, `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated for every test project whose test count changes, and the teaching statements about these members still pass their compile checks (#1772; #1798 once it has merged).
```

**Why**

The decision belongs in the breaking window for the reason given under Moved from, and any new effect spelling goes through the window record. The current body asks only that the scorecard lose no native conversion; since 0.30 a withdrawn contract shows up as more `! unknown` rows with the native file count unchanged, so the amended Done when adds the density ratio, the newly preserved files and the unknown-effect share, with #1802 supplying the before figure. ### #1802 · [0.32] Report the unknown-effect share per corpus project and in every agent-run report

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, ci · **Depends on:** #1779 · **Blocks:** #1731, #1811, #1841, #1708 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Count how much Calor code is outside effect checking because of `unknown`, with one measurement that two reports use. The work has two parts, delivered in order. Part 1 (steps 1 and 2) is on the 0.32 exit chain. Part 2 (step 3) may follow it and does not hold the 0.32 release; if 0.32 ships first, the issue carries over to 0.33 with part 1 recorded as done.

1. **Measurement (part 1).** Add an opt-in recorder to `EffectEnforcementPass` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs`), reachable from `Program.Compile` so that a test can read it (for example on `CompilationResult`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:1599`), and one command or build switch that writes it as JSON per source file. The command surface is the implementer's choice. The measurement compile runs with effect checking on and the default unknown-call policy, whatever the project's own build uses, and with the project's own references, because a type from an assembly that was not passed does not resolve and its calls are charged `unknown` (`CHANGELOG.md:66`). It only reads the record, so a compile error does not fail it. Per file it records:
   - the callables the pass checked (functions, methods, constructors, accessors, static-initializer rows) and how many declare `! unknown`;
   - the callables whose effects are assumed, not verified (`Calor0419`: the body holds preserved C# or an unrecognized construct, or calls such a callable);
   - the call sites for which a contract was looked up, and how many were charged `unknown`;
   - the callee of each unknown charge: declaring type and member, or the source text when the receiver has no type.

   The per-file JSON also lists each callable by name and span with two flags, declares `! unknown` and effects assumed (`Calor0419`), so that a share can be taken over any subset of callables, such as the functions on one task's change path (#1708). A charge is counted where the pass makes it, not from diagnostics.
2. **Corpus report (part 1).** The corpus report job (#1779) migrates MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation with default `calor migrate` and writes one JSON report per project. Following the rule #1779 defines for later columns (an object under the issue's own key in the report's `columns`), add a column holding: both shares (unknown-declared callables over callables, unknown-charged call sites over call sites), the raw counts, and the 20 most frequent unknown callees with their site counts. The job artifact keeps the full callee list.
3. **Agent-run report (part 2).** Print the same quantities per arm and pair for the Calor arms, computed from each run's archived `final-src/` directory (`epochs/<epoch>/<pair>/<arm>/run-N/final-src/`). An arm is one benchmark configuration; a pair is one task with a Calor and a C# starter and a shared hidden test suite. Add this to the evaluation report script (#1760) if it has merged; otherwise write a standalone script under `bench/phase0-agent-native/` that #1760 then calls. Run it over the archived outputs of the 0.30 evaluation (#1605) and commit the table beside the #1605 results. No agent is run and nothing is spent. If 0.31 closed with "no paid run authorised" there are no #1605 archives: the script is then merged with a test on a synthetic `final-src/` tree, and the table is recorded as not measured.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Nothing reports an unknown share. `calor effects suggest` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/EffectsCommand.cs:333`) lists the distinct unresolved callees of the files it is given. Its collector reads receiver types from the bound tree but takes no assembly references, drops duplicate calls (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/ExternalCallCollector.cs:160-174`; `EffectsCommand.cs:501`) and looks each call up by a key built from strings (`EffectsCommand.cs:492-499`). It gives no site counts and nothing per callable, and it is not the charge the effect pass makes.
- The pass keeps computed effects and assumption reasons per callable (`EffectEnforcementPass.cs:117`, `:124`) and no record of unknown charges. It charges `unknown` at more than one place: `ReportUnknownCall` (`:5937`) has two callers (`:4468`, `:8845`), and other paths charge on their own (for example an unresolvable free name, `:4562-4594`). `Calor0411` is not reported inside a callable that declares `! unknown` (`:5932-5940`), and `calor convert` writes that row on every callable it cannot resolve (`DeclareUnknownRows`, `:1376`). Counting diagnostics would therefore undercount converted code in particular.
- The pass runs only when `CompilationOptions.EnforceEffects` is true, which is the default (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:1007-1011`, `:1385`; MSBuild property `CalorEnforceEffects`, `src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:152-155`). Under `UnknownCallPolicy.Permissive` an unresolved call is charged nothing (`EffectEnforcementPass.cs:4461-4465`, `:8842-8843`), and the migrator's own validation compiles turn enforcement off and use that policy (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/ProjectMigrator.cs:853-854`, `:915-916`). A build of the migrated corpus with enforcement off, or a count taken from the migrator's compiles, would record nothing.
- `EffectResolver.Resolve` already counts per call site for the key ledger (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectResolver.cs:71-81`, `:110`).
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:106-125` archives each run's final sources to `final-src/`. It copies `.calr` and `.cs` files only (`:110`), so a `.calor-effects.json` that an agent wrote is not in the archive, and the back-computed figure charges the calls it covered `unknown`; the table says so. Loop telemetry holds only a code and a declaration id per diagnostic (`bench/phase0-agent-native/loop-telemetry-schema.md:32`). Archives made before that function existed, such as `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution`, have no `final-src/`.
- None of the 50 translated pair files under `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs` declares `! unknown` (`grep -rl '! unknown'` returns nothing). On those pairs the agent-run figure shows only what agents add.

#### Why

A callable that declares `! unknown` is not effect-checked, and every caller has to declare the same row (`docs/syntax-reference/effects.md:292-302`). Since 0.30 a member that the framework and ecosystem manifests do not list is charged `unknown` (`CHANGELOG.md:64`), and no number says how much real code that covers. Four other issues need the number. #1841 chooses the callee families that get exact contracts by their share of unknown-charged call sites. #1708 puts on each task card the share of functions on the change path that declare `! unknown` or sit in preserved C#, and excludes a task above a threshold from effect evidence. #1731 reports the share before and after each contract change, because a withdrawn contract now appears as more `! unknown` rows while the native file count stays the same. #1811 reports it per agent. This is new capacity and displaces nothing: it is a 0.32 exit criterion and no existing issue produces the figure.

#### Done when

Part 1, which satisfies the 0.32 exit criterion "unknown-effect share reported per corpus project":

- The corpus report for each of the three projects contains the quantities of step 2, and a scheduled run that produced them is linked on this issue.
- A unit test compiles a module in which one callable declares `! unknown` and makes two calls that have no effect contract (`new System.Version()` has none, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NativeEffectSuggestionTests.cs:117`). It asserts one unknown-declared callable, listed by name with its flag, two unknown-charged call sites and no `Calor0411`.
- With the recorder off, which is the default, generated C# and diagnostics do not change: the existing tests pass unedited, and `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` rises only by the tests this issue adds (`scripts/check_trx.py` enforces the count, `.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).
- The first corpus figures are posted on #1731, #1841 and #1708.

Part 2, after which the issue is closed:

- The table of step 3 is committed for the archived #1605 runs, and a run without `final-src/` is listed as not measurable. If no #1605 archive exists, the script and its synthetic-tree test are merged instead and "not measured" is recorded on this issue.

#### Not in scope

- Lowering the share (#1841) and any threshold. The figures are descriptive.
- Whole-file preserved C# (a `.calr` file in which `calor migrate` kept the original C# byte for byte). The pass sees no callables there; #1779 counts those files.

Part of epic #1808. Depends on #1779. Blocks #1731, #1841, #1811 and #1708.

### #1798 · [0.32] Compile-check every "remains unknown" statement in the teaching text

**Action:** new · **Labels:** documentation, tests · **Depends on:** #1772, #1745 · **Blocks:** #1841 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Make CI fail when a statement in agent-facing text about whether a .NET member is pure, is `unknown` or needs an effect code stops matching the compiler.

1. **Register the claims.** The generated teaching text has one source, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`. Add a list of effect claims there, or in a file beside it, that no generated output renders. A claim names the text it backs (a prose field of `reference.json`, or for a hand-written page the file and the sentence quoted verbatim), the member, the compile setting (default `CompilationOptions`, or `StrictEffects = true` where the sentence says "under strict checking"), the expected outcome and a probe: a complete module in current syntax whose one function declares the empty row `!` and uses the member. For example:

   ```calor
   module EffectClaimProbe
     func Has (string:text, string:part) → bool
       !
       return text.Contains(part)
   ```

   Start from the probes #1772 committed as tests when it corrected the text.
2. **Check the claims** in `calor self-check docs` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/`). The expected outcomes are:
   - `pure`: the probe compiles with no error and no `Calor0411`, `Calor0419` or `Calor0425` diagnostic;
   - `unknown`: the probe reports `Calor0410` for the effect `unknown`, and has no error once its row is `! unknown`;
   - `needs <codes>`: the probe reports `Calor0410` naming exactly those codes, and has no error once its row declares them.
3. **Cover three bodies of text.**
   - *The prose fields of `reference.json`* (`constructs[].description`, `constructs[].calorSyntax`, `primer.introduction`, `overview`, `introduction`, the rule text in `agentInstructions` or in the fields #1823 split it into, and any prose field added to `reference.json` later; the check enumerates string fields and fails on one it does not classify as prose, program or data). Every sentence there that contains `pure`, `unknown` or `effect contract`, in any letter case, is either backed by a claim or listed verbatim in an exemption list kept beside the claims. The exemption list is for sentences about syntax, such as "empty ! declares a pure row".
   - *The `[needs ...]` annotations in `primer.introduction`*, such as `Console.WriteLine(expression)  Print line [needs cw]`. Each annotation has a `needs` claim, and an annotation with no claim is a finding.
   - *The member-level statements of the effects reference*, which is written by hand in `website/content/syntax-reference/effects.mdx` and generated into `docs/syntax-reference/effects.md`. A claim for this page quotes its sentence, and the check also fails when the quoted sentence is no longer in the page, so that an edit forces the claim to be re-read. No completeness rule applies to this page: its claims are the statements listed under Baseline, plus any assumption #1731 lists there.

   A statement whose probe needs an assembly outside the default reference set (the `IConfiguration` indexer) goes on the exemption list with that reason.
4. **Reporting.** Report failures under a new code in the self-check band (`Calor1335` is the first unused code after `Calor1334`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1362`), and add it to the tables in `docs/cli/self-check.md` and `docs/cli/structured-output.md`; an implemented `Calor13xx` code missing from the second table is itself a finding (`Calor1327`).

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `ExemplarCompileChecker.GenerateTeachingResources` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/ExemplarCompileChecker.cs:42-193`) renders `reference.json` into the full reference, the primer (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md`, served as `calor://primer`), `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md`, the website page, the lookup JSON and the four `calor init` templates. Checking the source covers all of them; a mirror that drifts is already `Calor1329`.
- Member-level statements sit in five fields of `reference.json`: `linq_query.description` (:655: `Enumerable.Where` and `ToArray` "remain unknown under strict checking"), `string_interpolation.description` (:673: "neither ${value} nor a format clause supplies an authoritative effect contract"), `string_operations.calorSyntax` and `.description` (:1374-1375: `Contains(string)`, `Substring` and trimming "remain unknown"; for `string.Concat` and comparison-mode searches "strict effect contracts are unavailable"; string `+` is "pure"), and `primer.introduction` (:2432). In all, 27 or 28 sentences (depending on how sentences are split) in 10 prose fields match the rule of step 3. #1772 changes this text before this issue starts.
- The manifest contradicts several of them. `System.String` methods are pure through a wildcard (`src/Calor.Compiler/Manifests/bcl-system.calor-effects.json:43-47`), and `Where` and `ToArray` are listed with no effects (:464, :506). `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/N1-003-csv-row/reference/calor/CsvRow.calr:3-5` calls `Contains(string)` under `!`, and `PairProgram_CompilesWithoutErrors` compiles it with default options (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/BenchCorpusCompilesTests.cs:61-67`). Interpolating an `int`, `long` or `double` is pinned as pure (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/StringInterpolationTests.cs:270-290`). Not verified by running the compiler: no .NET SDK was available when this was written.
- `primer.introduction` carries four annotations: `[needs cw]` on `Console.WriteLine(expression)`, `[needs alloc]` on `new string(chars)`, and `[needs mut]` on `items.Add(value)` and on `set ages[key] value` (rendered at `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md:22`, `:59`, `:69`, `:73`). Whether each holds was not verified; the manifest gives the `string(char[])` constructor an empty list (`bcl-system.calor-effects.json:54-56`), so the `alloc` claim rests on how the pass charges `new`.
- The effects reference makes these member-level statements (line numbers in `website/content/syntax-reference/effects.mdx`; the generated copy has them at `docs/syntax-reference/effects.md:379-381`, `:608-609`, `:613-616`): `System.String.Empty` is pure and a field with no entry, such as `System.EventArgs.Empty`, remains Unknown (:374-376); a dotted name the compiler cannot resolve is charged `unknown` (:603-604); `List<T>`, `Dictionary<K,V>` and `string` indexers are pure, `config["key"]` on `IConfiguration` is `env:r`, and an indexer with no contract is `unknown` (:608-611).
- `calor self-check docs` compiles complete example programs only: for the teaching source, the primer and reference programs; for website pages, `calor` fences that start with `module`, where an `expect=` annotation lists the codes a negative example must report (`docs/cli/self-check.md:97-107`). Its documentation lists "wrong descriptions of behavior" under what it cannot catch (`docs/cli/self-check.md:242-249`). CI runs it on every pull request (`.github/workflows/test.yml:266-267`).
- The teaching tests compile reference programs with `StrictEffects = true` and assert no `Calor0411`, `Calor0419` or `Calor0425` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ExemplarCompilesTests.cs:253-260`). That is the setting "under strict checking" refers to.
- Token budgets that the claim list must not touch: primer 1,600 tokens and full reference 17,000 tokens (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:54`, `:63`).

#### Why

Agents read the teaching text, not the manifests. A statement that a common call "remains unknown" makes an agent write a loop by hand, and one that wrongly says "pure" produces a `Calor0410` the agent was told not to expect. The text was already wrong at 0.30: it calls `Contains(string)`, `Where` and `ToArray` unknown while the manifest lists them as pure. #1772 corrects it once, and nothing keeps it correct while #1731 and #1841 change the contracts it describes. #1745 runs the reference examples; this issue extends that to the prose claims about effects, including the primer's `[needs ...]` annotations and the hand-written effects reference, which #1772 leaves to this issue. It is new capacity and displaces nothing: it is one check in an existing CI step.

#### Done when

- Every member-level effect statement present in `reference.json` after #1772, each `[needs ...]` annotation, and each effects-reference statement listed under Baseline has at least one claim or an exemption with a reason, and the pull request lists them.
- Unit tests in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/DocDriftCheckerTests.cs` feed the checker a copy of its inputs in which (a) one claim's expected outcome is inverted, (b) a sentence that matches the completeness rule of step 3 has neither a claim nor an exemption, or a string field is added that the check does not classify, (c) a `[needs ...]` annotation has no claim, and (d) a quoted effects-reference sentence has been edited. Each yields a finding with the new code, and `calor self-check docs` exits non-zero on such inputs.
- On the repository as merged, `calor self-check docs` reports no drift.
- No generated mirror changes because of the claim list, and `TokenBudgetTests` passes with no constant changed. `expectedTotal` for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` in `eng/test-manifest.json` (:8) is raised by the tests added.
- `docs/cli/self-check.md` lists the check and no longer counts effect statements among the things it cannot catch, and `docs/cli/structured-output.md` lists the new code.

#### Not in scope

- Correcting the statements in `reference.json` (#1772) and changing any contract (#1731, #1841). If a claim for the primer annotations or the effects reference fails when first written, this issue corrects that sentence.
- Other hand-written pages. Their complete examples already have the `expect=` checks.

Part of epic #1808. Depends on #1772 and #1745. Blocks #1841.

### #1801 · [0.32] Pin: effect-row and contract changes are never marked safe to apply

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Pin by test that no path applies an effect-row or contract edit unless a person or an agent chooses it.

1. **Site inventory.** Add a test class to `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests` (for example `SafeFixPolicyTests`). It parses every `.cs` file under `src/` with Roslyn, as `ArchitectureTests` does (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ArchitectureTests.cs:633-636`), and collects every `SuggestedFix` creation whose `isSafeToApply` argument is anything other than absent or the literal `false`. The set must equal a list in the test, with one entry per site and a note of what the fix edits. The failure message states the rule: a fix that adds, removes or changes an effect row (a `!` line), a parameter refinement (`| (...)`) or a postcondition (a `=>` line) is never marked safe. The rules of the batch rewriter (#1806) are outside this inventory: they are applied only by its batch migration mode and never carry the flag. If one were built as a `SuggestedFix` with the flag set, it would fail this test like any other new site.
2. **Behaviour.** For each of the six sites that build an effect-row or contract edit (see Baseline), trigger it. Assert that the fix is offered, that `IsSafeToApply` is false, and that `DiagnosticFixes.Repair` returns the source unchanged with zero edits.
   - `Calor0410`, extend a row (`EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1068`): `Program.Compile` on a function whose row lacks a code it uses, as the existing test does.
   - `Calor0410`, insert a row (`EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1084`): the site needs a callable that has no row and is neither private nor internal. For a public function the parser stops at `Calor0124` first (`MissingNestedRow_IsInsertedInsideTheMethodBeforeContracts`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NativeEffectSuggestionTests.cs:91-110`). Whether any source text reaches the site was not verified. If none does, run `EffectEnforcementPass` on a parsed module from which the row has been removed, or list the site in the test as unreachable with the reason.
   - `Calor0928`: no production code sets `EnableContractInference` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/VerificationAnalysisPass.cs:38`, `:187-191`). Run `ContractInferencePass` directly, as `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/BugDetectionImprovementTests.cs:486-487` does.
   - `Calor0926`: `PreconditionSuggester` is registered at `src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/BugPatternChecker.cs:170-171` and reports only when `ReportOnlyVerified` is off (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/Patterns/PreconditionSuggester.cs:178`; the CLI's `--all-findings`).
3. **Language server.** `src/Calor.LanguageServer/Handlers/CodeActionHandler.cs:77` sets `IsPreferred = true` on every fix; a preferred action is the one an editor's auto-fix command applies. Set it from `diagnostic.Fix.IsSafeToApply`. The server does not run the effect pass today, so the test uses a fix that does reach it: in a handler-level test modelled on `tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/Utilities/BindingDiagnosticRoutingTests.cs:175-185`, assert that the similar-name suggestion for an undefined reference (the source of `UndefinedReference_WithSimilarName_GeneratesFix`, `tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/Handlers/CodeActionHandlerTests.cs:24`) is offered and not preferred. No safe fix appears to reach the server from source text (see Baseline), so test the `true` case on the mapping itself, with a constructed `SuggestedFix` whose `isSafeToApply` is true.
4. **Agent text.** Three statements tell agents that effect errors are fixed automatically: two steps of the workflow catalog served as `calor://workflows` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json:2710`, `:2722`) and the `calor_compile` tool description (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/CompileTool.cs:21-22`). #1772 (0.31) corrects them. Add a test with a detector for that claim over every workflow step's `why` and every MCP tool description: the three sentences as they read at HEAD are its positive cases, and the current catalog and descriptions must not be flagged. If #1772 has not merged, correct the three statements here, to say that only safe syntactic patches are applied and effect rows are never changed, and regenerate with `calor self-check docs --fix`.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `SuggestedFix.IsSafeToApply` defaults to false (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1595-1621`), and `DiagnosticFixes.Apply` uses only safe fixes (`:1633`). `DiagnosticFixes.Repair` (`:1701`) is the automatic path: `calor fix --apply` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/FixCommand.cs:107`), the fix path of `calor lint` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/LintCommand.cs:396`) and the MCP check, compile, fix and migrate tools call it.
- `src/` has 21 `new SuggestedFix(` sites (counted with `grep`). Five pass `isSafeToApply`: removing a legacy closer (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:618-621`), tab and indent-width normalisation (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:396-398`, `:405-407`, each under a condition), trailing whitespace (`src/Calor.Compiler/Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:438-441`) and merging two literal return branches (`:478-481`).
- Six sites build an effect-row or contract edit, and none is marked safe: adding codes to a row or inserting a row for `Calor0410` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1068`, `:1084`); inferred refinements and postconditions, `Calor0928` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/ContractInference/ContractInferencePass.cs:66`, `:110`, `:135`); and a suggested divisor refinement, `Calor0926` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/Patterns/PreconditionSuggester.cs:183`).
- The rule is documented (`docs/cli/fix.md:16-17`, `CHANGELOG.md:88-91`) and pinned for the extend-a-row fix only: `KnownEffects_OfferApplicableNativeEditsWithoutAutomaticPolicyChanges` covers four variants of it (with and without existing codes, LF and CRLF) through `DiagnosticFixes.Apply` and the MCP fix tool (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NativeEffectSuggestionTests.cs:31-63`).
- `Repair` refuses a batch that changes the generated C# only when the input compiled without errors (`Diagnostic.cs:1731-1733`). A source that reports `Calor0410` did not.
- The language server analyses a document in five phases: lexing, parsing, binding, bind validation and return validation (`src/Calor.LanguageServer/State/DocumentState.cs:99-106`). It does not run `EffectEnforcementPass`, so no `Calor0410` fix reaches an editor today. The fixes that do reach it are heuristics such as the similar-name suggestion; the indentation fixes, which can be safe, come from the lexer's indentation pass, which the server runs with a discarded diagnostic bag (`:390`). Every fix it offers is marked preferred, and no test reads `IsPreferred`.
- `CompileTool` applies only safe patches and its `autoFix` option says so (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/CompileTool.cs:85-89`, `:215-218`). Its top-level description says the opposite: "Auto-fixes parser, ID, and effect errors by default" (`:21-22`). The workflow catalog is outside every budget in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs`; the tool description is inside the MCP-surface budget.

#### Why

An effect row is a promise about what a function may do. The compiler's suggested fix for an undeclared effect is to add that effect to the row. If any tool applied the edit on its own, every `Calor0410` would repair itself by widening the row, and the check would constrain nothing. The property holds today only because nobody has written `isSafeToApply: true` at six sites, and one test covers one of them. #1790 will add fixes for the diagnostics agents hit most and mark the syntactic ones safe, which is when a wrong flag is most likely to slip in. The language server's preferred flag and the agent-facing text already point the other way. This is new capacity and displaces nothing: it is one small pull request, and it protects #1790.

#### Done when

- The inventory test passes with the five sites above and fails when a sixth appears without a list entry. A unit test feeds the collector a C# snippet that constructs a `SuggestedFix` with `isSafeToApply: true` and asserts that it is found, so the inventory cannot pass by finding nothing.
- The behaviour tests pass for the six effect-row and contract sites, or for five with the insert-a-row site listed in the test as unreachable from source text.
- The language-server tests of step 3 pass: the similar-name fix is offered with `IsPreferred` false, and a constructed safe fix maps to `IsPreferred` true.
- The test of step 4 passes: its detector flags each of the three HEAD sentences and flags nothing in `reference.json`, its generated `workflows.json` or the MCP tool descriptions as merged. `calor self-check docs` reports no drift.
- The new tests run in pull-request CI: `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` (:8) and `Calor.LanguageServer.Tests` (:66), which `scripts/check_trx.py` enforces (`.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).

#### Not in scope

- A runtime refusal inside `DiagnosticFixes.Repair` for batches that change a row or a contract.
- Which diagnostics get a fix and which fixes are marked safe (#1790); reporting fix coverage of agent-hit diagnostics (#1760).
- The rules of the batch rewriter and the mode that applies them (#1806).
- Running the effect pass in the language server.
- `calor effects suggest --merge`, which is not a diagnostic fix (#1814).

Part of epic #1808.

### #1814 · [0.32] calor effects suggest --merge never turns an unresolved call into a pure manifest entry

**Action:** new · **Labels:** bug, p1 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

`calor effects suggest` lists the calls in the given `.calr` files for which no effect contract is found and writes a manifest template with one entry per call. With `--merge` it writes those entries into the project's live manifest, `.calor-effects.json`, where an empty effect list means pure. Change the command (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/EffectsCommand.cs`) so that an entry nobody has filled in can never be read as pure, and so that it never replaces a manifest it could not read. For example, this module reports `Calor0410` today, because the `System.Version` constructor has no effect contract (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/NativeEffectSuggestionTests.cs:115-125`):

```calor
module Sample
  func Make () → System.Version
    !
    return new System.Version()
```

By reading the code, `suggest --merge` writes `"constructors": { "()": [] }` for `System.Version` into the manifest, and from the next compile the constructor call is no longer charged `unknown`.

1. **Unfilled entries.** Either of two behaviours closes this issue. Choose one and state the choice in the pull request.
   - (a) Every entry the command writes, in the merged manifest and in the stand-alone template alike, is `["unknown"]`, the manifest code that charges the caller `unknown`. A call with such an entry resolves, so `Calor0411` is no longer reported for it and a later `suggest` no longer lists it; a caller with a pure row still reports `Calor0410`. The command's output says so.
   - (b) `--merge` adds no unfilled entry to `.calor-effects.json`: the template goes to `.calor-effects.suggested.json` as it does without the option, the live manifest is left byte-identical, and the command says so. Since `suggest` never fills an entry, this retires `--merge` in all but name.

   Recommended: (a). It keeps the to-do list in the file the user will edit, and the built-in manifests already use the code for members that run code they cannot name.
2. **Output text.** The summary prints `→ []  (fill in effects)` for each call, and the closing hint ends "Use [] for pure methods with no side effects." (`EffectsCommand.cs:616`, `:649-651`). Reword both to show what was written and to say that `[]` is a claim of purity to make only after reading the member.
3. **Unparseable manifest.** With `--merge`, when the existing `.calor-effects.json` cannot be parsed, the command leaves the file byte-identical, prints the parse error and exits non-zero. Today it replaces the file with the template (see Baseline).
4. **Tests and documentation.** Update the merge tests in `tests/Calor.Enforcement.Tests/EffectsSuggestTests.cs`, add a command-level test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/EffectsSuggestCliTests.cs` (it runs the CLI through `CliTestHarness.RunCli`), and update the two `--merge` passages of `website/content/cli/effects.mdx` (`:26-27`, `:58-60`).

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `BuildSuggestedManifest` writes `new List<string>()` for every constructor, getter, setter and method it lists and labels the manifest `"confidence": "inferred"` (`EffectsCommand.cs:680-730`; the label at `:690`, the empty lists at `:709`, `:713`, `:717`, `:721`).
- With `--merge` the output path defaults to `.calor-effects.json` in the `--project` directory, or the current directory (`:623-631`). If the file exists, `MergeManifest` adds each new type and member to the existing manifest and returns it, so its `confidence` value is kept, `verified` included (`:732-778`); existing entries are never overwritten (`MergeDictionary`, `:786-798`). If the file does not exist, the template itself is written there (`:639`). If the existing file cannot be parsed, the `catch` returns the template, which then replaces the file (`:779-783`).
- The compiler loads `.calor-effects.json` from the project directory at the highest priority (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/Manifests/ManifestLoader.cs:85-89`, `:157-164`) and reads an empty list as an explicit pure contract (`CreateResolution`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectResolver.cs:526-540`). Without `--merge` the template goes to `.calor-effects.suggested.json` (`EffectsCommand.cs:634`), which the loader does not read.
- `"unknown"` is a valid manifest code (`ManifestLoader.cs:313-315`). It resolves to the unknown effect with status `Resolved`, so no wildcard, type default or IL analysis replaces it (`EffectResolver.cs:517-529`). The built-in manifests use it, for example for `GetService` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Manifests/bcl-framework-interfaces.calor-effects.json:89`).
- The compiler sends users to the manifest: the `Calor0411` message reads "Add effect declaration in a .calor-effects.json manifest" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:5949-5953`), and the diagnostics table says "Add the method to a `.calor-effects.json` manifest" (`docs/syntax-reference/effects.md:637`).
- `Merge_PreservesExistingEffects` pins today's behaviour: a manifest marked `verified` gains the method `Cancel` with an empty list and stays `verified` (`tests/Calor.Enforcement.Tests/EffectsSuggestTests.cs:881`, assertions at `:930` and `:937`). No test in `EffectsSuggestCliTests.cs` passes `--merge`.
- The website page says of `--merge` only that the generated file and its entries need review (`website/content/cli/effects.mdx:26-27`, `:58-60`).
- Not verified by running: no .NET SDK was available when this was written.

#### Why

An empty list in a manifest is a claim that a member has no effects. `--merge` makes that claim for every call the compiler could not resolve, in the file the compiler trusts most, and keeps whatever confidence label the file had. A pure function that called the member was charged `unknown` and reported `Calor0410` before the command; after it the charge is gone, and nobody has read the member. That defeats effect checking more thoroughly than widening a row does, because a widened row at least stays visible in the source. The `Calor0411` message points users at the manifest, so this is the path an agent told to fix an unknown call will take. It also means that the unknown-effect share (#1802) of any project where the command has been run understates what nobody reviewed. This is new capacity and displaces nothing: it is one small pull request.

#### Done when

- A command-level test writes a module whose pure function makes a call that `suggest` lists as unresolved, runs `calor effects suggest --merge` in that directory, then compiles the module with that directory as `CompilationOptions.ProjectDirectory` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:1405`) and asserts that it still reports `Calor0410` for the effect `unknown`. The test fails on the command as it is at HEAD. It runs twice: with no `.calor-effects.json` present, and with one whose `confidence` is `verified`.
- If (a) was chosen: a unit test asserts that the manifest `BuildSuggestedManifest` returns contains no empty effect list, and `Merge_PreservesExistingEffects` asserts that the added method is `["unknown"]`. If (b) was chosen: a test asserts that `.calor-effects.json` is byte-identical after `--merge` and that `.calor-effects.suggested.json` was written.
- A test runs `--merge` against a `.calor-effects.json` that is not valid JSON and asserts a non-zero exit code and a byte-identical file.
- `Merge_ExistingEmptyEffectsNotOverwritten` and `MergeDictionary_OnlyAddsNewKeys` pass unchanged: entries a person wrote, empty or not, are still never overwritten.
- The summary line and the closing hint no longer present `[]` as the value to leave in place, `website/content/cli/effects.mdx` describes the new behaviour, and `calor self-check docs` reports no drift.
- `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised for `Calor.Enforcement.Tests` (:24) and `Calor.Compiler.Tests` (:8) by the tests added.

#### Not in scope

- The suggested fix for `Calor0410` and the rule that it is never applied automatically (#1801).
- Exact contracts for the callees `suggest` lists (#1841).

Part of epic #1808.

## Epic #1812 · [0.32] Epic: seeded-defect replication on the edition syntax with three conditions

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Re-run the nine seeded-defect pairs on the post-rewrite language under three conditions (conventional C#, C# equipped with executable specifications, and Calor) and publish the result with catches by channel and cost per condition. If the maintainer declines the spend, record the decline. The 0.32 exit criterion reads: "Seeded-defect replication with three conditions published on the post-rewrite commit, or the maintainer's decline recorded; if a collection is authorised but unpublished at release, the edition declaration is deferred (#1805) and this criterion is recorded as carried to 0.33".

#### Why

The project has one adjudicated result in which Calor beat C#. In `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/ws5-probe-001` (2026-07-28, model `claude-opus-4-8`, nine pairs each carrying one defect planted by the maintainer, two arms, meaning two conditions under test, three runs each, 54 of 54 runs valid) the Calor arm ended with 9 of 9 defects removed and the C# arm with 4 of 9 (`VERDICT.md:21-26` in that directory). The delta of 5 cleared a threshold of 3 fixed before the run and was adjudicated a hit (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/m5-compare-001/VERDICT.md:83-85`).

Three things limit what that result says.

- **The control had no executable specification.** The C# arm carried each property only as a doc comment (the same `VERDICT.md`, `:67-68`; for example `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/W5B-001-shipping-quote/csharp/Quote.cs:12-15`). The Calor arm carried it as a postcondition or an effect declaration that the toolchain enforces.
- **Three of the five deltas are by construction.** For the three contract pairs, fixture acceptance requires the agent-visible smoke suite to fail on the defective Calor starter and pass on the defective C# starter (`bench/phase0-agent-native/check-probe-pair.sh:19-23`, `:185-189`). The other two deltas are effect defects (W5A-003 and W5C-003); both arms caught the remaining four effect defects (the same `VERDICT.md`, `:40-50`).
- **It ran on the pre-0.30 syntax**, and the C# arm has not been run on these pairs since (`ls -d bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/*/W5*/csharp` lists only that epoch).

0.37 plans the fair version of this comparison at real scale (epic #1698, with the three conditions built by #1709), behind an external pilot that has no secured maintainer. The same question can be asked now on the nine pairs for a projected $59 to $94 at archived per-run costs (derivation in #1816). Either answer is useful. If Calor's lead survives an equipped C# arm, the July result is a language effect at fixture scale. If it does not, the July result was mostly "a specification was present", and 0.35 and the #1698 hypothesis are adjusted as #1816 writes in advance. The harness and fixtures built here are also the first use of what #1709, #1710 and #1711 need.

The run comes after the 0.32 rewrite, so that the first fair benefit test is not published on spellings the same milestone retires. It comes before the edition declaration (#1805), so that the edition is not declared on spellings no agent has run.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1815 | Harness for three conditions: arm definitions outside `pair.json` with per-arm project content, third-arm fixture acceptance, a three-arm epoch driver with a spend stop, and a catch-channel analyzer that works on 0.30 sources |
| #1813 | Equipped C# fixtures for the nine pairs, the list of what C# tooling cannot match, and a fairness review against a written rubric, labelled non-independent |
| #1816 | Decision: register and authorise the 0.32 paid runs (this replication and the #1645 re-run): thresholds, a consequence written in advance for every outcome, ceilings, abort triggers, decline branches |
| #1810 | Run and publish the replication on the post-rewrite commit, and apply the registered consequence |

#### Sequence

1. #1815, #1813 and #1816 are prepared while the breaking window (epic #1787) is open. None changes a spelling or what the compiler accepts. #1815 comes first, on the 0.31 harness work it extends (#1766, #1764), because the fixtures and the registration are written against its formats, and #1813 can start authoring once parts 1 and 2 of #1815 (arm definitions, third-arm acceptance) have merged; #1813 comes before #1816 because the registration names what the equipped arm can and cannot check.
2. #1816 has two parts, which may be signed on different dates. Part A (this replication) needs #1815 and #1813. Part B (the #1645 re-run) also needs the regressions #1605 filed and the two pairs #1789 authors.
3. No live agent run of the three-condition set happens before Part A is signed. A trial run would show results before the thresholds are fixed.
4. #1810 runs on the commit that #1807 posts after the rewrite (#1803), and publishes before #1805 decides the edition. Until Part A carries a decision line and that commit is posted, #1810 is held: it stays open and makes no paid run. If Part A is authorised and the result is unpublished when 0.32 is released, #1805 defers the edition declaration and the exit criterion is recorded as carried to 0.33.

#### Done when

- #1815 and #1813 are closed.
- The registration is merged as a new entry in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`, and the spend record `docs/plans/0.32-paid-run-registration.md` carries a dated decision line for each of its two parts (#1816).
- Either the replication is published on the post-rewrite commit (epoch directory with `VERDICT.md`, a section in the 0.32 release notes in `CHANGELOG.md`, a row in `docs/benchmarking/evidence-ledger.md`), or the decline is recorded in the spend record and #1810 is closed as not planned with that record linked. If the collection is authorised and unpublished at the 0.32 release, the criterion is recorded as carried to 0.33, #1805 has deferred the edition declaration, and this epic stays open until one of the two holds.
- The consequence registered for the outcome (outcome D if Part A was declined) is posted on #1662, on the epics #1861 and #1872, and on #1698.
- No pull request under #1815, #1813 or #1816 adds an epoch directory whose `pins.json` has `mode: "live"`.

#### Not in this epic
