### #1815 · [0.32] Harness for three conditions

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Depends on:** #1766, #1764 · **Blocks:** #1813, #1816, #1651, #1827, #1710 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Extend the pair harness so that one epoch can run three conditions on the nine seeded-defect pairs and report how each defect was caught. Script and schema file names, and paths beginning `pairs/` or `epochs/`, are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`; other paths are from the repository root. No paid agent run is part of this issue.

Terms. A *pair* is one task directory with a specification, a held-out test suite and a starting fixture per arm. An *arm* is one condition under test. An *epoch* is one complete collection: all arms, one pinned model. A *seeded-defect pair* (the nine `pairs/W5*` directories) ships a starter with one planted defect, a *probe test* that fails while the defect is present, and a *smoke suite* the agent can run. *Equipped C#* is a C# arm whose fixture also carries executable specifications: assertions in the code, analyzer packages in the project. `--null-agent` applies the reference solution instead of calling an agent, at zero API cost. A *catch channel* is how a defect first surfaced to the agent.

The work has four ordered parts. Each may be its own pull request; the issue closes when the done-when below holds. No existing file under `pairs/` or `epochs/` is modified, the rule #1766 set for the frozen originals.

1. **Arm definitions outside `pair.json`.** Let `--arm-config <key>` resolve a key that the pair's `pair.json` lacks from a JSON arm definition. Definitions live in the directory #1766 created for 0.30-only harness inputs. A definition that takes a starter, a reference or project content from outside the pair directory is honoured only under the source-selection option #1766 added (`--syntax 0.30` in that issue's example). A definition declares:
   - the key and the label written to results;
   - the language (`csharp` or `calor`), which selects the shims and the grader;
   - where each pair's starter and reference live: a per-pair directory beside the definition, or the pair's own `csharp` fixture;
   - the project file the harness writes as `Src.csproj`, and side files copied beside it (for example a banned-symbols list);
   - the arm's catch channels (part 4);
   - optionally, the product binding and prompt inputs of the arm, passed through to `run-pair.sh`: `--arm-repo-root`, `--calor-dll`, `--exemplar`, and whether the source-selection option is set. The pre-0.30 arm A that #1766 documents sets the first two and leaves the option off; Part B of #1816 runs it through the driver of part 3.

   Write it as a description of an arm, not as a patch to `pair.json`: #1827 extends the same document to arm types beyond the two languages. For a C# definition with a project file, `materialize()` (`run-pair.sh:729`) writes that file in place of the inline text and restores packages before the agent starts; the restore must work under the isolation #1762 added. `result.json` records the SHA-256 of the project and side files at the start and at declared-done.
2. **Third-arm acceptance.** `check-probe-pair.sh` accepts extra arm keys. For each one it checks that the reference builds and passes the held-out suite, the probe and the smoke suite, and that the starter is in the state a per-pair overlay file declares for that arm. The overlay files are kept with the arm definitions, outside `pairs/`. The states are `build-blocked` with a named diagnostic code, `smoke-fails` with a named exception type, and `unmatched` (builds, smoke passes, probe fails). The same per-pair file names the seeded function by name (`QuoteWithSurcharge`, not `f003`); commit it for all nine pairs, taking each name from the `description` in the pair's `defect.json`. The existing two-arm checks do not change.
3. **Epoch driver.** A new script, or an extension of `run-epoch.sh` if #1766 gave that script an interleaving arm list, that takes an epoch id, a pair list, an ordered list of arm keys and a run count. It interleaves one run per arm per pair, runs `--canary-only` for each Calor arm first, and refuses a live run without an explicit confirmation flag. A per-arm switch turns build-time verification on for a Calor arm by setting `CALOR_P0_VERIFY_EXPECTED=1` and `CALOR_P0_VERIFY_GATE=1` for that arm's runs, as `run-guarantees-epoch.sh:173-176` does. It writes `pins.json` with the fields the existing drivers write (`epochId`, `mode`, which is `"live"` for a paid epoch, `startedAt`, `modelPin`, `agentVersion`, `runsPerArm`, the product commit) and, per arm, the key, label, verification setting and project hash. Between runs it stops when the summed `total_cost_usd` of the finished runs' `agent.json` files reaches `--spend-ceiling`, or when one run exceeds `--run-cost-limit`. It supports `--null-agent`.
4. **Catch-channel analyzer.** A new script; do not edit `guarantees-analyze.py`, which implements instruments frozen in Annex A-1.3. It reads an epoch with any number of arms and reports per defect and arm:
   - caught or not, by majority over the runs `pins.json` records, an invalid or absent slot counting as not caught;
   - the earliest channel in the registered order: `build-proof`, `build-block`, `runtime-guard`, `caught-unattributed` (the agent noticed), `missed`. The per-defect channel is the plurality over runs, and a tie resolves to the later channel, as `guarantees-analyze.py:188-200` does;
   - mean iterations and summed cost per arm.

   Channel evidence comes from the arm definition: diagnostic codes on the seeded function for the two build channels (for Calor, `Calor0711` and `Calor0712`, then `Calor0410`), and an exception type name in the agent-visible transcript (`agent.json`) for the runtime channel.

   A journal entry cannot place a diagnostic on a function in 0.30 sources (see Baseline). If the journal as #1764 left it already records a location on each entry, use it and change no schema. Otherwise add what is missing to each entry of `diagnostics` and of the `build_diagnostics` array #1764 adds, which is where a C# analyzer's diagnostic appears. Either record the enclosing function's name, resolved at capture time from the diagnostic's file and line and the source copy that was compiled, or record the file and line and resolve them in the analyzer. Bump the schema discriminator from the one #1764 left, keep `validate-telemetry.sh` accepting archived records, and document the field in `loop-telemetry-schema.md`.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `run-pair.sh:282` accepts `--arm calor` or `--arm csharp` only. `--arm-config <key>` (`:196`, `:212`) is resolved against `arms[key]` of the pair's own `pair.json` (`:382`), and `harness-capture.py:790-797` rejects a key that file lacks. All nine `pairs/W5*/pair.json` files define exactly `calor` and `csharp`.
- For every C# arm, `materialize()` overwrites `Src.csproj` with an inline project (`run-pair.sh:767-777`): nullable reference types and `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild`, no package reference, no analyzer configuration. The prompt for seeded-defect pairs tells the agent not to modify the project file (`:1255`).
- Shims are chosen by language, not by arm key (`:782`, `:816`, `:866`), so a second C# configuration can use `TestShim.csharp.cs` and `SmokeShim.csharp.cs`.
- `check-probe-pair.sh` checks two arms. It reads the Calor starter from `<pair>/calor` (`:141`) and the C# starter from `<pair>/csharp` (`:180`), loops `for arm in calor csharp` over the references (`:202`), and writes its own copy of the inline C# project (`:59-68`). It requires the C# starter to build and to pass the smoke suite (`:181-189`).
- `run-epoch.sh` passes `--arm` only, runs every run of one arm before the next arm (`:58-66`) and has no spend stop. `run-pair.sh` passes a budget to the agent client only on the PP-W ticket path (`:1368-1376`) and calls its timeout "NOT a provider spending guarantee" (`:1308`). Interleaving exists in `run-m5-epoch.sh:246-256`, and a confirmation flag for paid epochs in `run-ppe1-epoch.sh:136`.
- `guarantees-analyze.py` is the only script that attributes a catch to a channel. It reads exactly two arm labels from `pins.json` (`:55-56`) and finds the seeded declaration by the retired markers `§F{<id>:` and `§S ` (`:81-87`, `:226`), using `declarationId` from `defect.json` (`f003` or `f005`). Its header says the frozen instruments are "implemented here, never changed here" (`:6`). The channel order is registered at `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:221-240`.
- The 0.30 copies of the starters carry no ids (`tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/W5B-001-shipping-quote/calor/Quote.calr:11-13`). In 0.30, declaration ids are kept in a `.calor/ids.json` sidecar (`src/Calor.Compiler/Ids/IdEntry.cs:69`). An id is a kind prefix and 12 random characters, minted when a compile first registers a file (`IdEntry.cs:839`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Ids/CompactIdGenerator.cs`; the CLI registers its inputs at `src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:498`), and a diagnostic's `declarationId` resolves only to declarations with a persisted id (`src/Calor.Compiler/Ids/DeclarationIdResolver.cs:24-27`). The harness takes journal diagnostics from a compile of fresh copies of the sources (`run-pair.sh:1121-1131`). Read from the code, not verified by execution: a `declarationId` in a 0.30 journal entry is either absent or minted for that copy, so it cannot equal the `f003` or `f005` in `defect.json`.
- A journal diagnostic entry may hold `code` and `declarationId` and nothing else (`loop-telemetry.schema.json:28-40`, `additionalProperties: false` at `:34`), and `telemetry-helpers.py:129-132` copies only those two keys. The compiler's JSON diagnostic also carries a message and a location with file and line (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/DiagnosticEnvelope.cs:14`, `:19`, `:34-40`).
- At HEAD the compile that fills `diagnostics` runs only when the arm label is exactly `calor` (`run-pair.sh:1025`, `:1119`; the PP-W policy path is the one other case), and no build diagnostic is recorded for a C# arm. #1764 gates that compile on the arm language, adds fix flags to each entry, and adds a `build_diagnostics` array, parsed from the rebuild log, for both languages.
- Not verified by execution, because nothing was built or run for this baseline: that a null run journals `Calor0410` for the six effect starters, and that package restore for a C# project with analyzer references works under the #1762 isolation. The null epoch in Done when is the check for both.
- No file under `bench/phase0-agent-native/tests/` mentions `check-probe-pair.sh` or `guarantees-analyze.py` (`grep -rl`). CI runs `python3 -m unittest discover -s bench/phase0-agent-native/tests` in a job that builds no compiler (`.github/workflows/test.yml:124`).

#### Why

The replication (#1810) needs a third condition, C# with executable specifications, and a table of catches by channel. Neither can be expressed today. A second C# configuration needs its own project content, the arm key cannot be added without editing files that #1766 keeps frozen, the fixture check knows two arms, no driver can interleave three arms or stop at a spend ceiling, and the only channel analyzer reads retired markers and two Calor arms.

This is new capacity and displaces no existing issue. It brings forward work that #1710 needs in 0.37 (three conditions in one epoch), gives #1827 a working arm definition to generalise, and gives the #1645 re-run a driver with a spend stop.

#### Done when

- `python3 -m unittest discover -s bench/phase0-agent-native/tests` passes in CI with new tests, none skipped for lack of a compiler, that cover:
  - a key resolved from a definition; an unknown key refused; a definition with fixtures outside the pair directory refused when the source-selection option is off;
  - the driver with a stub in place of `run-pair.sh` that writes `agent.json` files with fixed costs: it starts no run after the summed cost reaches `--spend-ceiling`, stops after a run above `--run-cost-limit`, refuses a live run without the confirmation flag, writes the `pins.json` fields listed in part 3, and passes a definition's product binding to the stub as those `run-pair.sh` options;
  - the analyzer on fixture epochs with three arms, covering each of the five channels, an invalid slot and a tied vote;
  - if a journal field was added, `validate-telemetry.sh` accepting a record with it and an archived record without it.
- A test arm definition is committed under `bench/phase0-agent-native/tests/fixtures/`: a second C# configuration whose project file differs from the inline one and whose starters are the pairs' own `csharp` fixtures.
- Run locally, because no CI job runs the harness against a built compiler, with the command lines and the machine stated in the pull request: the driver with `--null-agent` over the nine pairs for `csharp`, `calor` and the test arm exits 0. Its `pins.json` and the analyzer's output are committed under a new `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/<name>-null/` directory. Every `result.json` has `taskSuccess: true` and `defect.caught: true`. The analyzer lists 27 cells: the Calor cells of the six `W5A` and `W5C` pairs read `build-block` (a null run builds the defective starter once before applying the reference, `run-pair.sh:1271-1275`), and the other 21 read `caught-unattributed`. This is the end-to-end proof that a diagnostic is placed on the seeded function in 0.30 sources.
- Also stated in the pull request, with the output pasted: `check-probe-pair.sh` with the test arm key exits 0 on the nine pairs, and its output without extra keys is unchanged.
- `git diff --diff-filter=MD --name-only <base> -- bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs bench/phase0-agent-native/guarantees-analyze.py` prints nothing.
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/README.md` documents arm definitions, the three starter states, the driver and the analyzer. If a journal field was added, `loop-telemetry-schema.md` and `loop-telemetry.schema.json` carry it.
- If the journal field needs a new field in the compiler's JSON diagnostic, the pull request adds xUnit cases for it and raises `expectedTotal` for that test project in `eng/test-manifest.json` by the number added. Otherwise no test project's count changes.

#### Not in scope

- The equipped C# fixtures and their fairness review: #1813.
- Thresholds, run counts, ceilings and readings: #1816.
- Arm types other than `calor` and `csharp`, and porting those two to definitions: #1827.
- The PP-W paths of `run-pair.sh` and their registration files.

Part of epic #1812. Depends on #1766, #1764. Blocks #1813, #1816, #1827, #1710.

### #1813 · [0.32] Equipped C# fixtures for the nine seeded-defect pairs, with a fairness review

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Depends on:** #1815 · **Blocks:** #1816, #1709 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Author the equipped C# condition for the nine seeded-defect pairs under `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/W5*`, record what C# tooling cannot match, and have the fairness of the three conditions reviewed against a written rubric.

Terms. A *seeded-defect pair* is one task whose starting code carries one planted defect, with a *probe test* that fails while the defect is present and a *smoke suite* the agent can run. *Equipped C#* is a C# condition whose fixture carries executable specifications: assertions in the code and analyzer packages in the project. An *arm definition* is the JSON document #1815 introduced, which gives an arm its project file and fixture locations without editing `pair.json`.

No paid agent run is part of this issue. No agent is run on these fixtures through the harness before Part A of #1816 is signed: a trial run would show results before the thresholds are fixed. Validation is by the acceptance script and `--null-agent` (the reference solution applied in place of an agent).

1. **Arm definition `csharp-equipped`.** Its project file is the conventional arm's project plus what the fixtures need: analyzer package references at exact versions and their configuration files.
2. **A starter and a reference per pair**, in the location #1815 reads them from. Each begins as a copy of the pair's `csharp` and `reference/csharp` file and adds only the specification, identically in both:
   - *W5-B, three pairs (a violated scalar postcondition).* The documented bound as an executable check on every return of the seeded function, raising a named exception when violated. State the Calor postcondition and nothing more; for W5B-001 that is `result <= cap`.
   - *W5-A, three pairs (a file write in a function declared pure), and W5-C, three pairs (a write reached through a call chain from a function declared read-only).* A publicly available analyzer that can state "this function writes no file" (W5-A) or "writes none through its callees" (W5-C), where one exists. Where none exists the pair is recorded as unmatched and its equipped fixture is the conventional one.
3. **Per-pair overlay** (#1815 format): the expected starter state for this arm, which is `build-blocked` with the diagnostic code, `smoke-fails` with the exception type, or `unmatched`.
4. **Record** at `docs/plans/0.32-equipped-csharp-fixtures.md`:
   - one row per pair: the property; the Calor mechanism and its channel; the C# mechanism (tool, version, rule id) and its channel; lines and files added; and matched, partly matched or unmatched with the reason;
   - the rubric of step 5 and the review of step 6.
5. **Rubric**, written before the review. At least these questions per pair:
   - Same property: does the equipped specification state what the Calor starter declares, no weaker and no stronger?
   - No answer leak: does it avoid naming the defect, the defective line and the triggering input? A rule that bans the one call the defect makes while the same capability stays legal elsewhere in the file fails this question.
   - Normal loop: does it surface through the build or the provided smoke suite, as the Calor one does?
   - Practical tooling: is it a public package at a pinned version, or code a C# team would write by hand? An analyzer written for this benchmark fails.
   - Same task: are `spec.md`, the held-out suite, the probe and the smoke suite the same files the other two conditions receive, and does the equipped reference pass all three suites?
   - Is the conventional C# fixture byte-identical to the July one?
6. **Review.** A separate agent session receives the rubric, the nine pair directories, the fixtures of all three conditions and the table. It does not receive the roadmap, the other issues of this epic, the July verdict or any expected outcome. It answers every question for every pair. Each finding is fixed, or kept with a written reason. The first paragraph of the record labels the review non-independent: the same maintainer and agents authored all three conditions.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

Script names, and paths beginning `pairs/` or `epochs/`, are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`; other paths are from the repository root.

- The nine pairs are three of each class (`pairs/W5*/defect.json`, field `class`): W5-A, W5-B, W5-C.
- Each C# starter states its property only in a doc comment: `pairs/W5B-001-shipping-quote/csharp/Quote.cs:12-15` ("the returned quote never exceeds" the cap), `pairs/W5A-001-report-audit/csharp/Report.cs:16` ("Pure: no side effects"), `pairs/W5C-001-catalog-cache/csharp/Catalog.cs:36` ("Read-only").
- The 0.30 Calor starter for W5B-001 declares `=> (result <= cap)` (`tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/W5B-001-shipping-quote/calor/Quote.calr:13`). The effect starters declare an empty or read-only effect row on the seeded function.
- Each W5-B smoke suite calls the seeded function on an input inside the defect window and asserts only a range that holds either way (`pairs/W5B-001-shipping-quote/smoke/QuoteSmokeTests.cs:31-36`; the same shape at `:35` of the other two). A check that throws on a violated bound would therefore fail that smoke test on the defective starter. This is an inference; nothing was run.
- All six effect-class C# starters contain an honest file write in the same class as the defect. In W5A-001 the defect calls `File.AppendAllText` (`Report.cs:21`) and the honest writer `File.WriteAllText` (`:29`). In W5A-003 both call `File.WriteAllText` (`pairs/W5A-003-config-echo/csharp/Config.cs:21`, `:29`). A project-wide banned symbol cannot say "this one function is pure".
- Not verified: whether a public analyzer exists that can state purity for one function, or the absence of a write through its callees. If none does, six of the nine pairs are unmatched and the equipped arm differs from conventional C# only on the three W5-B pairs; the record then says so.
- All nine `spec.md` files say "Do not add dependencies", and the harness prompt forbids changing the project file (`run-pair.sh:1255`). Any analyzer therefore arrives through the harness-written project, which is what #1815 provides.
- `check-probe-pair.sh:181-189` requires the conventional C# starter to build and pass smoke. An equipped starter that is matched will fail one of the two by design, which is why #1815 adds declared starter states.
- In July the C# arm missed W5A-003, W5B-001, W5B-002, W5B-003 and W5C-003 (`epochs/ws5-probe-001/VERDICT.md:40-50`).
- The C# arm's registered toolkit already permits analyzers and asserts written by the agent (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:27`); no fixture supplied one.
- #1708 says: "Independence means separate ownership and withheld outcomes, not merely a different agent session."

#### Why

The July C# arm was conventional C#: the property was prose. The question this epic asks is how much of Calor's lead remains when the C# side has the same property in executable form, so the equipped fixtures are the experiment's control, and a control that is too weak or that leaks the answer makes the result uninterpretable in either direction. The rubric and the unmatched list are how a reader can judge that. The review is by a second agent session because no independent reviewer exists yet (the seat is sought under #1777, and #1857 holds the independent review in 0.37), and it is labelled accordingly.

This is new capacity and displaces no existing issue. #1709 reuses the fixture pattern and the unmatched list in 0.37.

#### Done when

- `check-probe-pair.sh` with the source-selection option and `csharp-equipped` exits 0 on all nine pairs. The output is pasted in the pull request, with the machine it ran on, because no CI job runs the harness against a built compiler.
- `run-pair.sh --null-agent --arm csharp --arm-config csharp-equipped` with the source-selection option gives `taskSuccess: true` and `defect.caught: true` for each of the nine pairs.
- `docs/plans/0.32-equipped-csharp-fixtures.md` exists with nine table rows and no empty cell, the count of matched, partly matched and unmatched pairs, the rubric, the review's answer to every question for every pair, a disposition for every finding, and the non-independence label in its first paragraph.
- Every analyzer package in the arm definition has an exact version.
- `git diff --diff-filter=MD --name-only <base> -- bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs` prints nothing.
- The pull request adds no epoch directory with a `csharp-equipped` run whose `pins.json` has `mode: "live"`.

#### Not in scope

- Thresholds, run counts and readings: #1816. The paid run: #1810.
- An independent fairness review: #1857 and #1709.
- New pairs, or any change to the nine pairs' specifications, tests or conventional fixtures.
- Writing a custom analyzer. If none exists for a property, the pair is unmatched and the record says so.

Part of epic #1812. Depends on #1815. Blocks #1816, #1709.

### #1816 · [0.32] Decision: register and authorise the 0.32 paid runs — the three-condition replication and the #1645 re-run

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Depends on:** #1815, #1813, #1789, #1605 · **Blocks:** #1645, #1805, #1810, #1662, #1866, #1869, #1871, #1870 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Take and record one maintainer decision in two parts: whether to run the three-condition seeded-defect replication (Part A) and the #1645 re-run (Part B), and on what terms fixed in advance. It produces two documents:

- **The registration**: a new entry in section A.3 of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` (the next free `A-1.N`, with the `Annex version` pointer updated) and, for Part A, a new row in section A.2 under an unused `PP-<LETTERS><DIGITS>` key. The entry holds, for both parts, the design fields, the readings, the four outcome paragraphs and the limits written below. It is the copy other issues read, and CI holds it append-only: once merged it is not edited, and a later change is a new sub-entry. The July row `PP-W1` is not edited. The pull request must not touch `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py` or its workflow, which the guard refuses in the same pull request as an annex change.
- **The spend record**: `docs/plans/0.32-paid-run-registration.md`, modelled on `docs/plans/pp-w5-spend-authorisation.md`. For each part it holds the option chosen, the projection with the command that produced it, the ceiling, the abort triggers and the decision line, and later the pinned commits and the realised spend. It cites the registration by its entry number and restates no threshold or reading.

Filing or closing this issue authorises no spend. Only a dated decision line in the spend record does. Values marked "recommended" are the roadmap's proposal: the maintainer confirms or replaces each one before the registration is merged, and no threshold, reading or run count changes after the first paid run. The parts may be registered and signed on different dates. Part B cannot be completed before #1605 has filed its regressions and #1789 has merged its two pairs. If Part A is declined before its registration is written, the entry records the decline with outcome D's paragraphs and adds no A.2 row.

Terms. An *arm* is one condition under test. An *epoch* is one complete collection: all arms, one pinned model, runs interleaved. A *seeded-defect pair* is a task whose starting code carries one planted defect. A defect is *caught* when it is absent at the end of a run, as judged by a held-out probe test. *Equipped C#* is C# whose fixture carries executable specifications (#1813).

**Part A: the replication (#1810)**

| Field | Recommended |
|---|---|
| Pairs | The nine `W5*` pairs, in the copies #1807 validates |
| Arms | `csharp` (the July fixtures, unchanged), `csharp-equipped` (#1813), `calor` |
| Calor configuration | Effects enforced, contract mode `debug`, Z3 required, and build-time verification on. Verification is an opt-in product setting, and the registration says so |
| Commit | The Calor product at the commit #1807 posts, recorded in `pins.json` |
| Model | One model id, written verbatim; the one #1605 pinned, or, if #1605 made no paid run, one named here |
| Runs | 5 per arm per pair, interleaved: 135 runs |
| Caught | As registered in July: per-defect majority over the runs; a slot still invalid after two retries counts as not caught; a run that modified the smoke suite is invalid |
| Primary measure | *d* = defects caught by `calor` minus defects caught by `csharp-equipped`, out of 9 |
| Also reported | `calor` minus `csharp` (the July comparison); `csharp-equipped` minus `csharp`; each split by defect class and by matched or unmatched pair; the earliest catch channel per defect per arm; iterations and cost per arm |

Options:

| Option | Runs | Projected | Ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 (recommended): 5 runs per arm per pair | 135 | $59 to $94 | $400 |
| A2: 3 runs per arm per pair | 81 | $35 to $57 | $250 |
| A3: decline | 0 | $0 | none; outcome D below applies |

A1 is recommended over A2 because a two-of-three majority turns on one run, and two C# defects in July were decided that way (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/ws5-probe-001/VERDICT.md:31-36`). The run count must be odd. Each ceiling is about four times the upper projection, the largest overrun this program has recorded, and it is cumulative: retries and any void attempt count against it.

One field has a second real alternative: verification off. That repeats July, where contract defects surface only at run time, in the same channel as a C# assertion, and the build-time-proof column stays empty. On is recommended because the question behind the "specifications were present" reading is what build-time proof adds.

Readings, fixed before any run:

- *d* ≥ 3: **language effect** at fixture scale.
- *d* ≤ 2: **specifications were present**. Most of the July lead is reproduced by giving C# the same property in executable form. A negative *d* is reported as prominently as a positive one.
- **Not adjudicated**: in any arm, fewer than 7 of the 9 defects have at least 4 of 5 runs in agreement (under A2, all 3 runs, as in July); or an abort trigger ended the collection early.
- Beside the primary reading: if `calor` minus `csharp` is below 3, "the July hit did not replicate on the post-rewrite language and this model". If `calor` catches fewer than 9, a regression issue is filed before #1805 decides.
- Expected outcome, stated in advance as an inference: the equipped arm reproduces the three contract catches, most effect pairs are unmatched, and *d* is 2. The threshold is therefore close to decided by the fixtures, so every publication gives the split by defect class and by matched or unmatched pair before *d*.

Consequence of each outcome (recommended text; the registration adopts or amends it before it is merged):

**L, language effect.**
Rows kept: every row of #1662, #1861 and #1872, as planned.
What changes in the #1698 hypothesis: nothing in its wording. Its starting record gains "the lead held against C# with executable specifications, at fixture scale, non-independent". 0.37 tests whether it holds on tasks the maintainer did not write.

**S, specifications were present.**
Rows kept: every row of #1662, and the 0.35 exit report counts only Proven obligations as a difference from equipped C#. Every row of #1861 except #1866, which is held, not closed, until a later dated line in the spend record lifts the hold: a lifted guard that is not Proven is a run-time check, which under this reading equipped C# matches. #1872 is unchanged: its gate is the #1605 base rate and this run measures none of its defect classes.
What changes in the #1698 hypothesis: it narrows. A higher catch rate on defects that an assertion can state is no longer expected. What remains to test against equipped C# is (a) properties C# tooling could not state in this run, by class; (b) how early a defect surfaces, at build or at run time; (c) what the specifications cost to write and keep. #1708's targets and the sizing (#1786) are refreshed for the smaller expected difference before #1867 is decided.

**N, not adjudicated.**
Rows kept: every row, as planned.
What changes in the #1698 hypothesis: nothing. Its entry criterion 3 records "replication not adjudicated" with the reason. A second collection needs a new decision line.

**D, declined.**
Rows kept: every row, as planned.
What changes in the #1698 hypothesis: nothing. Its entry criterion 3 records "no equipped-C# reading exists at fixture scale". #1810 is closed as not planned, and the 0.32 exit criterion is met by the "Declined" line in the spend record.

Limits the registration and every publication state: fixture scale (nine small modules); seeded by the maintainer, so detection and not incidence; non-independent (one maintainer and the same agents wrote all three conditions and the fairness review); the Calor arm was at the ceiling of 9 of 9 in July, so this run can show the lead holding or shrinking and cannot show it growing; for the contract pairs the defect surfaces by construction wherever a check exists, so the run measures what the agent does next.

**Part B: the #1645 re-run**

| Field | Recommended |
|---|---|
| Pairs | Each pair #1605 filed as regressed (at most three; if more, the three with the largest registered regression), plus the two #1789 pairs. If #1605 filed none (including under its reduced design, or with no paid run), Part B is the two #1789 pairs only |
| Arms | Regressed pairs: `calor` at the pinned commit, `csharp`, and arm A (the pinned pre-0.30 compiler) if #1770 registered one. #1789 pairs: `calor` and `csharp` |
| Runs | 10 per arm per pair for the regressed pairs; 15 per arm per pair for the two #1789 pairs (30 Calor-arm runs, the floor #1770 item 8 registers for a base-rate reading); interleaved |
| Model and commit | As Part A |
| Measures | Per pair, iterations-to-green and cost per run as ratios to the `csharp` arm of this epoch; the regression rule #1770 registered for its before/after comparison, applied inside this epoch. Where both penalised pairs (`N1-003-csv-row`, `W3-003-kv-journal`) are in the set, the close rule of #1770 item 6 is applied to this epoch's `calor` and `csharp` arms and reported as Closed, Open or Not resolved; otherwise the report says "close rule not re-read" |
| Readings | The three #1645 proposes for a regressed pair. *Recovered*: the regression rule #1770 registered no longer fires when applied to this epoch's own arms; the #1645 triage row closes as fixed and confirmed. *Not recovered*: it fires; the row closes as not recovered with the numbers, the 0.32 release notes list the construct as a known regression, and a follow-up issue is filed and named to #1805 as a candidate exception. *Too few valid runs to decide*: after retries an arm of the pair has fewer than 8 valid runs of 10 (recommended floor; if #1770 registered a validity floor, that one applies); the row reads "fixed, not confirmed". For the #1789 pairs the ratios to C# are reported with no threshold, and the base-rate rule of #1770 item 8 is applied unchanged to this epoch's Calor arm: each of null, missing case and resource leak reads above threshold, below threshold, held or not measurable. #1869, #1871 and #1842 read that line |
| Ceiling | **$600** |
| Decline | #1645 closes on its fixes and the release notes say "fixed, not re-measured" |

The projection is $414: three regressed pairs at 10 × ($4.20 + $4.20 + $0.60) each, plus two #1789 pairs at 15 × ($4.20 + $0.60) each. It already prices every Calor run at the dearest July mean, the one measured without a primer, so the ceiling is set at about 1.45 times the projection and not at four times. No cost exists for a converted-module pair, which is the main uncertainty. No figure from this epoch is compared with a #1605 figure.

**Rules for both parts**

- Zero-cost pass first, on the pinned commit: `--null-agent` green for every arm and pair, and the arm canary (`run-pair.sh --canary-only`) green for every Calor arm, with the verification canary where verification is registered on. For Part A, the three-arm fixture acceptance (`check-probe-pair.sh` with the `csharp-equipped` key) as well. A collection does not start on a failure here.
- Shakedown: the first interleaved round is inspected for validity fields only (invalid, smoke modified, canary, project hash, transcript present), not for catches. A harness defect voids the round, which still counts against the ceiling.
- Abort triggers, each ending the collection with what exists reported as incomplete: the ceiling is reached; one run costs more than $4 in Part A or $16 in Part B (twice the dearest archived run of the same kind, $1.99 and $7.97); a transcript reports a model other than the pin; two consecutive slots in one arm stay invalid after retries; an arm canary fails; an isolation violation under #1762. The #1815 driver enforces the first two (`--spend-ceiling`, `--run-cost-limit`). The operator checks the others after each interleaved round, and the spend record says so.
- Before signing, the projections and the per-run limits are recomputed from #1605's realised per-run costs on 0.30. If #1605 made no paid run, the July means stand and the spend record says so. If an upper projection exceeds its ceiling, runs are cut; if that takes the #1789 pairs below 30 Calor-arm runs, the registration states that their base-rate reading will be not measurable. A ceiling is raised only by a new decision line.
- Decision line per part: `Authorised: <date>, Part <A|B>, <option or run count>, ceiling <amount>` or `Declined: <date>, Part <A|B>, <reason>`.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The July registration is the A.2 row `PP-W1` (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:323`): two arms, exactly 3 runs, hit at a delta of at least 3 of 9, not adjudicated unless at least 7 of 9 defects are unanimous in both arms, and "Measures detection capability, not organic incidence". "Caught" is defined at `:186-203`, and the channel order at `:221-240`.
- Annex A is held append-only by `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py`, run from the base branch by the `annex-freeze` job (`.github/workflows/experiment-registry-tamper-check.yml:98`). Existing A.2 rows and A.3 entries must stay byte-identical, and every annex change needs a new A.3 entry and a matching version pointer, which reads A-1.12 today (`agent-native-gates.md:102`). An A.2 row key must have the form `PP-<LETTERS><DIGITS>`; the `PP-W` keys in use are `PP-W1`, `PP-W2`, `PP-W5` and `PP-W6` (`grep -oE 'PP-W[0-9]+'` over the file; the newest row is at `:328`). The guard refuses a pull request that changes the annex together with the guard or its workflow (`.github/workflows/experiment-registry-tamper-check.yml:139-143`). The precedent keeps registration and spend apart: the A-1.3 entry says "Spend is NOT authorized by this registration" (`:1694`).
- Per-run cost on these nine pairs, pre-0.30 syntax, `claude-opus-4-8`, measured by summing `total_cost_usd` over `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/<epoch>/*/*/run-*/agent.json`: `ws5-probe-001`, Calor $0.80 and C# $0.25 (27 runs each, $28.48 in total, dearest $1.39); `guarantees-probe-001`, two Calor arms at $0.92 and $0.84 (45 runs each, $79.39, dearest $1.99). The "≈ $7.93" at `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/ws5-probe-001/VERDICT.md:74` was priced by hand from token counts; the projections here use the summed `total_cost_usd`.
- Projection for 135 runs: 45 × ($0.25 + *e* + $0.80 to $0.92), where *e*, the unknown cost of an equipped C# run, lies between the C# and the Calor mean. That is $59 to $94; for 81 runs, $35 to $57.
- Recorded overruns: $92.83 realised against a $15 to $89 range (`docs/plans/pp-w5-spend-authorisation.md:18`, `:86`), and $79.39 against an estimate under $20 (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/guarantees-probe-001/VERDICT.md:20-23`). The gates document's epoch ceiling is $1,500, provisional (`agent-native-gates.md:81`).
- July means for Part B, pre-0.30 syntax, unpinned model, measured the same way over `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution` (180 runs, $370.52, dearest $7.97; `modelPin: "default"` in its `pins.json`): C# $0.50 and $0.60; Calor $3.65 and $4.20 without an exemplar, $1.17 and $2.23 with one.
- Build-time verification is off unless `CalorVerify` is set (`src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:103`; `src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:227`). With it on, a refuted postcondition is a build warning and the build still succeeds (`CompileCalor.cs:222-223`). The harness arms it per arm through `CALOR_P0_VERIFY_GATE` and proves it with a canary (`bench/phase0-agent-native/templates/calor-arm/CalorArm.csproj.template:36`; `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:677-709`). July ran with it off, and the contract defects surfaced as a run-time exception (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/ws5-probe-001/VERDICT.md:64-68`). With it on, in `guarantees-probe-001`, they surfaced as build-time refutations (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/guarantees-probe-001/VERDICT.md:32-39`).
- Comparisons are valid only between arms run together on one pinned model in one epoch (`agent-native-gates.md:12`). Invalid runs are retried at most twice (`:13`; `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:58`).
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh` passes a budget to the agent client only on the PP-W ticket path (`:1368-1376`), and `run-epoch.sh` has no spend stop. The driver from #1815 supplies the stop.

#### Why

The plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`) requires every paid run to have a ceiling, abort triggers, a zero-cost pass, one pinned model and a reading written in advance, and requires spend authorisation to be a maintainer decision with its options written out. 0.32 has two paid collections and neither has such a record: the seeded-defect comparison was registered in July for two arms only, and the re-run step of #1645 had no spend clause at all.

The consequences are written here, before the run, because the scope of 0.35 and the hypothesis of #1698 both depend on whether the lead survives an equipped C# arm, and nothing else says how. A consequence decided after the number is known is a threshold moved after the fact. The 0.35 entry criterion (on #1662) and entry criterion 3 of #1698 both read this registration.

#### Done when

- The registration is merged as a new A.3 entry of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` (two entries if the parts are registered separately), in a pull request on which the `annex-freeze` job passed and which changed no existing row or entry. It holds every field of Parts A and B with nothing still marked "recommended" (for Part B, including the base-rate reading for the #1789 pairs and the run count behind it), the four outcome paragraphs (L, S, N, D) each with "Rows kept" and "What changes in the #1698 hypothesis", and the limits paragraph. Unless Part A was declined first, an A.2 row for Part A exists under a new key.
- `docs/plans/0.32-paid-run-registration.md` exists, cites the registration by entry number, and holds for each part the option chosen, the recomputed projection with the command that produced it, the ceiling, the abort triggers with who checks each, and a dated decision line from the maintainer.
- No epoch directory in the repository holds a `csharp-equipped` run under a `pins.json` with `mode: "live"` and a `startedAt` earlier than the date of the Part A line.
- #1810, #1645, #1805, #1662 and #1698 each carry a comment that links the registration and the spend record.
- If Part A is declined: #1810 is closed as not planned with the spend record linked, and outcome D's two paragraphs are posted on #1662, on the epics #1861 and #1872, and on #1698. If Part B is declined: the decline is linked on #1645, which then closes on its fixes.

#### Not in scope

- Running either collection: #1810 and #1645.
- The #1605 registration (#1770) and the 0.33 and 0.37 registrations (#1832, #1867).
- Changing any 0.35 row now. The registration states what happens for each outcome; #1810 posts it and the owning epics apply it at their entry.

Part of epic #1812. Depends on #1815, #1813, #1789, #1605. Blocks #1810, #1645, #1805.

### #1810 · [0.32] Run and publish the replication

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, documentation · **Depends on:** #1816, #1807 · **Blocks:** #1805, #1642, #1698 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Run the three-condition replication and publish whatever results. #1816 leaves two documents that govern it: the *registration*, an entry in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` with the design, the readings and the consequence of each outcome; and the *spend record*, `docs/plans/0.32-paid-run-registration.md`, with the ceiling, the abort triggers and the maintainer's decision line for Part A. Filing this issue does not authorise spend, and this issue changes nothing in either document. As #1816 proposes them:

- spend: at most $400 for 135 runs (three arms, nine pairs, five runs each), counting retries and voided rounds;
- one pinned model, and a zero-cost pass before any paid run;
- abort triggers: the ceiling is reached; one run costs more than $4; a transcript reports a model other than the pin; two consecutive slots in one arm stay invalid after retries; an arm canary fails; an isolation violation is logged;
- readings fixed in advance, with *d* the number of defects caught by Calor minus the number caught by equipped C#, out of 9: *d* of 3 or more is a language effect at fixture scale; *d* of 2 or less means "specifications were present"; the result is not adjudicated if runs disagree on too many defects or a trigger ended the collection early.

Where the merged documents differ from this list, they govern. Script names, and paths beginning `pairs/` or `epochs/`, are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

Terms. The three conditions (*arms*) are conventional C#, C# equipped with executable specifications (#1813), and Calor. The nine *seeded-defect pairs* (`pairs/W5*`) each start from code with one planted defect. `--null-agent` applies the reference solution in place of an agent, at zero API cost. A *catch channel* is how a defect first surfaced to the agent: a build-time proof, a build block, a run-time guard, or none (the agent noticed).

**Gate.** Its values come from the Part A decision line in the spend record and from the post-rewrite commit that #1807 posts on this issue.

- Open: Part A carries "Authorised" and the commit is posted.
- Closed: Part A carries "Declined". Close this issue as not planned and link the spend record; the 0.32 exit criterion is then met by the decline.
- Held: Part A has no decision line, or no commit is posted. The issue stays open and no paid run is made.
- Carried: Part A is authorised and the result is unpublished when 0.32 is released. #1805 defers the edition declaration, the exit criterion is recorded as carried to 0.33, and this issue stays open and finishes under the same registration.

Steps when the gate is open:

1. **Pin.** Bind the Calor product to the commit #1807 posted. Before any paid run, append to the spend record that commit and the commit of the driver and the analyzer (#1815). Neither script changes after the first paid run.
2. **Zero-cost pass** on the pinned commit, archived under `epochs/<epoch-id>-null/`:
   - `check-probe-pair.sh` with the `csharp-equipped` key exits 0 on the nine pairs;
   - the driver with `--null-agent` passes for all three arms;
   - the Calor arm canary (`run-pair.sh --canary-only`) passes, and so does the verification canary if Part A registered verification on;
   - the analyzer runs on the null epoch.
3. **Paid collection** with the registered arms, runs and model, under the authorised ceiling and triggers, through the driver with its `--spend-ceiling` and `--run-cost-limit` set to the authorised values. Inspect the first interleaved round for validity fields only (invalid, smoke modified, canary, project hash, transcript present), not for catches. Start with a new epoch id if a round is voided; its cost still counts against the ceiling.
4. **Verdict** in `epochs/<epoch-id>/VERDICT.md`:
   - pins and command lines; the authorised ceiling against realised spend; every run, including invalid and retried ones;
   - the per-defect table for the three arms;
   - first among the results, the catches per arm split by defect class and by matched or unmatched pair; then the primary delta (Calor minus equipped C#) and the two other deltas, each with the same split;
   - the catch-channel table per defect per arm; mean iterations and cost per arm;
   - the registered reading that applies and the limits paragraph, both quoted from the registration. If an abort trigger fired, the trigger, with the collection reported as incomplete under the "not adjudicated" reading.
5. **Publish.**
   - A section in the 0.32 release notes (`CHANGELOG.md`), labelled fixture-scale, seeded and non-independent. It gives the split by defect class and by matched or unmatched pair before the primary delta.
   - A row in the evidence ledger (`docs/benchmarking/evidence-ledger.md`, created by #1774) with comparison type, scope, model pin and syntax version, and the same split beside the delta. `docs/benchmarking/results.md` is generated and is not edited.
   - A new A.3 entry in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` recording the outcome. It edits no registered row.
   - Realised spend appended to the spend record.
6. **Apply.** Post the applicable outcome's "Rows kept" and "What changes in the #1698 hypothesis" paragraphs, quoted from the registration, on #1662, on the epics #1861 and #1872, and on #1698. Post the verdict link on #1709 and #1711 as an input, and on #1805.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The July epoch is `epochs/ws5-probe-001`: nine pairs, two arms, three runs each; Calor 9 of 9 and C# 4 of 9 (its `VERDICT.md:21-26`); mean iterations-to-green 1.63 against 1.0 (`:73`). It ran on the pre-0.30 syntax with `claude-opus-4-8` (its `pins.json`).
- That epoch needed one harness fix found only in live runs (the smoke-suite hash counted build output; "null-agent never builds smoke", the same `VERDICT.md`, `:80-89`), and one full attempt was discarded after an API incident (`:97-99`). A zero-cost pass is necessary and not sufficient, which is why step 3 inspects the first round.
- A third condition, a three-arm driver with a spend stop and a channel analyzer for 0.30 sources do not exist at HEAD. #1815 and #1813 add them.
- Cost is `total_cost_usd` in each run's `agent.json`. `result.json` carries tokens and the `defect` object (`run-pair.sh:1586-1590`).
- Comparisons are valid only between arms run together on one pinned model within one epoch (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:12`). No figure from this epoch is compared with July's.
- The evidence ledger does not exist at HEAD; #1774 creates it. Annex A accepts additions only (`scripts/check-annex-freeze.py`); the pattern of recording an adjudicated outcome as a new A.3 entry is at `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:1107`.

#### Why

This is the cheapest test available before 0.37 of whether Calor's one adjudicated lead over C# is an effect of the language or of having a specification at all, and it is the first agent run on the spellings 0.32 is about to freeze. It is published whatever it shows: a result that says "specifications were present" redirects 0.35 toward what only build-time proof gives, and that is worth knowing before that milestone starts. It also rehearses, at small scale, the three-condition procedure of #1709 to #1712.

This is new capacity and displaces no existing issue. Its cost is the authorised spend and one collection session.

#### Done when

One of the following.

- **Run.** The epoch directory and its `-null` companion are committed, and `VERDICT.md` has every item of step 4. It shows realised spend beside the ceiling and names any abort trigger that fired. The `CHANGELOG.md` section, the evidence-ledger row, the Annex A outcome entry and the realised-spend line in the spend record are merged; the `annex-freeze` job passed on the pull request that added the entry. The outcome's paragraphs are posted on #1662, on the epics #1861 and #1872, and on #1698, and the verdict is linked on #1709, #1711 and #1805. The product commit in the epoch's `pins.json` is the one #1807 posted, and the line appended in step 1 was committed before the `startedAt` in that file.
- **Declined.** The issue is closed as not planned with the "Declined" line of the spend record linked.

Either outcome meets the 0.32 exit criterion: "Seeded-defect replication with three conditions published on the post-rewrite commit, or the maintainer's decline recorded; if a collection is authorised but unpublished at release, the edition declaration is deferred (#1805) and this criterion is recorded as carried to 0.33". In the carried state the issue is not yet done.

#### Not in scope

- Changing a threshold, a reading, the run count or the ceiling. A second collection needs a new decision line in the spend record.
- Editing a 0.35 row or the #1698 body. This issue posts the registered consequence; the owning epics apply it at their entry.
- The #1645 re-run (Part B of #1816).
- Any comparison with Rust, or any claim about natural defects or real-scale tasks.

Part of epic #1812. Depends on #1816, #1807. Blocks #1805, #1698.

## Epic #1804 · [0.32] Epic: one toolchain — write, test and check in Calor (part 1)

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

By the end of 0.32 an agent that follows the rules `calor init` gives it can add a test and run it. The time between an edit and its diagnostics is a measured number with a budget on each of the three feedback paths. The incremental-build fix that shipped in 0.30 is recorded as shipped. There is one tested answer to "how do I change C# that the converter kept".

#### Why

The plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`, section 3) keeps a capability scorecard of what a Rust user takes for granted. Three of its lines are about the toolchain: tests written in the language, one command that builds, tests and checks, and fast warm feedback. At HEAD `bc80daeb` four gaps stand behind them.

- **Tests cannot be added through the sanctioned path.** `calor check` returns exit 0 only when at least one test ran (`docs/cli/index.md:83`). It and `calor test` discover only top-level `tests/*.cs` xUnit files (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:404-431`). Rule 1 of the text `calor init` writes says "Never create new `.cs` files" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template:10`), and the write hook it installs blocks both creating and editing every `.cs` path outside `obj/` that is not a `.g.cs` file (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/HookCommand.cs:256-323`, `:347-348`). The adoption guide's first suggested agent task asks the agent to "add tests" (`website/content/guides/adding-calor-to-existing-projects.mdx:130-131`).
- **Feedback latency is unmeasured on 0.30.** The last record is dated 2026-08-01, on a fixture in the retired syntax, for the MCP write path only (`bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/ml1-003/VERDICT.md`). `calor check` is new in 0.30 (`CHANGELOG.md:170`), and no record, test or CI job times it. What `dotnet build` recompiles after an edit is pinned by tests and not timed.
- **The incremental-build fix shipped and its issue is still open.** The 0.30 release notes say both that `dotnet build` is incremental again (`CHANGELOG.md:188`, citing #1732) and that it recompiles every file after any edit (`CHANGELOG.md:354-359`).
- **Preserved C# has contradictory instructions.** A preserved file is a `.calr` file in which the converter kept the original C#, because it could not express it as Calor. Rule 2 tells the agent to convert C# and "then edit the `.calr` file" (`CLAUDE.md.template:11`). The teaching reference says "Do not handwrite raw C# passthrough" (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:22`). The hook blocks editing the original `.cs`. On the default leg of the round-trip harness the native-file floors are 5 of 32 (MediatR), 5 of 139 (FluentValidation) and 10 of 112 (Serilog) (`native_fraction` 0.156, 0.036 and 0.089 under `blocking_floors.default` in `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json`; the file totals are in `docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:363-365`). The fraction counts files converted with no recorded loss (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/RoundTripReport.cs:512-531`), so on that leg most files of a project either hold at least one recorded loss or were not converted.

This is part 1. It takes what can be done with one validator change, one measurement and text. Part 2 (epic #1819, in 0.33) takes what needs compiler work: tests written in `.calr` and the remaining incremental-reuse tier.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1809 | Agents may create and edit C# test files under `tests/`: one exemption in the write-hook validator, the rule text, and messages that name the path. A 0.32 exit criterion |
| #1732 | Verify the shipped fix against its acceptance criteria, correct the stale release note, close; what remains goes to #1836 |
| #1818 | Edit-to-diagnostic time on 0.30 for the MCP write path, `dotnet build` and `calor check`, each with a budget; a nightly CI job; the sizing input for #1836 |
| #1822 | One sanctioned, tested procedure for changing converter-preserved C#, stated the same way by the template rule, the teaching reference and the hook (may slip) |

#### Sequence

1. #1732 and #1818 have no prerequisite and can start when 0.32 opens. Part 1 of #1809 (validator and messages) depends on no issue, but merges only after the commit pin of stage 2 of #1605, or after #1605 records stage 2 as not run, so that stage 2 is measured on the unexempted hook.
2. Part 2 of #1809 (rule text) and #1822 follow #1823, which sets the `calor init` delivery default and the token budgets their text must fit.
3. #1809 and #1822 both change `HookCommand.ValidateWriteWithReason` and the rule text in `reference.json`. Whichever merges second rebases on the first.
4. The Calor text that #1818 adds (the sources for its timed project and its 0.30 edit script) and the Calor literals in the tests of #1809 and #1822 are 0.30 text. Whatever exists before the single rewrite (#1803) merges is part of that rewrite's inventory.

#### Done when

- #1809 is closed. This is the 0.32 exit criterion "Agents may create test files through the sanctioned path": it needs both the write-hook exemption and the rule text in whichever of the agent file and the MCP server instructions the #1823 record leaves carrying the rules.
- #1732 is closed as completed, and the 0.30 entry "`dotnet build` recompiles every Calor file after any edit" carries a dated correction in `CHANGELOG.md` and in `website/content/changelog.mdx`.
- The latency record of #1818 is published with the three paths reported separately, each budgeted quantity is marked within or over, the nightly job runs, and #1836 carries the figures it is sized by.
- #1822 is closed, or it is moved to 0.33 with a comment that says so. It is off the 0.32 exit chain.

#### Not in this epic

- Tests written in `.calr`, and discovery beyond top-level `tests/*.cs`: #1825 (0.33).
- Making any feedback path faster: #1836 (0.33). Index build time: #1738 (0.31).
- The `calor init` delivery default and every token budget constant: #1823. The always-on MCP budget: #1824.
- Tests on what `calor init` writes for each agent, including the test-authoring path: #1650 (0.33).
- Reducing how many files are preserved: #1647 and epic #1659. Preserved originals that fail under their own nullable context (CS8669): #1782.
- Counting how often agents were blocked when creating a test file in the #1605 run: #1760.
- Two follow-ups that no issue owns:
  - Sparing a caller from recompilation when only a callee's declared effect row changes (criterion 5 of #1732 and the "while the caller is skipped" clause of its criterion 3). #1836 covers body-only edits and does not build this tier.
  - Receipt handling in the MCP tools `calor_convert` and `calor_migrate`, which write `.calr` files without reading or writing the conversion receipt that `calor convert` and `calor migrate` check. #1822 names one entry point for its procedure and documents the mismatch; it does not remove it.

### #1809 · [0.32] Agents may create test files under tests/: hook exemption and template text

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1823 · **Blocks:** #1650, #1825 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Let an agent that follows the `calor init` rules create and edit C# test files. Two ordered pull requests.

**Part 1: validator and messages (no issue prerequisite).**

Order. Part 1 merges after the commit that the stage 2 sub-entry of #1605 records, or after #1605 records stage 2 not run, so that the stage 2 arms and the blocked-attempt count of #1760 are taken on the unexempted hook. Each pull request of this issue states whether it merged before or after the commit #1807 records for the 0.32 paid runs.

1. In `HookCommand.ValidateWriteWithReason` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/HookCommand.cs:256-323`), allow a `.cs` path when one of its directory segments is exactly `tests`. The validator receives only the tool-input JSON, often with an absolute path, and has no project root, so the rule is on path segments. Split on `/` and `\` and count only directory segments, never the file name, as `ExecutionWorkspace.IsInExcludedDirectory` does (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ExecutionWorkspace.cs:222-231`). Compare ordinally (that function ignores case; this check does not): `tests` is the only spelling `calor check` finds on a case-sensitive file system (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:412`). Ordinal matching is the plan's recommendation; if the maintainer asks on this issue for case-insensitive matching before part 1 merges, `Tests/Foo.cs` moves from the blocked to the allowed list under Done when, `Tests/` leaves the Not in scope list, and nothing else changes. Put the check before the block at `:297`. This one function serves `calor hook validate-write` (`:192`), `validate-edit` (`:348`), the Gemini output format of both, and `codex-write` (`:115`), so creating and editing are covered for every agent that has a hook. Everything else stays blocked, including a file named `tests.cs` and directories such as `contests/` or `tests-old/`.
2. Name the path at the two places where an agent is stopped. This costs no always-loaded tokens.
   - Add one sentence to the block reason (`:302-310`) and to the Gemini `systemMessage` (`:226-230`): C# test files are allowed under `tests/`.
   - Replace the last line of both messages. They send the agent to a `/calor` skill, `/calor-semantics` and `@calor` (`:310`, `:230`), which no initializer installs. Name only what `calor init` installs: the `calor_help` tool of the `calor` MCP server, or the agent file.
   - Make both "No tests found" messages (`TestCommand.cs:242`, `:381-382`) name the file to create (`tests/<Name>Tests.cs`, xUnit) and say in one sentence how a C# test reaches a Calor function: root functions are emitted in `GlobalModule`, or under a namespace `N` in `N.<last segment of N>Module` (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:2385-2388`, `docs/syntax-reference/structure-tags.md:42-44`; the fixture test calls `MathUtil.MathUtilModule.Add`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ValidationLoopTests.cs:24-41`).
3. Update the text that describes the block: the behaviour tables in `docs/cli/hook.md:37-42` and `website/content/cli/hook.mdx:44-49`; the sentences "blocks Claude from creating `.cs` files" and "blocks Gemini from creating `.cs` files" in `docs/cli/init.md:172` and `:260`; the sentence at `website/content/getting-started/gemini-integration.mdx:36`; and the hook description string in `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/gemini-settings.json.template:17`.

**Part 2: rule text (after #1823).**

4. Reword Rule 1 in the rule text in `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json` (the `agentInstructions` field at line 6, or the field or fields #1823 split it into). It still forbids new `.cs` product files. It permits C# test files under `tests/`, for editing as well as creating, because Rule 2 ("Convert C# to Calor before modifying it") would otherwise send the agent to convert a test file. Add one line to the primer (the `introduction` or `mistakes` text of the `primer` object in the same file, `:2431`): tests are xUnit C# files in `tests/`, and `calor check <dir>` runs them. Regenerate with `calor self-check docs --fix`. Do not edit the four templates or `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/Generated/server-instructions.md` by hand.
5. Fit the budgets in force after #1823, within the share of the always-loaded headroom its record gives this issue, and raise no constant. If #1823 closes without changing a constant (including the case where stage 2 of #1605 did not run), fit the constants as they are today; if there is no room, shorten other text in the same field in the same pull request. `agentInstructions` is emitted into each of the four templates and into the MCP server instructions (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/ExemplarCompileChecker.cs:138`, `:149`, `:158-162`). A token added to it therefore counts twice in `InitTemplatePlusServerInstructions_FitsBudget` and once in `AlwaysOnMcpSurface_FitsRatchet` at HEAD; after decision 3 of #1823 takes option b or c it counts once.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `ValidateWriteWithReason` allows `.calr` (`HookCommand.cs:277-281`), `.g.cs` (`:283-287`) and any path under `obj/` (`:289-295`), and blocks every other `.cs` path (`:297-313`). The edit hook calls the same function (`:347-348`), so editing an existing C# test is blocked as well as creating one. By that code, `calor hook validate-write '{"file_path": "tests/MathTests.cs"}'` exits 1 today (command form as in `docs/cli/hook.md:46-48`).
- The block reason ends "Use /calor skill for syntax, /calor-semantics for behavior rules." (`HookCommand.cs:310`) and the Gemini message ends "Use @calor for syntax, @calor-semantics for behavior rules." (`:230`). The initializers install no skill files; each says MCP tools replace them (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/ClaudeInitializer.cs:9`, `GeminiInitializer.cs:9`).
- Installed hooks. Claude Code: `Write` runs `validate-write` and `Edit` runs `validate-edit` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/ClaudeInitializer.cs:219-253`). Gemini CLI: `write_file|replace` runs `validate-write --format gemini` (`gemini-settings.json.template:11-16`). Codex: `calor hook codex-write --phase pre` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/CodexInitializer.cs:224`), which validates every path the patch adds, updates or moves a file to (`HookCommand.cs:94-121`, `:139-168`). Copilot has no hook (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/GitHubCopilotInitializer.cs:10`). The hooks run the installed `calor` tool, so a validator change reaches existing projects on upgrade; the rule text reaches them only when `calor init` runs again.
- Tests pin the block for create, edit and Codex patches: `ValidateWrite_BlocksCsFilesWithPath` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/HookCommandTests.cs:229`), `EditCsFile_IsBlocked` (`tests/Calor.Ids.Tests/ValidateEditTests.cs:14-24`), `CodexWriteHook_BlocksCSharpAddedByApplyPatch` (`HookCommandTests.cs:12`). No hook test passes a `.cs` path with a `tests` segment (searched `HookCommandTests.cs` and `HookCommandAgentInvocationTests.cs` in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests`, and `GeminiFormatTests.cs`, `HookEdgeCaseTests.cs`, `HookIdValidationTests.cs`, `HookSecurityTests.cs`, `ValidateCalrContentTests.cs` and `ValidateEditTests.cs` in `tests/Calor.Ids.Tests`).
- `calor check` exits 0 only with at least one test (`docs/cli/index.md:83`) and exits 3 with none (`TestCommand.cs:31`, `:240-242`; `calor test` at `:380-383`). `FindTestFiles` returns top-level `tests/*.cs` plus `tests/shims/TestShim.calor.cs` (`:404-431`). The exit code and the stage status are pinned by `NoTestFiles_ReturnsDistinctExitThree` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ValidationLoopTests.cs:131`), and the `calor test` message by an assertion on "No tests found" (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/RunTestCommandTests.cs:352`).
- Rule 1 reads "Never create new `.cs` files. Write new code in Calor (`.calr`)." (`CLAUDE.md.template:10`). The four templates are byte-identical (checked with `cmp`).
- Budgets, o200k proxy tokenizer, measured 2026-11-20: template 317 plus server instructions 275 is 592 of 600; primer 1,569 of 1,600; always-on MCP surface 7,900 of 8,000 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:41-47`, `:49-54`, `:65-71`). At today's constants the field can grow by four tokens.
- No agent-facing text says where tests go: `reference.json` contains none of "xunit", "tests/" or "calor_check" (searched).
- The repository's own CI guard already exempts tests: `is_test_source` in `scripts/check-calor-first-diff.sh:78-84` passes every path under `tests/`.
- The adoption guide's first agent prompt asks the agent to "add tests" (`website/content/guides/adding-calor-to-existing-projects.mdx:130-131`).

#### Why

`calor check` is the one command that builds, tests and verifies, and it does not pass without a test. The rules and the hook forbid the only kind of test file it discovers. An agent that obeys cannot reach exit 0 on new code, and an agent that wants a test must break Rule 1. This is the minimum that removes the contradiction: the tests stay C#. Tests written in Calor need compiler work and are #1825. The report on the 0.30 agent evaluation (#1605) counts how often an agent tried to create a test file and was blocked (#1760); that count is a before figure, which is why part 1 merges after the stage 2 pin and does not wait for the report.

This is new capacity and displaces nothing: part 1 is one condition and its tests, part 2 is two sentences, and the 0.32 exit criterion "Agents may create test files through the sanctioned path" names this issue.

#### Done when

- Hook tests in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/HookCommandTests.cs` and `tests/Calor.Ids.Tests/` cover `validate-write`, `validate-write --format gemini`, `validate-edit` and `codex-write --phase pre` (an `Add File` and an `Update File` patch). Allowed: `tests/MathTests.cs`, `tests/shims/TestShim.calor.cs`, an absolute path with a `tests` segment, and the same with `\` separators. Blocked: `src/Foo.cs`, `tests.cs`, `contests/Foo.cs`, `tests-old/Foo.cs`, `Tests/Foo.cs`.
- One end-to-end test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ValidationLoopTests.cs`, run through the CLI and through `calor_check` as its `RunAsync` helper does (`:45-69`): in a directory with one `.calr` module and no tests, the loop returns exit 3 and its message names `tests/` and the container rule; `HookCommand.ValidateWriteWithReason` then allows `tests/MathTests.cs` under that directory; with that file written the loop returns exit 0 and `testsRun` of at least 1.
- A test asserts that the block reason for a `.cs` path outside `tests/`, and the Gemini `systemMessage` for the same path, contain the sentence about test files and none of the strings "/calor skill", "calor-semantics" and "@calor". The assertion on the `calor test` message (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/RunTestCommandTests.cs:352`) is extended to the file to create and the container rule.
- Part 2: a test asserts that every generated text that the #1823 record (decision 3) leaves carrying the rule block contains the sentence that permits C# test files under `tests/` (under a, the four templates and `server-instructions.md`; under b, the four templates; under c, `server-instructions.md`, and each template then carries a one-line pointer to it), and that the primer contains the test line; `calor self-check docs` exits 0 (CI step at `.github/workflows/test.yml:267`); `TokenBudgetTests` passes with no constant changed by this issue; the pull request states o200k tokens added and removed for template plus server instructions, the primer and the MCP surface (the three tests print these figures).
- `docs/cli/hook.md`, `website/content/cli/hook.mdx`, `docs/cli/init.md` and `website/content/getting-started/gemini-integration.mdx` state the exemption.
- `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised by the tests added, for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` (`:8`) and `Calor.Ids.Tests` (`:50`); `scripts/check_trx.py` enforces it (`.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).
- Both parts are merged, and each pull request states its order against the two commits named under Order. The exit criterion needs the rule text as well as the validator: Copilot has no hook, so for that agent the text is the whole path. Under option c the #1823 record must say how Copilot receives the rule; if the record does not, this bullet is not met.

#### Not in scope

- Tests written in `.calr`, and discovery beyond top-level `tests/*.cs` (#1825).
- C# test projects whose path has no segment spelled exactly `tests`, for example `MyApp.Tests/` or `Tests/`. They stay blocked for creating and editing. On a case-insensitive file system `calor check` also finds `Tests/`; the hook still blocks it, and the messages name `tests/`.
- Tests on what `calor init` writes for each agent (#1650).
- Editing the original C# of a preserved file (#1822).
- Making hooks effective in a client that does not run them.

Part of epic #1804. Depends on #1823 (part 2 only; part 1 is ordered after the stage 2 pin of #1605). Blocks #1650, #1825.

### #1732 · [0.32] MSBuild task: port the CLI's cross-module independence proof, and accept namespaced static partial module wrappers

**Action:** amend (from none) · **Labels:** enhancement · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

**Current**

The issue reports that since commit 9ce37a970 any edit in a multi-file `dotnet build` recompiled every Calor file: the MSBuild task could not take the independence proof the CLI driver used, and that proof rejected modules with a namespace header. It asks for (a) one reuse decision shared by the CLI and the task and (b) a proof that accepts namespaced `static partial` module wrappers, with five acceptance criteria. It is open, with no milestone and no labels.

**Amendment**

Set the milestone to 0.32 and the label to `enhancement`. The title gains only the `[0.32]` prefix. Then five steps, in order.

1. Verify. The table in step 2 was written by reading the code and the tests at `bc80daeb`; the tests were not run. Confirm on `main` that the tests it names pass in the `tests (tasks)` and `tests (compiler)` shards of `.github/workflows/test.yml`, and keep the link to that run. Every "Met" in the table rests on it; if one of the tests fails, stop and report on the issue instead of closing it.

2. Append this section to the body, above the "Generated with" footer:

```markdown
## Status at 0.30

The fix shipped in 0.30 (`CHANGELOG.md:188`). Both parts of the proposed work are in the tree at `bc80daeb`:

- (a) One reuse decision, `CrossModuleCacheReuse` (`src/Calor.Compiler/CompilationDriver.cs:291`), called by the CLI driver (`:588`) and by the task (`src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:635`).
- (b) The proof accepts a namespaced `static partial` module wrapper. The soundness argument is the comment at `src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/CrossModuleTypeRegistry.cs:249-278`.

| Criterion | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1. A body-only edit recompiles only that module | Met | `FullLifecycle_BuildSkipEditCleanBuild` (`tests/Calor.Tasks.Tests/CompileCalorIntegrationTests.cs:175`): build 3 logs no "global invalidation", logs `[cross-module-sources-changed]`, skips Bar and compiles Foo (`:212-223`) |
| 2. The two `IncrementalCliBuildTests` pass with `block` headers | Met | `ChangedFile_IsRecompiled_OthersStaySkipped` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/IncrementalCliBuildTests.cs:149`) and `MidCompileEdit_IsNotRecordedAsCompiled_NextRunRecompiles` (`:167`); the fixture is `module Alpha Alpha block` (`:73-88`) |
| 3. Soundness pins on both surfaces | Met, except the words "while the caller is skipped" | Rows `library-rename`, `library-signature`, `library-row-unused`, `library-body`, `corrupt-output`, `missing-output` and `sibling-body` of `IncrementalSurfaceAgreementTests` (`tests/Calor.Tasks.Tests/IncrementalSurfaceAgreementTests.cs:79-98`), and `CrossModuleViolation_AppearsAndDisappearsWithTheCalleeRow_OnBothSurfaces` (`:159`). Calor0410 appears and disappears correctly with the caller recompiled, not skipped |
| 4. The CLI and the task agree file by file | Met | `CliAndTaskSkipTheSameFiles_AndOnlyProvenIndependentOnes` (`:102`) |
| 5. ES-04 amendment "with the caller a cache hit again" | Not met, by maintainer decision | The proof spares only outputs that name nothing another module declares, so a caller is recompiled on every callee edit. The maintainer kept that behaviour for 0.30 and recorded the finer reuse tier as follow-up (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:374-393`). Section 7 of F-3′ in `docs/plans/v0.13-freeze-registrations.md` (`:563`) is unchanged |

Not part of this issue any more: skipping a caller when the callee's declaration surface is unchanged, and skipping a derived class on a body-only edit to its base. That work moves to #1836, which is conditional on the latency measurement in #1818. Criterion 5 and the "while the caller is skipped" clause of criterion 3 are not delivered by #1836 in any branch: ES-04 edits a declared effect row, and sparing a caller on a row-only change is a further tier that no issue builds. #1836 records that in its closing comment.
```

3. Correct the release notes. In `CHANGELOG.md` replace the bullet at lines 354-359, and the identical bullet at `website/content/changelog.mdx:360-365`, with the text below. It follows the repository's form for correcting a released section: a dated correction that keeps the original text (`CHANGELOG.md:3051-3053`). Replace `YYYY-MM-DD` with the date the correction merges.

```markdown
- **Correction (YYYY-MM-DD): `dotnet build` is incremental again.** The entry quoted below describes the build before the #1732 fix and was stale when 0.30 shipped; see "Fixed" above. The MSBuild task makes the same skip decision as `calor --cache` and `calor watch`, and modules with a namespace header can be skipped. Still less incremental than 0.29: after an edit to another file, a file is skipped only if its generated C# names nothing another module declares, no other module declares a class or an interface, and no module holds preserved C#. So a caller is recompiled after any edit to any file, and a derived class after any edit to its base class. Results are correct. Original text follows.
  > **`dotnet build` recompiles every Calor file after any edit.** Since each file's
  > content became part of the cross-module fingerprint, the MSBuild task can no
  > longer prove that an unedited file is unaffected. Results are correct; builds are
  > less incremental than in 0.29. `calor build` still skips files it can prove
  > independent, but only modules without a namespace header. Restoring incremental
  > MSBuild builds is tracked as follow-up work.
```

The release body on GitHub was rendered from the changelog when 0.30 was published (`scripts/render_release_notes.py`, `.github/workflows/publish-nuget.yml:495`) and does not follow this edit. Whether the maintainer also edits it by hand is stated in the closing comment. Not verified: what that release body says today.

4. Section 7 of F-3′. The additive text that discharges criterion 5 as "revisited, caller still recompiled" is already drafted and marked "not applied" (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:385-393`). Applying it to `docs/plans/v0.13-freeze-registrations.md` is the maintainer's decision. Options: apply it in the pull request of step 3, which leaves no criterion of this issue without a written outcome; or leave the section unchanged, in which case the closing comment is the only record that criterion 5 lapsed. The plan recommends applying it. The closing comment states which was done.

5. Close with this comment and `state_reason: completed`:

```markdown
Closing as completed. The fix shipped in 0.30: the MSBuild task and the CLI share one reuse decision (`CrossModuleCacheReuse`), and the independence proof accepts namespaced module wrappers. Criteria 1, 2 and 4 are met, and criterion 3 is met with the caller recompiled; the tests are listed under "Status at 0.30" and passed in <link to the CI run from step 1>. Criterion 5 (ES-04 with the caller a cache hit again) needs a reuse tier that spares a caller when only the callee's declared row changes; the maintainer deferred it (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:374-393`) and no issue builds it. <number of #1836> covers the body-only tiers and records this.
```
