F-3′ section 7: <amended with the text proposed in the triage document | left unchanged>. The stale 0.30 release-note entry is corrected in <pull request>. GitHub release body: <edited | left as published>.

**Why**

The fix shipped in 0.30 while the issue stayed open with no milestone, and the 0.30 notes contradict themselves: `CHANGELOG.md:188` says `dotnet build` is incremental again, and `CHANGELOG.md:354-359` says every file recompiles. An open issue with no milestone reads as a missing feature, and the stale note tells adopters the build is slower than it is. Criterion 5 cannot be met by this issue's design and no later issue delivers it, so closing has to say that it lapses and why.

### #1818 · [0.32] Measure warm calor check and MCP write latency on 0.30; CI budget for edit-to-diagnostic time

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, ci · **Blocks:** #1836 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Measure the time between an edit and its diagnostics on the 0.30 compiler for the three ways an agent gets feedback, publish one record, and put a budget on each. Report the three paths separately; they share no cache. The work may land as two ordered pull requests: first the `durationMs` field of item 3 with its test, then the scripts, the CI job, the budget file and the record.

1. **MCP write path.** Re-run `bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/measure-ml1.py` on the 0.30 mirror of its fixture (`tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/fixture-10k/src`, 106 files). Give the driver an option that selects the mirror and a 0.30 edit script, added as a new file beside the committed one. The 200 safe edits of `bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/edits/edit-script.jsonl` apply unchanged. Its 30 breaking edits match retired header text and need 0.30 equivalents with the same intent (a rename that breaks callers). Report P50, P90, P99 and maximum of the tool's own `mcp-write/2` `latencyMs`. Report the watch surface if the driver's one-file-rebuild check passes (`measure-ml1.py:250-253`); otherwise record it as not measured, with the cause. The check is expected to fail on 0.30 (see Baseline); do not weaken it.
2. **`dotnet build`.** A script builds an SDK project from the same 106 files against a locally packed `Calor.Sdk` (the method of `.github/scripts/test-sdk-package.sh`) with default properties. It times `dotnet build --no-restore` for a build in which every Calor file compiles, a warm build with no edit, and three body-only edits, each reverted before the next:
   - (a) an unrelated file: a module that no other file names and that names no other module. The fixture has none (see Baseline), so one such module is added;
   - (b) a callee body: a function that another file calls, for example one in an L3 file;
   - (c) a base-class body: a method of a base class that a class in another file extends. The fixture declares no class, so a base file and a derived file are added.

   For each build report the wall time, the time of the `CompileCalor` task (from MSBuild's performance summary or a binary log) and the Calor files compiled and skipped, as the median of five runs. With `-p:CalorVerbose=true` the task logs `Compiling Calor: <file>` for each file it compiles and `Calor: skipping (up-to-date): <file>` for each it skips (`src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:794`, `:760`). Use two project copies. P1 is the fixture plus the module for (a); time (a) and (b) on it. P2 is P1 plus the base and derived class files; time (c) on it, and report (a) and (b) on P2 as unbudgeted rows. The split is needed because one instance class in a project stops the reuse proof for every other file (see Baseline), and converted .NET code is mostly classes.
3. **`calor check`.** Time `calor check <dir> --format json` on a pair-sized directory (one module and one xUnit test, the shape of the `ValidationLoopTests` fixture) and on the 106-file project with one test file. One cold run, then five runs, each after a one-file body edit. Report total wall time and three segments: the in-process compile and verification, the `dotnet build` child process, the `dotnet test` child process. To make that possible add an optional `durationMs` to the `compile`, `build` and `test` entries of `stages` in the JSON result (`LoopStage`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:99`; additive, and `calor_check` returns the same object). Verification runs inside the compile call (`:173-192`), so the `verify` entry has no time of its own and the documentation says that `compile` includes it. Document the field where the result is described: `docs/cli/index.md:74-81` and `website/content/cli/test.mdx:44`.
4. **Budgets.**

   | Path | Budgeted quantity | Budget | Source |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | MCP write | `latencyMs` over the edit script | P50 at most 300 ms, P99 at most 1 s | PP-L1, the warm-latency proof point whose threshold is frozen in Annex A.2 (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:318`). Reported against; the row is not edited |
   | `dotnet build`, edits (a), (b), (c) | `CompileCalor` task time, median of five | 1 s each (recommended) | The P99 bound of PP-L1, reused |
   | `calor check`, 106-file project | in-process segment, median of five | 1 s (recommended) | The same |

   The two recommended values are a maintainer decision. Options: accept 1 s; state another value with its reason; or leave a path without a budget, which leaves #1836 with no gate on that path. The plan recommends 1 s because it is the only warm-feedback bound the project has registered, and because it is placed on the part of the wait that Calor code controls. MSBuild start-up, the C# compile and the test run are reported, and no Calor change removes them. The maintainer confirms or replaces the values in a comment on this issue before the first CI figures are read. If they are set after figures exist, the record says so in words.
5. **CI.** Run the three measurements in `.github/workflows/performance.yml` (nightly and on dispatch, not per pull request). Keep the budgets in a checked-in file (for example `eng/latency-budgets.json`) that holds, for each quantity, the budget, whether it was within or over when registered, and the issue that owns an overrun. The job fails when a quantity registered as within measures over. A quantity registered as over is reported and does not fail the job until its owner changes the entry. Give the job a self-test step in the manner of `python3 scripts/run_performance_gate.py --self-test`, which the same workflow already runs.
6. **Record.** Write `bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/ml1-004/VERDICT.md` and `result.json` in the form of `ml1-003`, taken from one dispatch of that job on the pull-request branch. The Calor sources added for (a) and (c) and the 0.30 edit script are 0.30 text, kept next to the script or as literals in it. If the repository-wide syntax rewrite of this milestone (#1803) has not merged when this lands, list them in a comment on #1803 so that its inventory includes them.

Validity rules. The warm build with no edit must compile zero Calor files in P1 and in P2; otherwise the cache is not in effect and the edit rows time a cold compile. Every timed `calor check` run must report its `compile` stage as `passed`, since a compile error skips the build and the tests (`TestCommand.cs:214-220`); the record gives each run's exit code and names the cause if it is not 0. If either rule fails, the record states the cause and marks the affected rows not measurable. The fixture is not changed to make them measurable.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Last latency record: `bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/ml1-003/VERDICT.md`, 2026-08-01, commit c1662210, the maintainer's machine. MCP write P50 2 ms, P99 7.42 ms over 230 edits; watch not measured, because every rebuild recompiled 105 of 106 files. The figure of 2 ms and 8.84 ms quoted elsewhere (for example `docs/plans/loop-m4-ws3.md:9`) is the earlier record `ml1-002`, dated 2026-07-27, on the fixture's previous generation.
- The driver is run as `measure-ml1.py --calor <path/to/calor.dll> --out <dir> [--surface mcp|watch|both]` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/measure-ml1.py:21`) and refuses to run on a dirty tree (`:280-302`). It reads the retired-syntax fixture and edit script (`:36-37`). A 0.30 mirror of the 106 files exists, and `IndexPerformanceTests` already resolves it through `ActiveSyntaxInputs.ForCurrentCompiler` (`tests/Calor.Performance.Tests/IndexPerformanceTests.cs:36-38`). Applied in sequence, all 200 safe edits find their text in the mirror; none of the 30 breaking edits does (for example `:l2m16_fn09:pub}`; checked with a script). Not verified: that the mirror compiles without warnings on 0.30, and that `calor check` passes on it.
- The fixture is `main.calr` plus 35 files in each of three layers; L1 calls L2 and L2 calls L3 (`bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/fixture-10k/README.md`, row "cross-module ref depth"). Every L2 and L3 module is called from another file, and every L1 module and `main.calr` calls into another module, so no file is unrelated to the rest (checked with a script on the mirror). The mirror declares no class (0 lines matching `class `).
- The driver's watch surface requires every rebuild to recompile exactly one file (`measure-ml1.py:250-253`). `ml1-003` could not meet that because the CLI driver caches only diagnostic-clean files (`src/Calor.Compiler/CompilationDriver.cs:936-941`) and the fixture produced warnings. On 0.30 there is a second cause: an edit also recompiles every file whose output names another module's declarations (rows `bystander-body` and `library-body`, cited below), which here is every L1 and L2 file and `main.calr`. This is expected from the code and was not run.
- No workflow runs the driver (searched `.github/workflows` and `scripts` for `measure-ml1`).
- `calor check` is new in 0.30 (`CHANGELOG.md:170-171`). Each run creates a new temporary workspace (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:150`), compiles a fresh snapshot with the verification cache off (`:173-192`), then starts `dotnet build` (`:229`) and `dotnet test --no-build` (`:249`). Its result has no timing field (`LoopStage`, `:99`).
- `dotnet build` runs the `CompileCalor` task (`src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:72-112`), which shares the skip decision `CrossModuleCacheReuse` with the CLI (`CompilationDriver.cs:291`; called from the task at `src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:635`). Unlike the CLI driver, the task caches a file that has warnings and replays them when the file is skipped (`CompileCalor.cs:746-755`, `:875-883`). Tests pin what recompiles for the three edit kinds on three-file projects: `bystander-body` recompiles the bystander and the caller, `library-body` the library and the caller (`tests/Calor.Tasks.Tests/IncrementalSurfaceAgreementTests.cs:86-88`), and a body edit to a base class recompiles the derived class (`DerivedRecompilesOnAnyBaseEdit_AndItsContractStaysCorrect`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/CrossModuleVarianceTests.cs:1150`). Nothing times them.
- Expected from that code, not run: on the fixture, edits (a) and (b) recompile nearly the same set, the edited file plus `main.calr` and the 70 L1 and L2 files, and (c) on P2 recompiles every file. The task times of (a) and (b) will then be close, and the files-compiled counts, not the difference between the two times, show how many callers were recompiled without need.
- The reuse proof returns false for a file when any other module declares an instance class or an interface (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/CrossModuleTypeRegistry.cs:299-326`), and for every file when any module contains preserved C# (`:204`, `:341-345`).
- `tests/Calor.Performance.Tests` (40 tests, `eng/performance-baselines.json:5`) has no warm-rebuild or `calor check` timing. `.github/scripts/test-sdk-package.sh:138-146` does an incremental rebuild and checks for a skip message, with no timing.
- Shared runners are noisy: the same commit measured suite medians from 22.2 s to 35.7 s over nine dispatches (`eng/performance-baselines.json`, `noisePolicy`). Hence medians of five runs, a nightly job and no per-pull-request gate.
- Annex A.2 rows are held byte-frozen by `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py` (job `annex-freeze`, `.github/workflows/experiment-registry-tamper-check.yml:98`).

#### Why

The capability scorecard in section 3 of the plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`) has the lines "Fast warm feedback" and "One command that builds, tests and checks", and neither has a number on 0.30. The remaining incremental-reuse work (#1836) changes a soundness-critical decision: a wrong skip hides a diagnostic. Whether it is worth doing depends on how much time today's conservative behaviour costs, and no record measures that. Reading the code says which files recompile; it does not say whether that takes 200 ms or 20 s.

This is new capacity and displaces nothing: one script, one nightly step and one additive JSON field. #1836 cannot be sized or closed without it.

#### Done when

- The maintainer's budget comment exists and predates the first CI figures, or the record states that the budgets were set afterwards.
- `ml1-004/VERDICT.md` exists and gives: commit, .NET SDK and CI run; the fixture lineage (0.30 mirror of seed 6089, not a regeneration); MCP write P50, P90, P99 and maximum over at least 200 edits; the `dotnet build` table (all-compile, no-edit, (a) and (b) on P1, (a), (b) and (c) on P2) with wall time, task time and files compiled and skipped; the `calor check` table for both subjects with the three segments; and each budgeted quantity marked within or over.
- The record shows that both validity rules held, or marks the affected rows not measurable with the cause.
- The budget file is checked in. The job has a self-test step showing that a quantity registered as within and measured over fails it. The record names the `performance.yml` run it was taken from.
- `calor check --format json` and `calor_check` carry `durationMs` on the `compile`, `build` and `test` stages; a case in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ValidationLoopTests.cs` asserts it on both surfaces; `docs/cli/index.md` and `website/content/cli/test.mdx` list it.
- A comment on #1836 gives the three `CompileCalor` figures and the `calor check` figure, each marked within, over or not measurable, and for each of the three edits the Calor files compiled and skipped and how many of the compiled files call nothing in the edited module.
- `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised by the tests added (`Calor.Compiler.Tests` at `:8`). If tests are added to `tests/Calor.Performance.Tests`, the manifest (`:74`) and `expectedTestCount` in `eng/performance-baselines.json` (`:5`) change together.
- `CHANGELOG.md` carries the figures in its `[Unreleased]` section under "Test and CI health", and the rows "One command that builds, tests and checks" and "Fast warm feedback" in section 3 of `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` cite the record.

#### Not in scope

- Making any path faster. The reuse tier, and caching in `calor check` if its figure is over budget, belong to #1836.
- Index build time (#1738).
- Regenerating the fixture with `generate-fixture.py`, or editing the committed fixture or its mirror.
- Adding or changing a row of Annex A.
- Timing a project that contains preserved C#. The baseline records that it disables reuse for every file.

Part of epic #1804. Blocks #1836.

### #1822 · [0.32] Define and test what an agent does with a preserved C# file

**Action:** new · **Labels:** dev-experience · **Depends on:** #1823 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

Give agents one sanctioned procedure for changing C# that the converter preserved, make every instruction surface state that procedure, and test it end to end. Preserved C# is original C# that the converter kept inside the `.calr` file, in a `raw` frame with a byte count, because it could not write it as Calor. The largest case is a whole file.

1. **Choose the procedure.** This is a maintainer decision. The maintainer names the option in a comment on this issue before the text is written, and the pull request quotes the comment and the reasons. Two options:
   - **A. Change the C#, then convert again.** The agent never edits a `raw` frame. It changes the C# and runs the converter (`calor_convert`, `calor convert` or `calor migrate`), which replaces the `.calr`: native Calor if the changed code now converts, a new frame otherwise. Costs: the procedure must say where the C# to edit comes from when the original `.cs` is no longer in the project (the frame and the conversion receipt both hold it); the write hook blocks editing any `.cs`, so either the validator learns this case or the new C# goes to a tool as text; and the converter's entry points disagree today. `calor convert` and `calor migrate` refuse to refresh a `.calr` that was edited by hand or has no matching receipt. The MCP tool `calor_convert` overwrites its `outputPath` without reading or writing a receipt, after which the command-line refresh refuses that file (see Baseline). The procedure therefore names one entry point, and the guide says what the other does with a file the procedure wrote. Making the two agree is compiler work and is not part of this issue.
   - **B. A tool rewrites the payload.** An MCP operation takes the `.calr` path and the new C#, writes the frame with the right byte count and checks the result. Costs: the file stays preserved even when the new code would convert, unless the tool also re-runs the converter, which is option A behind one call; and the always-on MCP surface has 100 tokens left, so this has to be a parameter of an existing tool, not a new tool.

   The plan recommends A, delivered through an existing tool where possible so that no `.cs` file has to be written. It keeps one owner for frames, which is what the teaching reference and the compiler's own error messages already say, and it is the only option that can turn preserved code into native Calor.
2. **Make the instructions agree.** Three texts must state or point to the chosen procedure, and none may tell the agent to edit a preserved `.calr` by hand: Rule 2 in the rule text of `reference.json` (`agentInstructions`, or the fields #1823 split it into) and the sentence "Do not handwrite raw C# passthrough; converters own byte-counted raw payloads." in `introduction` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`, lines 6 and 4), and the guidance string of the `calor_help` features answer (`FeatureSupportGuidance`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/HelpTool.cs:905-907`). Regenerate with `calor self-check docs --fix`. Fit the token budgets in force after #1823, within the share of the always-loaded headroom its record gives this issue, and raise none. If the procedure does not fit the always-loaded text, Rule 2 points to `calor_help` and the detail lives there.
3. **Make the hook agree.** `HookCommand.ValidateWriteWithReason` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/HookCommand.cs:256-323`) allows every write the procedure requires and nothing else new. If the procedure never writes a `.cs` file, add a test that says so and leave the validator alone. #1809 changes the same function and the same rule text; whichever merges second rebases on the first.
4. **Document it.** Add a section "Changing preserved C#" to `website/content/guides/csharp-interoperability.mdx`, linked from `docs/cli/convert.md` and `docs/cli/migrate.md` and from `website/content/cli/convert.mdx` and `website/content/cli/migrate.mdx`. `docs/cli/convert.md:510-546` already gives three ways to handle the original `.cs` after conversion (exclude it from compilation, move it to a reference directory, delete it). The new section says where the C# to change comes from under each of them, which entry point the procedure uses, and what the other entry point does with a file the procedure wrote.
5. **Test it**, as Done when lists.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Rule 2: "Convert C# to Calor before modifying it (`calor_convert`), then edit the `.calr` file." (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template:11`). Applied to a preserved file, it tells the agent to edit the frame.
- The reference says the opposite (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:22`), as does the guide: "Do not write these frames by hand" (`website/content/guides/csharp-interoperability.mdx:29-30`). Neither says what to do instead. No page under `docs/cli` or `website/content/guides` describes changing preserved C# (searched for "edit", "modify" and "re-convert" near "preserved").
- A frame is a header line `raw <reason> <UTF-8 byte count>` followed by exactly that many bytes (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:3768-3775`). The lexer consumes exactly that many bytes and reports `Calor0013` (`InvalidRawFrame`) when the file ends first (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:863-864`) or when the counted bytes end in the middle of a line (`:875-877`). A hand edit that shortens the payload of a frame at the end of a file by two or more bytes is therefore rejected (a count that is too large is pinned at the lexer level by `AssertCountedRawPayload`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/LiteralRawSemanticsTests.cs:771-775`). One that lengthens it is rejected by this check only if the count happens to end inside a line; otherwise the rest of the payload is lexed as Calor. With a corrected count the compiler accepts a hand-edited frame; it checks only that the reason is present and known (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Token.cs:563-586`). The messages of both checks already point to re-conversion: "run calor convert on the original C# and retain its marked fallback" (`Lexer.cs:827-828`, `Token.cs:576`).
- Refresh exists on the command line. `calor convert` and `calor migrate` regenerate an unchanged output when its original C# changes, and refuse an output that was edited or has no matching receipt (`docs/cli/convert.md:126-135`, `docs/cli/migrate.md:188-190`; `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:3094-3114`; `Convert_Receipt_UnchangedOutputAllowsChangedOriginalRefresh`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ConvertFormatEnvelopeTests.cs:609`). The receipt next to the output holds the original source (`docs/cli/convert.md:127-129`).
- The MCP tool `calor_convert` takes C# as `source` or `inputPath` and writes `outputPath` with a plain atomic write (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/ConvertTool.cs:35-46`, `:353-361`, `:678-686`). It does not use the receipt-checked writer (`ConversionGeneration`, `CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:3048`), so it overwrites an existing `.calr` whether or not it was edited and leaves any receipt beside it stale. The MCP tool `calor_migrate` writes repaired `.calr` files the same way (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/MigrateTool.cs:335`, `:568`). Both read from the code, not run.
- The write hook blocks editing a `.cs` file (`HookCommand.cs:297-313`, `:347-348`; `EditCsFile_IsBlocked`, `tests/Calor.Ids.Tests/ValidateEditTests.cs:14-24`). An MCP tool call is not a `Write` or `Edit` and does not pass through the hook.
- `calor migrate` writes each `.calr` next to its source and deletes nothing (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/ProjectDiscovery.cs:112-115`; no `File.Delete` in `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/MigrateCommand.cs` or `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/`). `Calor.Sdk` compiles every `**/*.calr` (`src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.props:19`). `docs/cli/convert.md:510-546` ("File Coexistence") tells the adopter to exclude the original `.cs` from compilation, move it to `.csharp-originals/`, or delete it. It does not say how preserved C# is changed afterwards, and no other page under `docs` or `website/content` repeats it (searched for "Compile Remove" and "csharp-originals").
- Whole-file preservation is routine. A file with several or nested namespaces is kept whole and recorded as the loss `namespace-topology` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/FeatureSupport.cs:826-832`, `CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:2135-2159`); a file whose native conversion or validation fails is kept whole and recorded as `whole-file-preservation` (`CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:981-989`). On the default leg of the round-trip harness the native-file floors are 5 of 32, 5 of 139 and 10 of 112 (`native_fraction` under `blocking_floors.default`, `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json`; totals in `docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:363-365`). A native file there is one converted with no recorded loss (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/RoundTripReport.cs:512-531`); the floors do not say how many of the other files are preserved whole.
- Budgets on the surfaces this text touches, o200k proxy tokenizer, measured 2026-11-20 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:41-71`): template plus server instructions 592 of 600, so `agentInstructions`, which is counted twice, can grow by four tokens; full reference 16,750 of 17,000; always-on MCP surface 7,900 of 8,000.

#### Why

Per-module adoption inside existing C# repositories is the adoption path the strategy relies on (`docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md:67`), and it is where an agent first meets preserved C#. Today the texts it reads disagree: Rule 2 sends it to edit the `.calr` file, which for a preserved file breaks the frame's byte count, and the reference forbids that edit without naming another route. The route the documentation supports (change the original `.cs` and convert again) needs an edit that the hook blocks. The benchmark tasks that modify a converted module of a migrated project (#1789) are the first measured tasks that put an agent in that position.

This is new capacity and displaces nothing: one recorded decision, a few sentences of generated text, one guide section and two tests. It changes no spelling, so the edition declared in 0.32 does not constrain it. It is off the 0.32 exit chain and may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.

#### Done when

- The maintainer's comment names the option, and the pull request quotes it with the reasons.
- A test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests` starts from a C# file that default conversion preserves whole, for example a file with two namespace blocks, which is recorded as the loss `namespace-topology`. It performs the documented procedure through the entry points an agent uses: `HookCommand.ValidateWriteWithReason` for every file write, and the MCP tools through `McpMessageHandler` (as `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ValidationLoopTests.cs:45-63` drives `calor_check`). It asserts that every hook call was allowed, that the resulting `.calr` compiles, that the generated C# contains the change, and that performing the procedure a second time with no further change succeeds. It also pins what the entry point the procedure does not use (`calor convert` on the command line, or `calor_convert`) does with the resulting `.calr`, refusal or refresh, as the guide section states it.
- A second test pins the failure the procedure avoids, on the whole-file `.calr` of the first test: deleting a line of the payload without updating the byte count makes compilation report `Calor0013`.
- The procedure sentence is one string in `reference.json`. A test asserts that it appears in the generated reference and in every generated text that the #1823 record (decision 3) leaves carrying Rule 2 (the templates, `server-instructions.md`, or both), or in the `calor_help` answer that text points to, and that no generated text still tells the agent to edit the `.calr` file in the preserved case.
- `calor self-check docs` exits 0. `TokenBudgetTests` passes with no constant raised. The pull request states o200k tokens added and removed for template plus server instructions, the full reference and the MCP surface (the tests print these figures).
- The guide section exists; `docs/cli/convert.md`, `docs/cli/migrate.md` and the two matching website pages link to it.
- `expectedTotal` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised by the tests added (`Calor.Compiler.Tests`, `:8`); `scripts/check_trx.py` enforces it in CI.

#### Not in scope

- Reducing how many files are preserved (#1647, epic #1659), and CS8669 on preserved originals (#1782).
- Effect checking inside preserved C#.
- Changing what `calor migrate` does with original `.cs` files; it leaves them in place. The three documented ways of handling them stay as they are.
- Test files under `tests/` (#1809).
- Making `calor_convert` and `calor_migrate` read and write conversion receipts as the command-line converters do. No issue owns it.

Part of epic #1804. Depends on #1823.

---

## Epic #1821 · [0.32] Epic: act on the delivery measurement — calor init default, budgets, teaching reference

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

At the close of 0.32, what `calor init` writes by default and the token budgets on the text an agent loads every turn are decisions on record, taken from the 0.31 delivery measurement by the rules registered before that measurement ran. Each agent client other than Claude Code has a recorded answer to whether it can read `calor://primer`. The full teaching reference is trimmed only after the measurement that read it.

Terms. The *primer* is the syntax sheet the MCP server offers as the resource `calor://primer`. The *agent file* is the instruction file `calor init` writes for an agent (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`). *Stage 2* is the part of the 0.30 evaluation (#1605) that runs inside `calor init` workspaces and compares four ways of delivering the primer: a pointer to it (as shipped), the primer inlined in the agent file, inlined plus a starter file, and inlined with no MCP server.

#### Why

- **The one measured lever ships behind a pointer nobody has tested.** In July 2026, on the pre-0.30 syntax, a syntax sheet of about 60 lines in the prompt removed 55% of the excess iterations of writing new Calor (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:13-28`). In 0.30 the agent file says "Read `calor://primer`" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template:9`) and the primer exists only as an MCP resource (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:124-130`). Stage 2 measures whether agents follow the pointer and whether the MCP server earns what it costs on every turn. The rules for reading both results are fixed in advance by the registration #1770. This epic is where those rules are applied.
- **Every teaching surface is at its ceiling.** In o200k tokens, the offline proxy for Claude's tokenizer that the budget tests count with (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:10-25`; measured 2026-11-20, `:41-71`): agent file plus MCP server instructions 592 of 600; primer 1,569 of 1,600; full reference 16,750 of 17,000; always-on MCP surface 7,900 of 8,000. #1809 and #1822 add rule text in 0.32, #1650 and #1825 add or reword always-loaded text in 0.33, and the 0.34 conversion work adds forms to the reference. Each needs a decided budget to fit into.
- **Stage 2 measures one client.** It runs Claude Code only. For Codex, Gemini CLI and Copilot the repository holds one manual Codex session from July, on the pre-0.30 syntax (`bench/phase0-agent-native/CODEX-SMOKE.md:27`). A pointer to a resource the client cannot read delivers nothing, so that capability is recorded before the default is decided.
- **#1734 rewrites the reference that #1605 measures.** It moved out of 0.31 so that the measured arm and the trimmed text are not confused. It ranks below the delivery decision: #1605 puts the July effect of the syntax sheet at about 1.1M input tokens per run and the effect of cutting 1.7K always-on tokens at 30K to 55K, and the full reference is not even always-on.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1820 | A script and one short session per client that records whether Codex CLI, Gemini CLI and Copilot (VS Code agent mode, the client `calor init --ai github` configures) can list and read `calor://primer`; client limits documented |
| #1823 | Decision record (`docs/plans/0.32-delivery-and-budget-record.md`): the `calor init` default (pointer or inlined primer, starter file or not) per agent, what the agent file and the MCP server instructions may repeat, the budget constants, the division of the remaining always-loaded headroom among #1809, #1822, #1650 and #1825, and where conversion-only forms are documented; then the default and constants are changed to match. A 0.32 exit criterion |
| #1824 | Conditional: the always-on MCP budget recorded, or the tool surface reduced, as the registered MCP rule reads; closed as not planned if stage 2 did not run |
| #1734 | Trim the full teaching reference toward the #1597 estimate without touching an example program (moved from 0.31; may slip) |

#### Sequence

1. #1820 has no prerequisite and starts when 0.32 opens. Its table must be merged before #1823 is decided.
2. #1823 waits for stage 2 of #1605 to be published or recorded as not run (a 0.31 record of "no paid run authorised" is such a record), for #1820, and for the `calor init` options #1773 built in 0.31. #1809 (rule text), #1822, #1734 and #1650 wait for it, because their text must fit the budgets it sets.
3. #1824 waits for the same stage 2 record. It does not wait for #1823, and writes its section into the same record file.
4. #1734 is last: after #1745 (0.31) so that a trim which breaks an example fails CI, and after #1823 so that it knows where conversion material goes.

#### Done when

- The #1820 table in `docs/cli/mcp.md` is merged with an outcome for each of Codex CLI, Gemini CLI and Copilot in VS Code agent mode.
- #1823 is closed: `docs/plans/0.32-delivery-and-budget-record.md` holds a dated decision on each of its five points, and `calor init` and `TokenBudgetTests` match it. This is the 0.32 exit criterion "calor init delivery default decided".
- #1824 is closed as completed (budget recorded, or surface reduced and the ratchet lowered) or as not planned with a link to the "stage 2 not run" record.
- #1734 is closed, or moved to 0.33 with a comment that says so. It is off the 0.32 exit chain.

#### Not in this epic

- Registering the rules and running stage 2: #1770 and #1605 (0.31).
- Building the `calor init` delivery and starter options, and serving the primer through `calor_help` and the CLI: #1773 and #1771 (0.31).
- The rule text about test files and about converter-preserved C#: #1809 and #1822 (epic #1804). They fit the budgets this epic sets.
- Per-agent generation of the agent files, per-agent tokenizers and tests on what `calor init` writes: #1650 (0.33).
- Whether each agent reads the primer when only the agent file tells it to: #1811 (0.33, if its spend is authorised; drivers in #1651). Client compatibility beyond the resource check: #1652 (0.33).
- Follow-up with no owner: a second measurement of an MCP-enabled arm against a CLI-only arm after stage 2. No 0.33 issue runs one; it exists only if the maintainer lists it under Part C of the #1832 record.
- New example programs for agents: #1835 (0.33).
- Any change to a language spelling: epic #1787.

### #1820 · [0.32] Resource-read capability check for Codex, Gemini CLI and Copilot

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Blocks:** #1823, #1651, #1652 · **Displaces:** none: new capacity justified in body

#### Change

Record, for each agent client that `calor init` configures other than Claude Code, whether the client can list and read an MCP resource from the Calor server. An *MCP resource* is a document a server offers by URI; `calor://primer` is the syntax primer that the generated agent file tells every agent to read. Deliver a script, one short session per client, and a table of results. An agent can deliver the script, the dry pass, the unit test and the empty table; the sessions and their evidence files are the maintainer's.

1. **Script.** Proposed location: `bench/phase0-agent-native/client-resource-check/`. For one client it:
   - creates a disposable project outside the checkout (a minimal `.csproj`, as the pair harness does with `src/Src.csproj`, `bench/phase0-agent-native/harness-capture.py:610-611`) and runs `calor init --ai <agent>`, leaving what init wrote unchanged;
   - puts a `calor` wrapper first on `PATH` which, for `mcp` invocations, adds `--verbose` and copies the server's stdin (the client's JSON-RPC requests, one per line) and stderr (the server's `[MCP]` log) to a journal. The journal is the archived transcript of the session. If #1767 shipped a server-side journal or a stdio proxy for its primer-read log, reuse that instead of writing a second mechanism;
   - has the wrapper write a start line to the journal on every invocation. A journal with no start line means the client did not resolve `calor` through the wrapper (an editor's extension host may not inherit the shell's `PATH`; not verified for VS Code): that attempt is void and is repeated after fixing the launch, not classified;
   - prints the fixed prompt, and after the session classifies the journal and writes `evidence/<client>.json`.
2. **Dry pass, no client, no cost.** The script pipes `initialize`, `resources/list` and `resources/read` for `calor://primer` through the wrapper (the requests at `harness-capture.py:640-647` plus a `resources/list`) and must classify the result as "reads". It needs a built compiler and calls no model. Run it before the first session and commit its journal.
3. **Sessions.** At most two attempts per client.
   - Codex CLI, non-interactive. The invocation on record is `codex exec --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust --sandbox workspace-write --json` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/CODEX-SMOKE.md:18`).
   - Gemini CLI, non-interactive. No invocation is recorded in the repository; the script documents the one used.
   - GitHub Copilot in VS Code agent mode. This is the Copilot client this issue checks, because it is the one `calor init --ai github` configures, through `.vscode/mcp.json`. It cannot be driven from a shell, so its "script" is a written step list the maintainer follows in the editor, with the same wrapper and prompt.
   - Optional fourth row: a headless `copilot` CLI, if installed. It is not what `calor init` configures; record whether it loads `.vscode/mcp.json` at all. "Not checked" is acceptable for this row.
   - If #1767 was closed as not planned, add Claude Code, because nothing else will then have recorded it. Use a disposable `HOME` (`harness-capture.py:602`): `calor init --ai claude` registers the server in `~/.claude.json` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/ClaudeInitializer.cs:95-97`).

   Fixed prompt: list the resources of the MCP server named `calor`; read `calor://primer` and reply with its first line; call `calor_help` with `action=syntax` and `query=overview`; write no file.
4. **Outcome per client**, read from the journal. The readings are fixed here, before any session:

   | Outcome | Journal shows | Consequence |
   |---|---|---|
   | reads | `resources/read` for `calor://primer` after the prompt alone | The pointer form can work for this client; #1823 applies the registered delivery rule |
   | cannot read | `initialize`, but no `resources/read` for `calor://primer` in two attempts | #1823 gives this client the inlined primer regardless of stage 2 |
   | server not loaded | a wrapper start line at most, and no `initialize` | The registration `calor init` wrote is not picked up: note it for #1652; #1823 treats the client as "cannot read" |
   | not checked | no session, with the reason | #1823 decides how to treat it |

   Also recorded, outside the outcome: client name and version from its `initialize` request; whether `tools/list` was requested; whether a user action (an attach command or menu) reads the resource when the model cannot; whether the `calor_help` call completed (the log line "Tool calor_help completed", `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:401`). #1652 replays the recorded `initialize` requests and links these journals as its evidence that each client lists the tools and gets a tool result.
5. **Record.** A table under "Client configuration" in `docs/cli/mcp.md`: client, version, date, Calor commit, outcome, limits. Write the commit, not a three-part version: `calor self-check docs` flags the current compiler version in `docs/cli` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/DocDriftChecker.cs:265-271`, `:951-978`). Commit the journals with absolute paths replaced and the evidence files; do not commit raw client transcripts, which hold session identifiers and local paths.

**Spend.** Each session sends prompts to a vendor's model. Limits: two attempts per client, ten prompts in total with the optional rows, and $5 of metered spend in total (a planning ceiling with no measurement behind it). Abort: stop a client at its first quota or rate-limit error, and stop everything when the prompt or spend limit is reached. One model per client, written into the evidence file before the session and not switched during it. The dry pass of step 2 comes first and costs nothing. Filing this issue authorises no spend: the maintainer authorises it by running the sessions, and records for each whether it was metered and what it cost. The 0.32 spend record (#1816) covers the two paid collections of 0.32, not these sessions; if the maintainer lists them there, the lower limit governs.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The server registers `calor://primer` as a resource (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:124-130`) and handles `resources/list` and `resources/read` (`:218-219`). With `calor mcp --verbose` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/McpCommand.cs:19-21`) it logs every request method and every resource read with its URI to stderr (`McpMessageHandler.cs:208`, `:416`, `:433`).
- All three registrations start the server as `calor mcp --stdio`: `.codex/config.toml` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/CodexInitializer.cs:47`, `:279-281`), `.gemini/settings.json` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/gemini-settings.json.template:2-7`) and `.vscode/mcp.json` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/GitHubCopilotInitializer.cs:50`, `:152-156`). A `calor` wrapper on `PATH` therefore sees each of them without editing a generated file; the pair harness uses the same device (`bench/phase0-agent-native/harness-capture.py:595-601`) and already replays `initialize` plus `resources/read` with no client (`:640-650`).
- Every agent file carries "Rule 0: Read `calor://primer` before writing Calor" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template:9`; the four templates have one MD5).
- One session with another client is on record: Codex CLI 0.144.6, 2026-07-22, Calor 0.7.0 (pre-0.30 syntax), "Codex read `calor://primer`" (`bench/phase0-agent-native/CODEX-SMOKE.md:3`, `:13-18`, `:27`). Its sanitised evidence is `bench/phase0-agent-native/codex-smoke-709/evidence.json`; raw transcripts were withheld because they hold session identifiers and local paths (`CODEX-SMOKE.md:62-65`). Nothing is recorded for Gemini CLI or Copilot.
- The product documents Copilot as VS Code agent mode reading `.vscode/mcp.json` (`website/content/getting-started/github-integration.mdx:81`). A headless invocation appears only in an old study script (`scripts/multi_model_runner.py:79-86`); whether that CLI reads `.vscode/mcp.json` is not verified.
- `docs/cli/mcp.md:213-231` ("Client configuration") names no client limit.
- The server reads and writes one JSON-RPC message per line (`docs/cli/mcp.md:235-236`), so a journal of its stdin is line-oriented.
- Harness unit tests run in CI with no agent client and no built compiler (`python3 -m unittest discover -s bench/phase0-agent-native/tests`, `.github/workflows/test.yml:122-125`).

#### Why

The delivery decision (#1823) is taken from stage 2 of #1605, which runs Claude Code only. The rule registered for that decision says a client that cannot read MCP resources gets the primer inlined regardless, and nothing in the repository says which clients those are: the one record is a manual Codex session on a release that predates the 0.30 syntax. Item 1 of #1652 (0.33) lists resources among its per-client checks; only that part is pulled forward, because the decision needs it in 0.32. It tests the capability under an explicit instruction, not the behaviour. Whether an agent reads the primer when only the agent file tells it to needs a harness driver per client (#1651) and is measured in #1811, if that spend is authorised. This is new capacity and displaces nothing: one script, one unit test and three short sessions, taken out of #1652, which reuses the script.

#### Done when

- The script and a README exist under `bench/phase0-agent-native/client-resource-check/`, and a unit test in a `test_*.py` file under `bench/phase0-agent-native/tests/` classifies three committed sample journals as "reads", "cannot read" and "server not loaded". The test runs in the step at `.github/workflows/test.yml:122-125`; it is Python, so `eng/test-manifest.json`, which pins .NET test projects only, does not change.
- The dry-pass journal is committed and classifies as "reads".
- One evidence file exists for each of Codex CLI, Gemini CLI and Copilot in VS Code agent mode, with: date, client name and version, model, Calor commit, operating system, the invocation or step list, attempts, the three journal observations (`initialize`, `resources/list`, `resources/read` of `calor://primer`), and one of the four outcomes. "Not checked" carries its reason. If #1767 was closed as not planned, a fourth file exists for Claude Code.
- `docs/cli/mcp.md` "Client configuration" holds the table, naming the Copilot client checked, and `calor self-check docs` exits 0 (`.github/workflows/test.yml:267`).
- The README states prompts sent and metered spend, both within the limits above.
- The table is merged before #1823 is decided.

#### Not in scope

- Whether an agent reads the primer unprompted, or follows the agent file: #1811 (drivers in #1651).
- Claude Code, unless #1767 was closed as not planned: the primer-read log of #1767 covers it.
- Schema rules, handshake replay in CI and every other compatibility check: #1652.
- Changing what `calor init` writes for any client: #1823, #1650.

Part of epic #1821. Blocks #1823, #1652.

### #1823 · [0.32] Decision: set the calor init default and the token budgets per the registered delivery rule

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Depends on:** #1605, #1820, #1773 · **Blocks:** #1809, #1822, #1734, #1650, #1835 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Decide and record what `calor init` writes by default and which token budgets hold the text an agent loads on every turn, then change the default and the constants to match. These are maintainer decisions. This issue lists the options, what each costs and what the plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`) recommends; it does not take them.

Terms. The *primer* is the syntax sheet served as `calor://primer`. The *agent file* is what `calor init` writes for an agent (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`). In the *pointer* form the agent file tells the agent to read the primer; in the *inlined* form it contains the primer. *Stage 2* is the part of #1605 that compares those forms in `calor init` workspaces, with Claude Code. The *delivery rule* was registered before stage 2 ran, as item 10 of the entry #1770 adds to Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`. The thresholds quoted below are the ones the plan recommended for that entry; where the entry as registered differs, the entry governs. Token counts are *o200k* tokens: the offline tokenizer the budget tests use as a proxy for Claude's (`ProxyTokenizer`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:10-25`).

**Part 1: the record.** Path `docs/plans/0.32-delivery-and-budget-record.md` (#1824 adds its section to the same file). One dated entry per decision, naming the input it read and the option taken, and marked "as the registered rule reads" or "departs from the registered rule, because ...".

Inputs. Stage 2 is in one of three states:

- *Published.* Use the read rate and the cost figures as the #1605 report states them.
- *Recorded on #1605 as not run* (a 0.31 record of "no paid run authorised" counts). Decision 1 reads "not measured, default unchanged" for every client #1820 recorded as "reads". The other decisions are still made.
- *Neither.* This issue waits and decides nothing on partial data. If 0.32 is otherwise ready to close, the maintainer either holds the release for stage 2 or records "stage 2 not run" on #1605, and the second state then applies.

Decisions:

1. **Default delivery form, per agent** (`claude`, `codex`, `gemini`, `github`): pointer or inlined. The registered rule makes inlined the default if the primer was read before the first `.calr` edit in fewer than 90% of pointer-arm runs, or if the pointer arm cost at least 10% and two standard errors more than the inlined arm; otherwise it reads "pointer sufficient at this n". A client that the #1820 table (`docs/cli/mcp.md`, "Client configuration") records as "cannot read" or "server not loaded" gets the inlined form regardless. Cost of inlined: the primer's 1,569 tokens are in context from the first turn of every session, including sessions that write no Calor; with the pointer they enter the context only when the agent reads the resource, at the price of one extra call. Stage 2 measured Claude Code only, so the record also says whether its reading extends to the other three agents. Recommended: follow the rule; extend the Claude Code reading to the other agents until #1811 reports a read rate for each; if #1811 is closed as not planned, the extension stands and the record says it rests on Claude Code alone; treat a "not checked" client as "cannot read".
2. **Starter file by default.** Whether `calor init` writes the starter `.calr` file without being asked. The registered rule: only if the inlined-plus-starter arm cost at least 10% and two standard errors less than the inlined arm. Recommended: follow the rule, and never write a starter into a project that already holds a `.calr` file.
3. **What the agent file and the server instructions repeat.** Today the same 275-token rule block is sent to an MCP-enabled agent twice: in the agent file and as the server's `initialize` instructions (see Baseline). Token figures below are sums of measured parts for the inlined form (wrapper about 42, rules 275, primer 1,569), not measurements.

   | Option | Agent file | Server instructions | Per turn | Who loses |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | a. Leave both | wrapper, rules, primer | rules | about 2,160 | Nobody; 275 tokens are sent twice |
   | b. Rules in the agent file only | wrapper, rules, primer | one-line pointer | about 1,890 plus the pointer | A client configured by hand, with no agent file, gets only the pointer |
   | c. Rules in the server instructions only | wrapper, primer | rules | about 1,890 | A client that runs without the MCP server, or does not pass server instructions to the model, gets no rules |

   The option taken applies to the pointer form too (592 today, of which 275 is repeated). Recommended: b. The agent file is the carrier `calor init` relies on for every client; whether each client passes MCP server instructions to the model is not verified in the repository. Option b also returns most of the 275 instruction tokens to the MCP surface, which is at 7,900 of 8,000. #1809 writes its test-file rule into whichever of the two the record leaves carrying the rules. The record also says whether the five "Avoid the most common errors" bullets stay in an inlined agent file, since the primer covers each of them.
4. **Budget constants** in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs`. For each, "unchanged" or the new value with its measurement.
   - `TemplatePlusInstructionsBudget` (600): the budget of the pointer form. A second constant is needed for the inlined form if any agent defaults to it. Recommended: the measured count of what `calor init` writes plus the server instructions, with at most 50 tokens of headroom.
   - `PrimerMinimum` and `PrimerBudget` (800 and 1,600; measured 1,569). Recommended: unchanged, because an inlined primer is in context in every session.
   - The record states, in o200k tokens, the headroom each constant leaves after this issue and how it is divided among the issues that add always-loaded text afterwards: #1809, #1822, #1650 (per-agent text) and #1825 (the `.calr` test wording), for the rule text and for the primer separately; or which existing sentences each may shorten. An issue with no allocation shortens other text in the same field and raises no constant. At today's constants the rule text can grow by four tokens.
   - `FullReferenceRatchet` belongs to #1734 and the MCP budget decision to #1824; the record says so. One mechanical exception: if decision 3 shrinks the server instructions, the pull request that does it lowers `McpSurfaceRatchet` to the next multiple of 250 above the new measurement, as the class comment requires (`TokenBudgetTests.cs:33-34`).
5. **Where conversion-only forms are documented.** A *conversion-only form* is syntax that exists so the converter can express a C# construct natively and that an agent is not expected to write unprompted; the 0.34 coverage work (#1659) adds them. The full reference has 250 tokens of room (16,750 measured against the 17,000 ratchet, `TokenBudgetTests.cs:56-63`).
   - a. In the reference under the unchanged ratchet: each 0.34 change removes at least what it adds.
   - b. In the reference, with the maintainer raising the ratchet per form. The on-demand reference grows and #1734's target recedes.
   - c. Outside the rendered reference: the form stays in `reference.json`, so `calor_help action=syntax` and editor hover still answer for it, but is marked so the generator leaves its section and example out of the reference; its limits go to `calor_help action=features`. Costs a generator change and relaxes the check that every program is rendered. #1734 implements the marking; #1734 may slip, so if it has not landed when the first 0.34 form needs the marking, that form's pull request adds it.

   Recommended: c for forms only the converter emits, a for any form an agent is asked to write.

**Part 2: apply the record** (the same pull request, or a second one).

- Set the default of the delivery option and of the starter option that #1773 added, per agent. Both forms stay selectable.
- If decision 3 chose b or c, give the agent file and the server instructions separate source text in `reference.json`, have the generator read both (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/ExemplarCompileChecker.cs:138-162`), and regenerate with `calor self-check docs --fix`. Do not edit generated files by hand.
- Edit each changed constant with a comment citing the record, and add a budget test that measures the agent file `calor init` writes in the default form.
- State the default per agent in `docs/cli/init.md` and `website/content/cli/init.mdx`. An existing project changes only when `calor init` is run again; say so in the release notes.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Budgets, o200k proxy tokenizer, measured 2026-11-20 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs`): template 317 plus server instructions 275 is 592 of 600 (`:41-47`); primer 1,569 in a band of 800 to 1,600 (`:49-54`); full reference 16,750 against a ratchet of 17,000 (`:56-63`); always-on MCP surface 7,900 of 8,000, of which the instructions are 275 (`:65-71`). The class comment requires a changed constant to say why (`:30-34`). Each budget test prints its measured count to the test output; `dotnet test tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests --filter "FullyQualifiedName~TokenBudgetTests" --logger "console;verbosity=detailed"` shows the counts at the commit being worked on (not run for this issue: the figures above are the ones the comments record).
- The agent file and the server instructions come from one field. `agentInstructions` in `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json` (line 6) is written unchanged to `server-instructions.md` and, inside a short wrapper (the BEGIN and END marker lines, a template-version comment, a heading and a version footer), to each of the four templates (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/ExemplarCompileChecker.cs:138`, `:149`, `:158-162`). Lines 5 to 22 of `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template` are byte-identical to `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/Generated/server-instructions.md` (checked with `diff`); the four templates have one MD5. The wrapper is therefore about 42 tokens (317 less 275; token counts are not exactly additive), and a token added to the field counts twice against the 600 budget.
- The server returns that text as `instructions` in its `initialize` result (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:254`, `:682-683`).
- The primer already covers the five "Avoid the most common errors" bullets of the rule block: two-space indentation, lowercase headers, `let`/`var`/`set`, effects and postconditions before the body (shown by its example programs), and `File.ReadAllLines` returning `string[]` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md`; rule block at `CLAUDE.md.template:15-20`).
- `InitTemplatePlusServerInstructions_FitsBudget` measures the embedded templates (`TokenBudgetTests.cs:84-103`), not what `calor init` writes.
- `calor init` has four options, `--ai`, `--project`, `--solution` and `--force` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/InitCommand.cs:16-41`), and writes no `.calr` file (its closing message tells the user to create one, `:333`, `:533`). The four agents are `claude`, `codex`, `gemini` and `github` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/AiInitializerFactory.cs:11`). The delivery and starter options are #1773.
- Every construct and every program in `reference.json` is rendered into the full reference, and the generator throws if a program is missing from it (`ExemplarCompileChecker.cs:108-131`). A per-construct flag has a precedent: `lookupOnly` exempts a construct from needing a complete example (`:75`). `calor_help action=features` answers from the `FeatureSupport` registry (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/HelpTool.cs:366-391`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/FeatureSupport.cs`).
- Annex A's newest entry at HEAD is A-1.12 (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:349`). The delivery rule does not exist until #1770 adds its entry.

#### Why

The only lever with a measured effect on the cost of writing new Calor is the syntax sheet in context: in July 2026, on the pre-0.30 syntax, a sheet of about 60 lines removed 55% of the excess iterations (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:13-28`). 0.30 delivers its successor as a pointer whose use has never been measured. Stage 2 measures it and the rule for reading the result was fixed in advance, so that the default is not chosen after looking at the data; someone still has to apply the rule, change the default and move the constants, and this is that issue. The surfaces involved are within 8, 31, 100 and 250 tokens of their limits (agent file plus instructions, primer, MCP surface, full reference), and later issues (#1809, #1822, #1650, #1825 and the 0.34 conversion work under #1659) add text to them, so the budgets have to be decided before that text is written. The 0.32 exit criterion "calor init delivery default decided" names this issue. It takes no capacity from another issue: the options were built in 0.31 by #1773, and what remains is the record, a default per agent, the constants and their tests. The generator change that options b and c of decision 3 need is the one part larger than that.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/0.32-delivery-and-budget-record.md` exists with a dated entry for each of the five decisions. Each entry names its input (the #1605 stage 2 report or its not-run record; the #1820 table) and is marked as following or departing from the registered rule. For each of the four agents it names the default form and whether a starter file is written.
- A test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/InitCommandTests.cs` asserts, for each agent, that `calor init --ai <agent>` with no delivery option writes the recorded form, and that the other form can still be selected.
- In `TokenBudgetTests`, every constant the record changed has the new value and a comment citing the record, and every constant it left alone is unchanged. A test measures the agent file `calor init` writes in the default form, plus the server instructions, against its constant.
- If decision 3 chose b or c: `calor self-check docs` exits 0 (`.github/workflows/test.yml:267`), `McpRegistryValidationTests` passes, and `McpSurfaceRatchet` equals the next multiple of 250 above the surface total that `AlwaysOnMcpSurface_FitsRatchet` prints.
- The record states the tokens left for #1809, #1822, #1650 and #1825.
- The pull request states o200k tokens before and after, per agent, for agent file plus server instructions, and for the MCP surface.
- `docs/cli/init.md` and `website/content/cli/init.mdx` state the default per agent.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` raised by the tests added (`scripts/check_trx.py` enforces it).

#### Not in scope

- The options themselves (#1773), the primer through `calor_help` and the CLI (#1771), and the measurement (#1605).
- Per-agent text, per-agent tokenizers and tests on everything `calor init` writes: #1650.
- The MCP tool surface: #1824. Trimming the reference and the generator marking for decision 5: #1734.
- The sentences about test files and preserved C#: #1809, #1822.

Part of epic #1821. Depends on #1605, #1820, #1773. Blocks #1809, #1822, #1734, #1650.

### #1824 · [0.32] Set the always-on MCP budget from the registered MCP rule

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement · **Depends on:** #1605 · **Displaces:** conditional

#### Change

Conditional. Set the budget for the *always-on MCP surface*, the text an agent with the Calor MCP server pays for on every turn: the `tools/list` result (names, descriptions and input schemas of all tools) plus the server instructions. It is counted in o200k tokens, the offline tokenizer the budget tests use as a proxy for Claude's (`ProxyTokenizer`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:10-25`), and held by the constant `McpSurfaceRatchet` (8,000 at bc80daeb).

**Gate.** The MCP rule, registered before the measurement as item 11 of the entry #1770 adds to Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`. It compares two arms of stage 2 of #1605 (the part of the 0.30 agent evaluation that runs in `calor init` workspaces), both with the syntax primer inlined in the agent file: one with the MCP server and one with the CLI only, on cost per run. The reading comes from the #1605 stage 2 report. The margins quoted in the table are the ones the plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`) recommended for that entry; where the entry as registered differs, the entry governs.

| Reading | What this issue does | Closes as |
|---|---|---|
| Stage 2 recorded on #1605 as not run | Nothing. `McpSurfaceRatchet` stays a no-growth ratchet | not planned, with that record linked |
| Earns its cost (the MCP arm costs at least 10% and two standard errors less) | Record the ratchet in force as the decided budget | completed |
| Not resolved | Record the ratchet in force, change no tool, and note that no issue in the plan measures the question again: it stays open unless the #1832 record lists an MCP-enabled against CLI-only comparison under its Part C | completed |
| Does not earn its cost (the MCP arm costs at least 10% and two standard errors more, and its iterations-to-green are not lower) | Reduce the surface, parts 1 and 2 below | completed |
| No reading yet | Held: the issue stays open and no tool is changed on partial data | |

"Record" means a dated section in `docs/plans/0.32-delivery-and-budget-record.md` (the file #1823 creates; create it if this issue lands first) with the reading and its numbers, and a comment on `McpSurfaceRatchet` that cites it. The ratchet in force is the constant's value on that date: #1823 lowers it if its decision moves the 275-token rule block out of the server instructions, whichever of the two issues lands first.

**Reduction branch, part 1: the target (a maintainer decision, recorded before any tool changes).** The rule names no target. Options:

- a. Shorten schemas and descriptions; remove no tool. Smallest saving, no change for any client.
- b. Merge tools behind an `action` selector, as `calor_help` and `calor_check` already are. Tool names change, so every text that names a merged tool changes in the same pull request.
- c. Lazy-load: `tools/list` returns a core set and the rest are reached through one dispatcher tool. Costs one extra call before a deferred tool is first used. The server cannot announce tools later (it declares `listChanged: false`), and whether clients honour such an announcement is not verified.

The record states the target in o200k tokens and the tools affected. Recommended: a, then b, starting with the five largest tools; c only if those cannot reach the target.

**Reduction branch, part 2: the change.** Schemas and descriptions are in `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/*.cs` (`GetInputSchemaJson`, `Description`); tools are registered in `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:48-92`. One pull request per merged group. Each one:

- updates the tool table in `docs/cli/mcp.md` and `website/content/cli/mcp.mdx`, the tool lists on the website integration pages (for example `website/content/getting-started/github-integration.mdx:83-95`), and the rule text and workflow steps in `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json` where they name a changed tool;
- keeps the pair harness working if `calor_file_write` or the session tools are touched;
- lowers `McpSurfaceRatchet` to the next multiple of 250 above the new measurement;
- is listed in the release notes as a tool-surface change. The edition promise (#1805) is written for language spellings; if it has been declared when a tool is renamed, the pull request states whether its text covers MCP tool names and follows it.

No paid run is part of this issue. No later issue re-measures whether a smaller surface earns its cost unless #1832 Part C lists a run; the record says so.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The server registers 19 tools (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:48-92`). The surface measures 7,900 o200k tokens, `tools/list` 7,625 plus instructions 275, against a ratchet of 8,000 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:65-71`, measured 2026-11-20). `AlwaysOnMcpSurface_FitsRatchet` (`:128-142`) enforces it and prints the three counts to the test output (`:136`); its failure message says "Merge or shorten tools rather than raising the ratchet". `EveryToolDescription_FitsPerToolCeiling` prints each tool's description count (`:155`). Ratchets are set to the next multiple of 250 above the measurement (`:33-34`).
- Descriptions are the small part: the longest, `calor_batch`, is 167 tokens (`:73-78`). By characters of the schema and description literals in `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/*.cs` (not tokens; counted for this issue with whitespace collapsed), five tools hold about half the surface: `calor_batch`, `calor_convert`, `calor_compile`, `calor_check` and `calor_query`.
- Merging has a precedent: `calor_help` replaced five tools (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/HelpTool.cs:14-15`), `calor_check` selects among five actions (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/CheckTool.cs:35`), and the old names are not aliases (`docs/cli/mcp.md:57-59`).
- The server declares `listChanged: false` for tools (`McpMessageHandler.cs:245`). Nothing gates which tools are listed.
- `McpRegistryValidationTests` fails when the Claude, Codex or Gemini template or the server instructions name a tool that is not registered (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/McpRegistryValidationTests.cs:19-58`). The rule text names `calor_help`, `calor_convert`, `calor_compile` and `calor_verify` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template:9-13`).
- The pair harness depends on `calor_file_write` and its write journal in its MCP edit mode (`bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:315`, `:1607`).
- The value of the MCP path was measured once: epoch `m5-compare-001`, 2026-07-28, pre-0.30 syntax, Calor against Calor, median paired tokens-to-green ratio 0.6465 for MCP writes with warm sessions against raw edits (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/m5-compare-001/VERDICT.md:31`). It compared edit mechanisms, not the per-turn cost of the tool list.
- No per-turn cost has been measured. #1605 states that cutting about 1.7K always-on tokens saves 30K to 55K input tokens per run; scaled linearly, 7,900 tokens are about 140K to 255K per run. That is an estimate.

#### Why

The tool list is the largest text an MCP-enabled agent carries on every turn, about five times the primer (7,625 against 1,569 tokens), and its only supporting result predates the 0.30 syntax and measured something else. Stage 2 of #1605 compares a run with the server against a run without it. The margin and the reading for each side were registered before the data exists, because "earns its per-turn cost" with no threshold would be decided after reading the result. This issue applies that reading and nothing more. It is conditional and reserves no capacity: of its four readings one changes nothing, two change one comment and add one section to a record, and only one is engineering.

#### Done when

One of the following, matching the reading:

- **Not run.** The issue is closed as not planned with a link to the "stage 2 not run" record on #1605. No file changes.
- **Earns its cost, or not resolved.** The record section exists with the reading, the two arms' cost per run, the registered margin and the budget in o200k tokens; the comment on `McpSurfaceRatchet` cites it; `AlwaysOnMcpSurface_FitsRatchet` passes with the constant at the recorded value, which is not above the value it had when this issue started (8,000 at bc80daeb).
- **Does not earn its cost.** The record states the target and the tools affected, dated before the first tool change. `AlwaysOnMcpSurface_FitsRatchet` prints a total at or under the target, and `McpSurfaceRatchet` equals the next multiple of 250 above that total. `McpRegistryValidationTests`, the tests under `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Mcp/` and the harness unit tests (`.github/workflows/test.yml:122-125`) pass. `calor self-check docs` exits 0. `docs/cli/mcp.md` lists the tools as registered. `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated for every test added or removed. Each pull request states the surface total before and after.

#### Not in scope

- The agent file, the primer and the server instructions text: #1823.
- Adding an action or a tool. #1771 added the primer action within the 8,000 ratchet.
- Client compatibility of tool schemas: #1652.
- A new measurement of the MCP path: none is planned; it would need a Part C line in the #1832 record.

Part of epic #1821. Depends on #1605.

### #1734 · [0.32] Bring the full teaching reference to the #1597 token estimate (6-7K)

**Action:** move-in (from 0.31) · **Labels:** documentation, p2 · **Depends on:** #1745, #1823 · **May slip:** yes

**Current**

Split from #1603, the issue asks to bring the generated full reference (16,750 o200k tokens against a 17,000 no-growth ratchet) toward the 6–7K that #1597 estimated. Its three steps are: move conversion-limit material into `calor_help features`, shorten construct descriptions that restate a rule, and calibrate the o200k proxy with one Anthropic `count_tokens` measurement before lowering the ratchet. It is done when the budget is the #1597-style target "or a measured, justified replacement" and passes in CI, and `calor_help query=` and LSP hover still work.

**Moved from** 0.31 because it rewrites the reference that the 0.30 evaluation (#1605) measures. Landing it before or during that run would change the measured arm, and the delivery decision (#1823) ranks above it.

**Amendment**

Retitle to `[0.32] Bring the full teaching reference to the #1597 token estimate (6-7K)`. Add labels `documentation` and `p2`. Keep the opening paragraph and the "State at the end of 0.30" table. Make four edits.

1. Insert before `## Change`:

```markdown
## Order

- After #1745. With every example executed in CI, a trim that breaks or drops an example fails the build.
- After #1823, whose record decides where conversion material is documented (its decision 5) and which budgets are in force.
- The pull request states whether it merged before or after the commit that #1807 records for the 0.32 paid runs, so that a run report can say which reference its arms could reach.
- `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json` is in the inventory of the single 0.32 rewrite (#1803). If both are open, this issue rebases on the rewrite.
- This issue is off the 0.32 exit chain and may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.
```

2. Replace the three numbered items under `## Change` with:

```markdown
1. **Measure first.** Before any trim, record in the pull request the o200k count (`ProxyTokenizer`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:19-25`) of each part of `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/Generated/current-language-reference.md`: introduction and overview, the construct sections, and the example programs. The example programs may not be cut (item 5), so their count is the floor of any target. At bc80daeb the 87 `## Example` sections are 20,177 of the file's 70,814 characters (28%); their token share has not been measured. If tokens follow characters they are about 4,800 of the 16,750 tokens, which would leave about 1,200 to 2,200 tokens under a 6-7K target for the introduction, the overview and the 83 construct sections.
2. **Move conversion-limit material out of the rendered reference**, to where the delivery and budget record puts it (`docs/plans/0.32-delivery-and-budget-record.md`, decision 5 of #1823). Conversion-limit material is text that says what the converter preserves as C# and why. Its home is `calor_help action=features`, which answers from the `FeatureSupport` registry (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/FeatureSupport.cs`). When a registry entry changes, regenerate `website/public/data/feature-support.json` with `calor feature-check --list`; `WebsiteFeatureSupportParityTests` fails on a difference. A construct description keeps at most one pointer sentence, as the pragma entry already does ("Query calor_help action=features for current support"). If the record chose to keep conversion-only forms out of the rendered reference, add the marking that the generator (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/ExemplarCompileChecker.cs`) honours, and narrow its "every program is rendered" check (`:127-131`) to unmarked programs. The marking is for forms added from 0.34 on; this issue applies it to no existing construct or program.
3. **Shorten construct descriptions** that restate one rule several times. A description is also what `calor_help action=syntax` returns and what editor hover shows, so a rule removed here must stay stated once where an agent can query it.
4. **Set the budget from the measurement.** Lower `FullReferenceRatchet` to the next multiple of 250 above the new count. Calibrate the o200k proxy against Claude's tokenizer once, with the tool from #1799 or as a probe in a token-count dispatch the maintainer is already making. Anthropic `count_tokens` runs only from the manual workflow `.github/workflows/syntax-token-count.yml`; do not request a dispatch for this issue alone. If no calibration count is available, set the budget in o200k tokens and say so.
5. **Do not touch the examples.** No entry leaves `programs[]` in `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`, no program's `calor` text changes, every program is still rendered in the reference, and the primer (`calor://primer`) is not edited.
```

3. Replace `## Done when` with:

```markdown
## Done when

- The pull request carries the per-part token table before and after the change.
- The measured count is at most 7,000 o200k tokens, or the pull request states a replacement target with the per-part table as its justification and the maintainer approves it there.
- `FullReferenceRatchet` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:63`) equals the next multiple of 250 above the measured count, its comment gives the measurement and its date, and `FullReference_DoesNotGrow` passes.
- The ids in `programs[]` and the SHA-256 of each `calor` field are the same before and after (the pull request shows the comparison), the generated reference has one `## Example` section per entry of `programs[]` (87 at bc80daeb), `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md` is unchanged, and the executed-example checks added by #1745 pass.
- `calor self-check docs` exits 0 (`.github/workflows/test.yml:267`).
- Lookup and hover still answer for every construct: `EveryTopic_ReturnsAtMostTwoByteIdenticalCanonicalPrograms_ThatActuallyCompile` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Mcp/Tools/SyntaxLookupToolTests.cs:22`) and the tests in `tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/Documentation/TagDocumentationProviderTests.cs` and `tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/Handlers/HoverHandlerTests.cs` pass.
- For each conversion limit removed from a description, a test asserts that `calor_help action=features` returns it.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` updated for every test added or removed.
```

4. Replace the last line ("Related: #1603, #1597, #1614.") with:

```markdown
Part of epic #1821. Depends on #1745, #1823. Related: #1603, #1597, #1614.
```

**Why**

The in-context syntax examples are the only lever with a measured effect on the cost of writing new Calor, so the reference is trimmed after #1605 has measured it and after #1745 executes its examples in CI, and the trim may not remove an example. The done-when as filed has no observable for "still work" and no way to check that the examples survived; the replacement names the tests and the comparison. The 6–7K figure is an estimate that #1597 never fixed, and the character shares at bc80daeb make the "measured, justified replacement" branch the likely outcome, which is why the per-part table comes first: the example sections are 28% of the file, and conversion-limit sentences are about 4,600 of the 24,100 characters of description text (keyword search for "preserv", "convert", "conversion", "passthrough" and "raw C#"), so moving all of them does not reach the estimate. The issue is off the 0.32 exit chain and may slip to 0.33 without holding the release.
